\tau_{\sf in},\tau_{\sf out}\in{\mathbb{R}}^{2}/\ell{\mathbb{Z}}
with
\tau_{\sf out}-\tau_{\sf in}\in{\mathbb{H}}_{+}/\ell{\mathbb{Z}}
, and
\Phi\colon C_{\ell}\to M
. A
negatively-oriented Euclidean cylinder in
M
is given by the same data but with
\tau_{\sf out}-\tau_{\sf in}\in{\mathbb{H}}_{-}/\ell{\mathbb{Z}}
in the negative half-space. A
Euclidean cylinder
in
M
is a positively or negatively oriented Euclidean cylinder in
M
. The circumference
\ell
loop
\Phi\circ i_{\tau_{\sf in}}\colon{\rm S}_{\ell}\to M
is the
incoming
boundary and
\Phi\circ i_{\tau_{\sf out}}\colon{\rm S}_{\ell}\to M
is the
outgoing
boundary of a Euclidean cylinder in
M
for
i_{\tau}
defined in (
160
).
An
isometry
between a pair of Euclidean cylinders in
M
is an isometry of Euclidean manifolds
{L}_{z,u}\colon C_{\ell}\to C_{\ell}
that fits into the commutative diagram

(199)

{\rm C}_{\ell}
{\rm S}_{\ell}
{\rm C}_{\ell}
{\rm S}_{\ell}
M
i_{\tau_{\sf in}}
i_{\tau_{\sf out}}
i_{\tau_{\sf in}^{\prime}}
i_{\tau_{\sf out}^{\prime},}
\Phi
{L}_{z,u}

where
{L}_{z,u}
is determined by the left action of
(z,u)\in({\mathbb{R}}^{2}\rtimes{\rm Spin}(2))(S)
on
{\mathbb{R}}^{2}
, and the source and target of the isometry are
(\ell,\tau_{\sf in},\tau_{\sf out},\Phi)
and
(\ell,\tau_{\sf in}^{\prime},\tau_{\sf out}^{\prime},\Phi^{\prime})
respectively.

Remark 5.2
.

Commutativity of (
199
) is equivalent to
\Phi^{\prime}=\Phi\circ({L}_{z,u})^{-1}
and

\tau_{\sf in}^{\prime}=(z,u)\cdot\tau_{\sf in},\qquad\tau_{\sf out}^{\prime}=(z,u)\cdot\tau_{\sf out}\in{\mathbb{R}}^{2}.

By taking the isometry
(z,u)=(-\tau_{\sf in},1)
, a Euclidean cylinder in
M
is isometric to one with
\tau_{\sf in}=0
. Hence, the space (or more precisely, sheaf)

(200)

\displaystyle{\sf Map}({\mathbb{R}}^{2}/{\mathbb{Z}},M)\times({\mathbb{R}}_{>0}\times{\mathbb{H}}_{+})/{\mathbb{Z}}\coprod{\sf Map}({\mathbb{R}}^{2}/{\mathbb{Z}},M)\times({\mathbb{R}}_{>0}\times{\mathbb{H}}_{-})/{\mathbb{Z}}

admits a surjective map to the category in Definition
5.1
. Explicitly, a point of (
200
) determines data in Definition
5.1
via

{\sf Map}({\mathbb{R}}^{2}/{\mathbb{Z}},M)\times({\mathbb{R}}_{>0}\times{\mathbb{H}}_{\pm})/{\mathbb{Z}}\ni(\phi,\ell,\tau)\mapsto(\ell,0,\tau,\phi\circ F_{\ell})=(\ell,\tau_{\sf in},\tau_{\sf out},\Phi)

for
F_{\ell}
the diffeomorphism in (
162
). In the notation here and below,
\phi\colon{\mathbb{R}}^{2}/{\mathbb{Z}}\to M
is a map from a standard cylinder, whereas
\Phi\colon{\rm C}_{\ell}\to M
is a map from a Euclidean cylinder with circumference
\ell
, and so
\Phi=\phi\circ F_{\ell}
.

The sheaf (
200
) inherits an action by isometries of Euclidean cylinders; such isometries are determined by the action of the stabilizer subgroup (
158
). Consider the action by the generator of
{\mathbb{Z}}/4\hookrightarrow{\mathbb{E}}^{1}\rtimes{\mathbb{Z}}/4\subset{\mathbb{E}}^{2}\rtimes{\rm Spin}(2)
, i.e., the isometry descending from
\sqrt{-1}\in U(1)\simeq{\rm Spin}(2)
. This gives an order 4 automorphism of (
200
) that we denote by
\sqrt{{\rm Fl}}
. We note that
\sqrt{{\rm Fl}}
exchanges positively- and negatively-oriented Euclidean cylinders in
M
.
The notation comes from the fact that
\sqrt{{\rm Fl}}\circ\sqrt{{\rm Fl}}={\rm Fl}
is the automorphism of (
200
) determined by the spin flip involution from Lemma
4.9
.

The 2-dimensional Euclidean geometry affords some additional structures on Euclidean cylinders. Consider the orientation-reversing map

(201)

\displaystyle{\mathbb{R}}^{2}\to{\mathbb{R}}^{2},\quad(x,y)\mapsto(x,-y)

that preserves the inclusion
{\mathbb{R}}\subset{\mathbb{R}}^{2}
. The
orientation reversal
of a Euclidean cylinder applies (
201
), giving the assignment

(\Phi,\ell,\tau_{\sf in},\tau_{\sf out},\phi)\mapsto(\Phi\circ{\sf or}^{-1},\ell,{\sf or}(\tau_{\sf in}),{\sf or}(\tau_{\sf out})).

Another structure comes from the diffeomorphisms

(202)

\displaystyle{\mathbb{R}}^{2}\xrightarrow{{\sf rg}_{\mu}}{\mathbb{R}}^{2},\qquad{\sf rg}_{\mu}(x,y)=(\mu^{2}x,\mu^{2}y),\qquad\mu\in{\mathbb{R}}_{>0}

that dilate the metric on
{\mathbb{R}}^{2}
. The action through the square of the dilation factor is required to specify an isometry of spin manifolds. The
\mu
-dilation
of a Euclidean cylinder
(\Phi,\ell,\tau_{\sf in},\tau_{\sf out})
applies the diffeomorphism (
202
), giving the assignment

(\Phi,\ell,\tau_{\sf in},\tau_{\sf out})\mapsto(\Phi\circ{\sf rg}_{\mu}^{-1},\mu^{2}\ell,{\sf rg}_{\mu}(\tau_{\sf in}),{\sf rg}_{\mu}(\tau_{\sf out}))

Identifying
{\sf or}(\tau)=\overline{\tau}\in{\mathbb{H}}_{\pm}\subset{\mathbb{C}}\simeq{\mathbb{R}}^{2}
, and using the description (
200
) of Euclidean cylinders, orientation reversal and
\mu
-dilation are determined by the maps

\displaystyle{\sf Map}({\mathbb{R}}^{2}/{\mathbb{Z}},M)\times({\mathbb{R}}_{>0}\times{\mathbb{H}}_{\pm})/{\mathbb{Z}}

\displaystyle\to

\displaystyle({\mathbb{R}}_{>0}\times{\mathbb{H}}_{\mp})/{\mathbb{Z}}\times{\sf Map}({\mathbb{R}}^{2}/{\mathbb{Z}},M)

(203)

\displaystyle(\Phi,\ell,\tau)

\displaystyle\mapsto

\displaystyle(\Phi\circ{\sf or}^{-1},\ell,\overline{\tau})

\displaystyle{\sf Map}({\mathbb{R}}^{2}/{\mathbb{Z}},M)\times({\mathbb{R}}_{>0}\times{\mathbb{H}}_{\pm})/{\mathbb{Z}}

\displaystyle\to

\displaystyle({\mathbb{R}}_{>0}\times{\mathbb{H}}_{\pm})/{\mathbb{Z}}\times{\sf Map}({\mathbb{R}}^{2}/{\mathbb{Z}},M)

(204)

\displaystyle(\Phi,\ell,\tau)

\displaystyle\mapsto

\displaystyle(\Phi\circ{\sf rg}_{\mu}^{-1},\mu^{2}\ell,\mu^{2}\tau).

In particular, the orientation reversal of a positively oriented Euclidean cylinder is a negatively oriented Euclidean cylinder. The incoming and outgoing boundary are preserved by orientation reversal, and dilated by
\mu
-dilation. We emphasize that (
201
) and (
202
) do not come from isometries in the 2-dimensional Euclidean geometry.

One might consider the extent to which Euclidean cylinders in
M
can be made into the morphisms of a category, with source and target maps determined by

{\sf in},{\sf out}\colon{\sf Map}({\mathbb{R}}^{2}/{\mathbb{Z}},M)\times({\mathbb{R}}_{>0}\times{\mathbb{H}}_{\pm})/{\mathbb{Z}}\rightrightarrows{\sf Map}({\mathbb{R}}/{\mathbb{Z}},M)\times{\mathbb{R}}_{>0},

sending a Euclidean cylinder in
M
to its incoming and outgoing boundaries. One expects composition to come from concatenation of Euclidean cylinders. The obstacle is that smooth concatenation is only partially defined: general gluings need not produce smooth maps to
M
.
There are various ways to resolve this problem, e.g., by introducing collars or working with a partially-defined composition.
However, for our purposes it will suffice to restrict to a subspace of (
200
) where the difficulties of concatenation disappear: the
constant
Euclidean cylinders in
M
. We define this category in two steps, starting with the following Lie category, i.e., a category internal to smooth manifolds.

Definition 5.3
.

Define the Lie category of
constant Euclidean cylinders in
M
, denoted
\widetilde{\mathscr{C}}(M)
, as having objects and morphisms the smooth manifolds

{\rm Ob}(\widetilde{\mathscr{C}}(M))={\mathbb{R}}_{>0}\times M\coprod{\mathbb{R}}_{>0}\times M,

{\rm Mor}(\widetilde{\mathscr{C}}(M))=({\mathbb{R}}_{>0}\times{\mathbb{H}}_{+})/{\mathbb{Z}}\times M\coprod({\mathbb{R}}_{>0}\times{\mathbb{H}}_{-})/{\mathbb{Z}}\times M.

The source and target maps in
\widetilde{\mathscr{C}}(M)
are given by the projections.
The unit map is determined by inclusion at
0\in{\mathbb{H}}_{\pm}
, and composition is determined by addition in
{\mathbb{H}}_{\pm}
.

Under the inclusion of the constant maps

(205)

\displaystyle({\mathbb{R}}_{>0}\times{\mathbb{H}}_{\pm})/{\mathbb{Z}}\times M\hookrightarrow({\mathbb{R}}_{>0}\times{\mathbb{H}}_{\pm})/{\mathbb{Z}}\times{\sf Map}({\mathbb{R}}^{2}/{\mathbb{Z}},M)

the (partially-defined) operation of concatenation corresponds to composition in the category
\widetilde{\mathscr{C}}(M)
.

The order 4 automorphism
\sqrt{{\rm Fl}}
determines an automorphism of
{\rm Mor}(\widetilde{\mathscr{C}}(M))
(given by restricting (
203
)), and restricts along the unit map
{\sf u}\colon{\rm Ob}(\widetilde{\mathscr{C}}(M))\to{\rm Mor}(\widetilde{\mathscr{C}}(M))
to an order 4 automorphism of
{\rm Ob}(\widetilde{\mathscr{C}}(M))
whose generator permutes the components. With respect to these
{\mathbb{Z}}/4
-actions on
{\rm Mor}(\widetilde{\mathscr{C}}(M))
and
{\rm Ob}(\widetilde{\mathscr{C}}(M))
, the source, target, unit and composition maps are all
{\mathbb{Z}}/4
-equivariant. This permits the following; we refer to §
C.1
for an overview of categories internal to stacks from
[
ST11
]
.

Definition 5.4
.

Define the category of
constant Euclidean cylinders in
M
, denoted
\mathscr{C}(M)
, as the category internal to stacks with objects and morphisms

{\rm Ob}(\mathscr{C}(M))=({\mathbb{R}}_{>0}\times M\coprod{\mathbb{R}}_{>0}\times M)/\!\!/{\mathbb{Z}}/4,

{\rm Mor}(\mathscr{C}(M))=\Big((({\mathbb{R}}_{>0}\times{\mathbb{H}}_{+})/{\mathbb{Z}}\times M\coprod({\mathbb{R}}_{>0}\times{\mathbb{H}}_{-})/{\mathbb{Z}}\times M)\Big)/\!\!/{\mathbb{Z}}/4

where the generator of
{\mathbb{Z}}/4
acts on objects and morphisms by
\sqrt{{\rm Fl}}
. The source, target, unit and composition maps are the
{\mathbb{Z}}/4
-equivariant extensions of the ones in
\widetilde{\mathscr{C}}(M)
.

Remark 5.5
.

We observe that
\mathscr{C}(M)
comes from a category strictly internal to Lie groupoids: the source, target, unit and composition functors are all smooth functors between Lie groupoids. Composition is strictly associative, and similarly the left and right unitors are trivial. The functor from Lie groupoids to stacks recovers
\mathscr{C}(M)
as defined above. The Lie groupoid perspective can be a bit more concrete. For example, the evident
{\mathbb{Z}}/4
-cover

(206)

\displaystyle\widetilde{\mathscr{C}}(M)\to\mathscr{C}(M).

is a groupoid quotient on objects and morphisms for the
{\mathbb{Z}}/4
-action. This allows one to construct a functor out of
\mathscr{C}(M)
in terms of
{\mathbb{Z}}/4
-equivariant maps out of the objects and morphisms of
\widetilde{\mathscr{C}}(M)
.

Following the previous remark, the assignments (
203
) and (
204
) extend to functors

(207)

\displaystyle{\sf Or}\colon\mathscr{C}(M)\to\mathscr{C}(M),\quad{\sf RG}_{\mu}\colon\mathscr{C}(M)\to\mathscr{C}(M),

where
{\sf RG}_{\mu}
for
\mu\in{\mathbb{R}}_{>0}
determine a (strict)
{\mathbb{R}}_{>0}
-action and
{\sf Or}
determines a strict involution of
\mathscr{C}(M)
, i.e.,
{\sf Or}\circ{\sf Or}={{\rm id}}_{\mathscr{C}(M)}
.

Next we explain the relationship between Euclidean cylinders and Euclidean tori in
M
. Our focus will be on the periodic-periodic tori (see §
4.2
), though there is an analogous discussion for the other spin structures. There is a
{\mathbb{Z}}
-action on (
200
) given by
(\Phi,\ell,\tau)\mapsto(n_{\tau}\cdot\Phi,\ell,\tau)
where
n_{\tau}\cdot\Phi\colon{\mathbb{R}}^{2}/\ell{\mathbb{Z}}\to M
is the map

(208)

\displaystyle(n_{\tau}\cdot\Phi)(z)=\Phi(z+n\tau),\qquad n\in{\mathbb{Z}}.

For
\tau\in{\mathring{{\mathbb{H}}}}_{+}\subset{\mathbb{H}}_{+}
in the open upper half plane, consider the
{\mathbb{Z}}
-fixed subspace

(({\mathbb{R}}_{>0}\times{\mathring{{\mathbb{H}}}}_{+})/{\mathbb{Z}}\times{\sf Map}({\mathbb{R}}^{2}/{\mathbb{Z}},M))^{\mathbb{Z}}\subset({\mathbb{R}}_{>0}\times{\mathbb{H}}_{+})/{\mathbb{Z}}\times{\sf Map}({\mathbb{R}}^{2}/{\mathbb{Z}},M)

whose points
(\Phi,\ell,\tau)
satisfy (
208
). Such points may be identified with Euclidean cylinders in
M
whose tori have conformal modulus
\tau/\ell\in{\mathring{{\mathbb{H}}}}_{+}/{\mathbb{Z}}
and volume
{\rm im}(\tau)\ell\in{\mathbb{R}}_{>0}
. Restricting to constant Euclidean cylinders of positive volume, the condition (
208
) is trivially satisfied, giving a span

(209)

\displaystyle{\rm Mor}(\mathscr{C}(M))\hookleftarrow({\mathbb{R}}_{>0}\times{\mathring{{\mathbb{H}}}}_{\pm})/{\mathbb{Z}}\times M\hookrightarrow\mathcal{L}^{2}(M)/\!\!/({\mathbb{E}}^{2}\rtimes{\rm Spin}(2)\times{\rm SL}_{2}({\mathbb{Z}}))

where the quotient stack on the right is defined in §
4.2
.

5.2.
Super Euclidean cylinders in
M

Analogously to the case of (nonsuper) Euclidean cylinders, the basic ingredient below is the super Euclidean pair (
191
). We will focus on the periodic spin structure, and so drop the
{\rm p}/{\rm a}
decoration

{\rm S}_{\ell}:=S^{\rm p}_{\ell}=(S\times{\mathbb{R}}^{1|1})/\ell{\mathbb{Z}}\qquad{\rm C}_{\ell}:=C^{\rm p}_{\ell}=(S\times{\mathbb{R}}^{2|1})/\ell{\mathbb{Z}}

for
S
-families of supermanifolds determined by
\ell\in{\mathbb{R}}_{>0}(S)
. Define the supermanifolds with boundary via their
S
-points

{\mathbb{H}}^{2|1}_{\pm}(S):=\{(\tau,\bar{\tau},\eta)\in{\mathbb{R}}^{2|1}(S)\mid(\tau,\bar{\tau})\in{\mathbb{H}}_{\pm}(S)\subset{\mathbb{R}}^{2}(S)\}

or equivalently, restrict the structure sheaf of
{\mathbb{R}}^{2|1}
to the half-spaces (
197
). Define the supermanifolds
{\mathring{{\mathbb{H}}}}^{2|1}_{\pm}
similarly via the open half-spaces (
198
). In the following, the quotients
({\mathbb{R}}_{>0}\times{\mathbb{E}}^{2|1})/{\mathbb{Z}}
and
({\mathbb{R}}_{>0}\times{\mathbb{H}}_{\pm}^{2|1})/{\mathbb{Z}}
are determined by the
{\mathbb{Z}}
-action (
193
) and its restrictions to
{\mathbb{H}}_{\pm}^{2|1}\subset{\mathbb{E}}^{2|1}
.

Definition 5.6
.

A
positively-oriented
S
-family of
2|1
-Euclidean cylinders in
M
is given by

(210)

\displaystyle(\ell,\tau_{\sf in},\bar{\tau}_{\sf in},\eta_{\sf in}),(\ell,\tau_{\sf out},\bar{\tau}_{\sf out},\eta_{\sf out})\in(({\mathbb{R}}_{>0}\times{\mathbb{E}}^{2|1})/{\mathbb{Z}})(S),\ \ \Phi\colon{\rm C}_{\ell}=(S\times{\mathbb{R}}^{2|1})/\ell{\mathbb{Z}}\to M

subject to the condition

(\tau_{\sf out},\bar{\tau}_{\sf out},\eta_{\sf out})\cdot(\tau_{\sf in},\bar{\tau}_{\sf in},\eta_{\sf in})^{-1}\in(({\mathbb{R}}_{>0}\times{\mathbb{H}}_{+}^{2|1})/{\mathbb{Z}})(S)\subset(({\mathbb{R}}_{>0}\times{\mathbb{E}}^{2|1})/{\mathbb{Z}})(S).

A
negatively-oriented
S
-family of
2|1
-Euclidean cylinders in
M
is given by the same data, but with the negative half-plane condition

(\tau_{\sf out},\bar{\tau}_{\sf out},\eta_{\sf out})\cdot(\tau_{\sf in},\bar{\tau}_{\sf in},\eta_{\sf in})^{-1}\in(({\mathbb{R}}_{>0}\times{\mathbb{H}}_{-}^{2|1})/{\mathbb{Z}})(S)\subset(({\mathbb{R}}_{>0}\times{\mathbb{E}}^{2|1})/{\mathbb{Z}})(S).

An
S
-family of
2|1
-Euclidean cylinders
in
M
is data (
210
) that over each component of
S
determines either a positively or negatively oriented super Euclidean cylinder in
M
.
The
S
-family of superloops
\Phi\circ i_{\tau_{\sf in},\bar{\tau}_{\sf in},\eta_{\sf in}}\colon{\rm S}_{\ell}\to M
is the
incoming
boundary and
\Phi\circ i_{\tau_{\sf out},\bar{\tau}_{\sf out},\eta_{\sf out}}\colon{\rm S}_{\ell}\to M
is the
outgoing
boundary of a super Euclidean cylinder in
M
for
i_{\tau,\bar{\tau},\eta}
defined in (
191
). Hence, an
S
-family of
2|1
-Euclidean cylinders in
M
gives a diagram

(211)

{\rm S}_{\ell}

\Phi

An
isometry
between a pair of Euclidean supercylinders in
M
is an isometry of
S
-families of super Euclidean manifolds
{L}_{z,\bar{z},\theta,u,\bar{u}}\colon C_{\ell}\to C_{\ell}
that fits into the commutative diagram

(212)

\Phi

where
{L}_{z,\bar{z},\theta,u,\bar{u}}
is (locally) determined by the left translation for
(z,\bar{z},\theta,u,\bar{u})\in({\mathbb{R}}^{2|1}\rtimes{\rm Spin}(2))(S)
and the source and target supercylinders of (
212
) are

(\Phi\colon{\rm C}_{\ell}\to M,(\tau_{\sf in},\bar{\tau}_{\sf in},\eta_{\sf in}),(\tau_{\sf out},\bar{\tau}_{\sf out},\eta_{\sf out})),\quad(\Phi\circ{L}_{z,\bar{z},\theta}^{-1}\colon{\rm C}_{\ell}\to M,(\tau_{\sf in}^{\prime},\bar{\tau}_{\sf in}^{\prime},\eta_{\sf in}^{\prime}),(\tau_{\sf out}^{\prime},\bar{\tau}_{\sf out}^{\prime},\eta_{\sf out}^{\prime})),

respectively, where

(213)

(\tau_{\sf in}^{\prime},\bar{\tau}_{\sf in}^{\prime},\eta_{\sf in}^{\prime})=(z,\bar{z},\theta,u,\bar{u})\cdot(\tau_{\sf in},\bar{\tau}_{\sf in},\eta_{\sf in}),\ (\tau_{\sf out}^{\prime},\bar{\tau}_{\sf out}^{\prime},\eta_{\sf out}^{\prime})=(z,\bar{z},\theta,u,\bar{u})\cdot(\tau_{\sf out},\bar{\tau}_{\sf out},\eta_{\sf out}).

Remark 5.7
.

There is an analogous version of Definition
5.6
for
2|1
-Euclidean cylinders whose incoming and outgoing boundaries are superloops with anti-periodic spin structures, i.e., for the Neveu–Schwarz sector. By Lemma
4.30
, the moduli spaces of antiperiodic cylinders are the reduced subspaces
({\mathbb{R}}_{>0}\times{\mathbb{H}}_{\pm})/{\mathbb{Z}}\subset({\mathbb{R}}_{>0}\times{\mathbb{H}}^{2|1}_{\pm})/{\mathbb{Z}}
, and so do not contain information about the odd direction in the super Lie group
{\mathbb{E}}^{2|1}
. For this reason, the categories of periodic supercylinders enjoy a richer supergeometry than the categories of antiperiodic supercylinders. In particular, the odd operators
\bar{G}_{0}
only arise for supersemigroup representations (
30
) associated with the odd spin circle. This matches the physics literature: when studying
\mathcal{N}=(0,1)
supersymmetry on a Riemann surface, a (globally-defined) supersymmetry operator requires the existence of a nonvanishing harmonic spinor, which only exists if the spinor bundle is trivializable e.g., see
[
AS02
, 2.2]
. In the case of cylinders, this supersymmetry operator
\bar{G}_{0}
therefore demands the odd spin structure.

-Euclidean cylinder. On the right,
{\mathbb{R}}^{2|1}
has two embedded copies of
{\mathbb{R}}^{1|1}
: the lower copy is the standard embedding, and the upper copy is the translate by
(\tau,\bar{\tau},\eta)\in{\mathbb{H}}^{2|1}_{+}
. The marked points correspond to the image of
0\in{\mathbb{R}}^{1|1}
.
The quotient by
\ell{\mathbb{Z}}
maps to the supercylinder with a pair of embedded supercircles. The marked points indicate the parameterizations of these embeddings; compare (
2.2
).

Lemma 5.8
.

The category over supermanifolds fibered in groupoids with objects (
211
) and morphisms (

) receives an essentially surjective functor from the presheaf of sets

(214)

\displaystyle{\sf Map}({\mathbb{R}}^{2|1}/{\mathbb{Z}},M)\times({\mathbb{R}}_{>0}\times\left({\mathbb{H}}^{2|1}_{+}\coprod{\mathbb{H}}^{2|1}_{-}\right))/{\mathbb{Z}}.

}{{{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@transformcm{1.0}{0.0}{0.0}{1.0}{74.6649pt}{-21.08852pt}\lxSVG@begingroup@{transform} \pgfsys@hbox{60}\lxSVG@closescope }}}
\lxSVG@closescope }}}
{}{{}}{}{{}}\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\hbox{\hbox{{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {{}{{}}{}{}}
\lxSVG@closescope }}}
\lxSVG@closescope {{{}}}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}\qquad\Phi\colon{\rm C}_{\ell}\xrightarrow{F_{\ell}}S\times{\mathbb{R}}^{2|1}/{\mathbb{Z}}\xrightarrow{\phi}M.

This assignment is natural in
S
, giving a functor between fibered categories. The functor is essentially surjective since any family of
2|1
-Euclidean cylinders is isomorphic to a family with
(\tau_{\sf in},\bar{\tau}_{\sf in},\eta_{\sf in})=0
via the translation by
(z,\bar{z},\theta)=(-\tau_{\sf in},-\bar{\tau}_{\sf in},-\eta_{\sf in})
.
∎

The presheaf (
214
) inherits an action by super Euclidean isometries; by Lemma
4.29
, these isometries are determined by
S
-points of
{\mathbb{E}}^{1}\rtimes{\mathbb{Z}}/4\subset{\mathbb{E}}^{2|1}\rtimes{\rm Spin}(2)
. We will be particularly interested in the generator of
{\mathbb{Z}}/4
, corresponding to the isometry
\sqrt{-1}\in U(1)\simeq{\rm Spin}(2)<{\mathbb{E}}^{2|1}\rtimes{\rm Spin}(2)
of the super Euclidean group. This isometry descends to an isometry between
2|1
-Euclidean cylinders denoted
\sqrt{{\rm fl}}
,

(215)

\displaystyle\hbox to155.4pt{\vbox to66.24pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 20.79134pt\lower-49.37639pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@closescope \lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@closescope \lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@closescope \hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} ;

where the vertical arrows are the
\ell{\mathbb{Z}}
-quotient maps in (
190
), and the notation comes from the fact that
\sqrt{{\rm fl}}\circ\sqrt{{\rm fl}}={\rm fl}
is the spin flip involution of
{\rm C}_{\ell}
from Lemma
4.9
.

Lemma 5.9
.

The isometry (
215
) of
{\rm C}_{\ell}
determines an order 4 automorphism of the category from Definition
5.6
that when restricted to the sheaf (
214
) is by the formula

(216)

\displaystyle(\Phi,\ell,(\tau,\bar{\tau},\eta))\mapsto(\Phi\circ\sqrt{{\rm fl}}{}^{-1},\ell,(-\tau,-\bar{\tau},\sqrt{-1}\eta)).

This automorphism exchanges positively- and negative-oriented
2|1
-Euclidean cylinders.

Proof.

Consider the specialization of (
212
)

(217)

{\rm C}_{\ell}
{\rm S}_{\ell}
{\rm C}_{\ell}
{\rm S}_{\ell}
M
i_{\tau_{\sf in},\bar{\tau}_{\sf in},\eta_{\sf in}}
i_{\tau_{\sf out},\bar{\tau}_{\sf out},\eta_{\sf out}}
i_{-\tau_{\sf in},-\bar{\tau}_{\sf in},\sqrt{-1}\eta_{\sf in}}
i_{-\tau_{\sf out},-\bar{\tau}_{\sf out},\sqrt{-1}\eta_{\sf out}}
\Phi
\sqrt{{\rm fl}}

where isometry between supercylinders is the map
\sqrt{{\rm fl}}
from (
215
). The inclusions are the unique arrows making the triangles commute. The diagram (
217
) determines an assignment on the objects (
211
) that uniquely extends to a map of categories fibered in groupoids by sending the isometry (
212
) determined by
(z,\bar{z},\theta)
to the isometry determined by
(-z,-\bar{z},\sqrt{-1}\theta)
. Specializing to an
S
-family of
2|1
-Euclidean cylinders over
M
gotten from an
S
-point of (
214
), we obtain the formula (
216
).
∎

5.3.
Additional structures on
2|1
-Euclidean cylinders in
M

The
2|1
-Euclidean geometry has some additional structures that enrich the geometry of
2|1
-Euclidean cylinders.

Lemma 5.10
.

The automorphism (
151
) of super Euclidean manifolds determines an
{\mathbb{R}}_{>0}
-action on (
214
) that (for
\mu\in{\mathbb{R}}_{>0}(S)
) is given by the formula

(218)

\displaystyle\mu\cdot(\Phi,\ell,(\tau,\bar{\tau},\eta))=(\Phi\circ{\sf rg}_{\mu}^{-1},\mu^{2}\ell,(\mu^{2}\tau,\mu^{2}\bar{\tau},\mu\eta)).

Proof.

The argument is analogous to that for Lemma
5.9
, but using the diagram

(219)

C_{\mu^{2}\ell}
{\rm S}_{\ell}
S_{\mu^{2}\ell}
{\rm C}_{\ell}
{\rm S}_{\ell}
S_{\mu^{2}\ell}
M
i_{\tau_{\sf in},\bar{\tau}_{\sf in},\eta_{\sf in}}
i_{\tau_{\sf out},\bar{\tau}_{\sf out},\eta_{\sf out}}
i_{\mu^{2}\tau_{\sf in},\mu^{2}\bar{\tau}_{\sf in},\mu\eta_{\sf in}}
i_{\mu^{2}\tau_{\sf out},\mu^{2}\bar{\tau}_{\sf out},\mu\eta_{\sf out}}
\Phi
{\sf rg}_{\mu}
{\sf rg}_{\mu}
{\sf rg}_{\mu}

in which the vertical arrows are determined by the map

(220)

\displaystyle{\sf rg}_{\mu}\colon{\mathbb{R}}^{2|1}\to{\mathbb{R}}^{2|1},\qquad(z,\bar{z},\theta)\mapsto(\mu^{2}z,\mu^{2}\bar{z},\mu\theta)

gotten from specializing (
150
).
∎

Next we analyze the effect of orientation reversal (
172
) and complex conjugation (
173
). For an
S
-point
f\colon S\to N
of a supermanifold
N
, we use the notation
\overline{f}\colon\overline{S}\to\overline{N}
to denote the corresponding
\overline{S}
-point of
\overline{N}
from conjugation of supermanifolds. Hence, the assignment
N\mapsto\overline{N}
determines a map of sheaves that covers the complex conjugation functor on supermanifolds; we emphasize that it does not give a map between
N
and
\overline{N}
(this would require
N
to have a real structure). The notation gets a little clunky for
S
-points of
{\mathbb{R}}^{2|1}
but we know of no great alternative; e.g., we will write

(\tau,\bar{\tau},\eta)\in{\mathbb{R}}^{2|1}(S)\mapsto\overline{(\tau,\bar{\tau},\eta)}\in\overline{{\mathbb{R}}}^{2|1}(\overline{S}).

Finally, for a sheaf
\mathcal{F}
on supermanifolds, define the conjugate sheaf by

(221)

\displaystyle\overline{\mathcal{F}}(S):=\mathcal{F}(\overline{S}).

Remark 5.11
.

The functor on presheaves determined by (
221
) is the Kan extension of the conjugation functor on supermanifolds composed with the Yoneda embedding. In particular, (
221
) is compatible with the standard notation for a representable sheaf.

Lemma 5.12
.

Restricted to
2|1
-Euclidean cylinders over
M
, the reflection functor (
174
) is determined by a map from the sheaf (
214
) to its conjugate

(222)

\displaystyle(\Phi,\ell,(\tau,\bar{\tau},\eta))\mapsto(\overline{\Phi}\circ\overline{{\sf or}}^{-1},\overline{\ell},\overline{(\bar{\tau},\tau,\eta)}).

Proof.

The argument again runs in parallel to those above, where we consider the diagram

(223)

{\rm C}_{\overline{\ell}}
\overline{{\rm S}_{\ell}}
{\rm S}_{\overline{\ell}}
\overline{{\rm C}_{\ell}}
\overline{{\rm S}_{\ell}}
{\rm S}_{\overline{\ell}}
\overline{M}
\simeq M
\overline{i_{\tau_{\sf in},\bar{\tau}_{\sf in},\eta_{\sf in}}}
\overline{i_{\tau_{\sf out},\bar{\tau}_{\sf out},\eta_{\sf out}}}
i_{\overline{{\bar{\tau}_{\sf in},\tau_{\sf in},\eta_{\sf in}}}}
i_{\overline{{\bar{\tau}_{\sf out},\tau_{\sf out},\eta_{\sf out}}}}
\overline{\Phi}
\overline{{\sf or}}
\overline{{\sf or}}
\overline{{\sf or}}

where
S_{\bar{\ell}}=(\overline{S}\times{\mathbb{R}}^{1|1})/\bar{\ell}{\mathbb{Z}}
and
C_{\bar{\ell}}=(\overline{S}\times{\mathbb{R}}^{2|1})/\bar{\ell}{\mathbb{Z}}
. The middle row is the value of the conjugation functor on a bordism over
M
, and the isomorphisms with the upper row use the orientation reversing map. Using (
173
) we obtain a
2|(1,0)
-Euclidean structure on the
\overline{S}
-family
\overline{{\rm C}_{\ell}}\simeq(\overline{S}\times{\mathbb{R}}^{2|1})/\overline{\ell}{\mathbb{Z}}
in the middle row, and then (
172
) equips the upper row with a
2|(0,1)
-Euclidean structure.
The isomorphism
\overline{M}\simeq M
uses the canonical real structure on an ordinary manifold.
∎

Lemma 5.13
.

The reflection functor (
174
) sends the isometry
\sqrt{{\rm fl}}\colon C_{\ell}\to C_{\ell}
defined in (
215
) to the isometry
\sqrt{{\rm fl}}{}^{-1}\colon{\rm C}_{\bar{\ell}}\to C_{\bar{\ell}}
. Equivalently, the maps (
216
) and (
222
) commute up to the spin flip automorphism of the family
{\rm C}_{\ell}
, as
\sqrt{{\rm fl}}{}^{-1}={\rm fl}\circ\sqrt{{\rm fl}}
.

Proof.

The statement essentially follows from Lemma
4.22
. To see this more explicitly, apply the conjugation functor to the diagram (
217
) and then using the diagram (
223
), we obtain an isometry of
\overline{S}
-families of
2|1
-Euclidean cylinders

{\rm C}_{\bar{\ell}}\xrightarrow{\overline{\sqrt{{\rm fl}}}}{\rm C}_{\bar{\ell}}\xrightarrow{\overline{{\sf or}}}{\rm C}_{\bar{\ell}},\qquad(z,\bar{z},\theta)\mapsto(-\bar{z},-z,-i\theta),

whereas if we postcompose (
223
) with
\sqrt{{\rm fl}}
we obtain an isometry

{\rm C}_{\bar{\ell}}\xrightarrow{\overline{{\sf or}}}{\rm C}_{\bar{\ell}}\xrightarrow{\sqrt{{\rm fl}}}{\rm C}_{\bar{\ell}},\qquad(z,\bar{z},\theta)\mapsto(-\bar{z},-z,i\theta),

These maps differ by the action of
-1\in{\rm Spin}(2)
, i.e., the spin flip automorphism of
{\rm C}_{\ell}
.
∎

5.4.
The category of nearly constant supercylinders

Restricting a super Euclidean cylinder to its incoming and outgoing boundaries determines maps of sheaves

(224)

\displaystyle{\sf in},{\sf out}\colon{\sf Map}({\mathbb{R}}^{2|1}/{\mathbb{Z}},M)\times({\mathbb{R}}_{>0}\times{\mathbb{H}}^{2|1}_{\pm})/{\mathbb{Z}}\rightrightarrows{\sf Map}({\mathbb{R}}^{1|1}/{\mathbb{Z}},M)\times{\mathbb{R}}_{>0}.

Attempting to construct a category with source and target data determined by the maps (
224
) runs into the same problems as in the case without supersymmetry: composition of
2|1
-Euclidean cylinders is only partially-defined, as concatenations need not be smooth. However, there is a (finite-dimensional) subsheaf of (
214
) on which smooth concatenations exist, defined as follows.

Definition 5.14
.

A
nearly constant

S
-family of
2|1
-Euclidean cylinders in
M
is an
S
-point of (
214
) lying in the subsheaf

(225)

\displaystyle{\sf Map}({\mathbb{R}}^{0|1},M)\times({\mathbb{R}}_{>0}\times{\mathbb{H}}^{2|1}_{\pm})/{\mathbb{Z}}

\displaystyle\subset

\displaystyle{\sf Map}({\mathbb{R}}^{2|1}/{\mathbb{Z}},M)\times({\mathbb{R}}_{>0}\times{\mathbb{H}}^{2|1}_{\pm})/{\mathbb{Z}}

\displaystyle(\Phi_{0},\ell,\tau,\bar{\tau},\eta)

\displaystyle\mapsto

\displaystyle(\Phi_{0}\circ p,\ell,\tau,\bar{\tau},\eta)

for the map
p

p\colon{\rm C}_{\ell}=(S\times{\mathbb{R}}^{2|1})/\ell{\mathbb{Z}}\to S\times{\mathbb{R}}^{0|1}

determined by the standard projection
{\mathbb{R}}^{2|1}\to{\mathbb{R}}^{0|1}
,
(z,\bar{z},\theta)\mapsto\theta
.

The restriction of (
224
) to nearly constant
2|1
-Euclidean cylinders gives maps

(226)

\displaystyle{\sf in},{\sf out}\colon{\sf Map}({\mathbb{R}}^{0|1},M)\times({\mathbb{R}}_{>0}\times{\mathbb{H}}^{2|1}_{\pm})/{\mathbb{Z}}\rightrightarrows{\sf Map}({\mathbb{R}}^{0|1},M)\times{\mathbb{R}}_{>0},

whose values are

(227)

\displaystyle{\sf in}(\Phi_{0},\ell,\tau,\bar{\tau},\eta)=(\Phi_{0},\ell),\qquad{\sf out}(\Phi_{0},\ell,\tau,\bar{\tau},\eta)=(\Phi_{0}\circ L^{-1}_{\eta},\ell).

where
{L}_{\eta}\colon S\times{\mathbb{R}}^{0|1}\to S\times{\mathbb{R}}^{0|1}
is left translation by
\eta\in{\mathbb{E}}^{0|1}(S)
.

Lemma 5.15
.

Suppose that
\Sigma=(\Phi,\ell,\tau,\bar{\tau},\eta)
and
\Sigma^{\prime}=(\Phi^{\prime},\ell,\tau^{\prime},\bar{\tau}^{\prime},\eta^{\prime})
are
S
-families of positively (respectively, negatively) oriented nearly constant
2|1
-Euclidean cylinders in
M
and that
{\sf out}(\Sigma)={\sf in}(\Sigma^{\prime})
. Then there is a well-defined concatenation
\Sigma^{\prime}*\Sigma
that uniquely determines an
S
-point of (
214
) that has image in the subsheaf of nearly constant
2|1
-Euclidean cylinders in
M
.

Proof.

In the universal case (i.e.,
S
is the representable subsheaf in (
225
)), concatenation of positively or negatively oriented supercylinders is given by

\displaystyle({\sf Map}({\mathbb{R}}^{0|1},M)\times({\mathbb{R}}_{>0}\times{\mathbb{H}}^{2|1}_{\pm})/{\mathbb{Z}})\times_{{\sf Map}({\mathbb{R}}^{0|1},M)\times{\mathbb{R}}_{>0}}({\sf Map}({\mathbb{R}}^{0|1},M)\times({\mathbb{R}}_{>0}\times{\mathbb{H}}^{2|1}_{\pm})/{\mathbb{Z}})

(228)

\displaystyle\simeq{\sf Map}({\mathbb{R}}^{0|1},M)\times({\mathbb{R}}_{>0}\times{\mathbb{H}}^{2|1}_{\pm}\times{\mathbb{H}}^{2|1}_{\pm})/{\mathbb{Z}}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle{{\rm id}}\times m}}{{\longrightarrow}}{\sf Map}({\mathbb{R}}^{0|1},M)\times({\mathbb{R}}_{>0}\times{\mathbb{H}}^{2|1}_{\pm})/{\mathbb{Z}}

where
m
is determined by the restriction of multiplication on
{\mathbb{E}}^{2|1}
to
{\mathbb{H}}^{2|1}_{\pm}
. The result follows.
∎

Definition 5.16
.

The super Lie category of
\widetilde{\mathscr{C}}^{2|1}(M)
, has objects and morphisms,

{\rm Ob}(\widetilde{\mathscr{C}}^{2|1}(M))={\sf Map}({\mathbb{R}}^{0|1},M)\times{\mathbb{R}}_{>0}\coprod{\sf Map}({\mathbb{R}}^{0|1},M)\times{\mathbb{R}}_{>0},

{\rm Mor}(\widetilde{\mathscr{C}}^{2|1}(M))={\sf Map}({\mathbb{R}}^{0|1},M)\times({\mathbb{R}}_{>0}\times{\mathbb{H}}^{2|1}_{+})/{\mathbb{Z}}\coprod{\sf Map}({\mathbb{R}}^{0|1},M)\times({\mathbb{R}}_{>0}\times{\mathbb{H}}^{2|1}_{-})/{\mathbb{Z}}

where source and target are given by the incoming and outgoing boundaries (
226
), while unit and compositions are determined by the super semigroup structure on
{\mathbb{H}}^{2|1}_{\pm}
(using Lemma
5.15
).

Lemma
5.9
gives a
{\mathbb{Z}}/4
-action on
{\rm Mor}(\widetilde{\mathscr{C}}^{2|1}(M))
generated by
\sqrt{{\rm Fl}}
and given by the restriction of the formula (
216
) to the subsheaf (
225
). This restricts along the unit map in
\widetilde{\mathscr{C}}^{2|1}(M)
to give a
{\mathbb{Z}}/4
-action on
{\rm Ob}(\widetilde{\mathscr{C}}^{2|1}(M))
corresponding to the pin flip on supercircles, see Remark
4.23
. The source, target, unit, and composition maps in
\widetilde{\mathscr{C}}^{2|1}(M)
are all
{\mathbb{Z}}/4
-equivariant. This allows one to form the following category internal to stacks.

Definition 5.17
.

The internal category
\mathscr{C}^{2|1}(M)
of
nearly constant Euclidean supercylinders in
M
, has objects and morphisms,

{\rm Ob}(\mathscr{C}^{2|1}(M))=({\sf Map}({\mathbb{R}}^{0|1},M)\times{\mathbb{R}}_{>0}\coprod{\sf Map}({\mathbb{R}}^{0|1},M)\times{\mathbb{R}}_{>0})/\!\!/{\mathbb{Z}}/4,

{\rm Mor}(\mathscr{C}^{2|1}(M))=({\sf Map}({\mathbb{R}}^{0|1},M)\times({\mathbb{R}}_{>0}\times{\mathbb{H}}^{2|1}_{+})/{\mathbb{Z}}\coprod{\sf Map}({\mathbb{R}}^{0|1},M)\times({\mathbb{R}}_{>0}\times{\mathbb{H}}^{2|1}_{-})/{\mathbb{Z}})/\!\!/{\mathbb{Z}}/4

where source, target, unit, and composition are determined by the
{\mathbb{Z}}/4
-equivariant structure maps in
\widetilde{\mathscr{C}}^{2|1}(M)
.

Remark 5.18
.

Following Remark
5.5
,
\mathscr{C}^{2|1}(M)
comes from a category strictly internal to super Lie groupoids for which composition is strictly associative. The functor from Lie groupoids to stacks recovers
\mathscr{C}^{2|1}(M)
as defined above. The
{\mathbb{Z}}/4
-cover of internal categories

(229)

\displaystyle\widetilde{\mathscr{C}}^{2|1}(M)\to\mathscr{C}^{2|1}(M)

is a groupoid quotient on objects and morphisms. This allows one to construct a functor out of
\mathscr{C}^{2|1}(M)
in terms of
{\mathbb{Z}}/4
-equivariant maps out of the objects and morphisms of
\widetilde{\mathscr{C}}^{2|1}(M)
.

5.5.
Characterizing structure maps of
\mathscr{C}^{2|1}(M)
via differential forms on
M

The isomorphism of supermanifolds
{\sf Map}({\mathbb{R}}^{0|1},M)\simeq\Pi TM_{\mathbb{C}}
with the odd tangent bundle gives descriptions of functions on the objects and morphisms of
\widetilde{\mathscr{C}}^{2|1}(M)
in terms of differential forms:

(230)

\displaystyle C^{\infty}({\sf Map}({\mathbb{R}}^{0|1},M)\times{\mathbb{R}}_{>0})

\displaystyle\simeq

\displaystyle C^{\infty}({\sf Map}({\mathbb{R}}^{0|1},M))\otimes C^{\infty}({\mathbb{R}}_{>0})

\displaystyle\simeq

\displaystyle\Omega^{\bullet}(M;C^{\infty}({\mathbb{R}}_{>0})),

(231)

\displaystyle C^{\infty}({\sf Map}({\mathbb{R}}^{0|1},M)\times({\mathbb{R}}_{>0}\times{\mathbb{H}}^{2|1}_{\pm})/{\mathbb{Z}})

\displaystyle\simeq

\displaystyle C^{\infty}({\sf Map}({\mathbb{R}}^{0|1},M))\otimes C^{\infty}(({\mathbb{R}}_{>0}\times{\mathbb{H}}^{2|1}_{\pm})/{\mathbb{Z}})

\displaystyle\simeq

\displaystyle\Omega^{\bullet}(M;C^{\infty}(({\mathbb{R}}_{>0}\times{\mathbb{H}}^{2|1}_{\pm})/{\mathbb{Z}})).

where
\otimes
is the projective tensor product (e.g., see
[
HST10
, Example 49]
).
We further observe that Taylor expansion in the odd coordinate
\eta\in C^{\infty}({\mathbb{H}}^{2|1}_{\pm})
provides an algebra isomorphism

(232)

\displaystyle C^{\infty}(({\mathbb{R}}_{>0}\times{\mathbb{H}}^{2|1}_{\pm})/{\mathbb{Z}})\simeq C^{\infty}(({\mathbb{R}}_{>0}\times{\mathbb{H}}_{\pm})/{\mathbb{Z}})[\eta],

and that there is an injective algebra homomorphism

(233)

\displaystyle C^{\infty}({\mathbb{R}}_{>0})\hookrightarrow C^{\infty}(({\mathbb{R}}_{>0}\times{\mathbb{H}}^{2|1}_{\pm})/{\mathbb{Z}})

induced by the projection
({\mathbb{R}}_{>0}\times{\mathbb{H}}^{2|1}_{\pm})/{\mathbb{Z}}\to{\mathbb{R}}_{>0}
, i.e., regarding a function on
{\mathbb{R}}_{>0}
as a function on
({\mathbb{R}}_{>0}\times{\mathbb{H}}^{2|1}_{\pm})/{\mathbb{Z}}
that is constant in
{\mathbb{H}}^{2|1}_{\pm}
. Consider the projection and action maps

{\sf pr}\colon(({\mathbb{R}}_{>0}\times{\mathbb{H}}^{2|1}_{\pm})/{\mathbb{Z}})\times{\sf Map}({\mathbb{R}}^{0|1},M)\to{\mathbb{R}}_{>0}\times{\sf Map}({\mathbb{R}}^{0|1},M)

{\sf act}\colon(({\mathbb{R}}_{>0}\times{\mathbb{H}}^{2|1}_{\pm})/{\mathbb{Z}})\times{\sf Map}({\mathbb{R}}^{0|1},M)\xrightarrow{p}{\mathbb{R}}_{>0}\times{\mathbb{R}}^{0|1}\times{\sf Map}({\mathbb{R}}^{0|1},M)\xrightarrow{{{\rm id}}_{{\mathbb{R}}_{>0}}\times a}{\mathbb{R}}_{>0}\times{\sf Map}({\mathbb{R}}^{0|1},M)

where
p
is an intermediate projection, and
a
is the left action of
{\mathbb{R}}^{0|1}
on
{\sf Map}({\mathbb{R}}^{0|1},M)
by precomposition.

Lemma 5.19
.

Using (
230
) and (
231
), the source and target maps
{\sf s},{\sf t}\colon{\rm Mor}(\widetilde{\mathscr{C}}^{2|1}(M))\rightrightarrows{\rm Ob}(\widetilde{\mathscr{C}}^{2|1}(M))
are determined by the superalgebra maps

{\sf s}^{*},{\sf t}^{*}\colon\Omega^{\bullet}(M;C^{\infty}({\mathbb{R}}_{>0}))\to\Omega^{\bullet}(M;C^{\infty}(({\mathbb{R}}_{>0}\times{\mathbb{H}}^{2|1}_{\pm})/{\mathbb{Z}}))

(234)

\displaystyle{\sf s}^{*}(\alpha)={\sf pr}^{*}(\alpha)=\alpha,\qquad{\sf t}^{*}(\alpha)={\sf act}^{*}(\alpha)=(\alpha-\eta d\alpha),

where we use the injective map
C^{\infty}({\mathbb{R}}_{>0})\hookrightarrow C^{\infty}(({\mathbb{R}}_{>0}\times{\mathbb{H}}^{2|1}_{\pm})/{\mathbb{Z}}
induced by the projection
{\mathbb{R}}_{>0}\times{\mathbb{H}}^{2|1}_{\pm}\to{\mathbb{R}}_{>0}
to regard a
C^{\infty}({\mathbb{R}}_{>0})
-valued form as a
C^{\infty}(({\mathbb{R}}_{>0}\times{\mathbb{H}}^{2|1}_{\pm})/{\mathbb{Z}}
-valued form.

Proof.

The
{\mathbb{R}}^{0|1}
-action on
{\sf Map}({\mathbb{R}}^{0|1},M)
can be characterized in terms of a map on differential forms
[
HKST11
, Lemma 3.8]

\displaystyle\Omega^{\bullet}(M)\simeq C^{\infty}({\sf Map}({\mathbb{R}}^{0|1},M))

\displaystyle\to

\displaystyle C^{\infty}({\mathbb{R}}^{0|1}\times{\sf Map}({\mathbb{R}}^{0|1},M))\simeq\Omega^{\bullet}(M;C^{\infty}({\mathbb{R}}^{0|1}))

(235)

\displaystyle\alpha

\displaystyle\mapsto

\displaystyle\alpha-\eta d\alpha,\quad\alpha\in\Omega^{\bullet}(M),\ C^{\infty}({\mathbb{R}}^{0|1})\simeq C^{\infty}({\mathbb{R}})[\eta]

where
d
is the de Rham differential on
\Omega^{\bullet}(M)
. Then from the description of source and target maps in (
227
), the result follows.
∎

Remark 5.20
.

The formula (
235
) differs from
[
HKST11
, Lemma 3.8]
by a sign; their formula is for the right action of
{\mathbb{R}}^{0|1}
on
\Pi TM_{\mathbb{C}}
, whereas (
235
) is for the left action.

Lemma 5.21
.

The
{\mathbb{Z}}/4
-action on
{\rm Ob}(\widetilde{\mathscr{C}}^{2|1}(M))
and
{\rm Mor}(\widetilde{\mathscr{C}}^{2|1}(M))
given by (
216
) is determined by

(236)

\displaystyle\sqrt{{\rm Fl}}\colon{\rm Mor}(\widetilde{\mathscr{C}}^{2|1}(M))\to{\rm Mor}(\widetilde{\mathscr{C}}^{2|1}(M)),\quad(\Phi,\ell,\tau,\bar{\tau},\eta)\mapsto(\Phi\circ\sqrt{{\rm fl}},\ell,-\tau,-\bar{\tau},\sqrt{-1}\eta).

In terms of a map of superalgebras,

\displaystyle\sqrt{{\rm Fl}}^{*}\colon\Omega^{\bullet}(M;C^{\infty}(({\mathbb{R}}_{>0}\times{\mathbb{H}}^{2|1}_{\pm})/{\mathbb{Z}}))

\displaystyle\to

\displaystyle\Omega^{\bullet}(M;C^{\infty}(({\mathbb{R}}_{>0}\times{\mathbb{H}}^{2|1}_{\mp})/{\mathbb{Z}}))

(237)

\displaystyle\sqrt{{\rm Fl}}^{*}(\alpha)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle(\sqrt{-1})^{-|\alpha|}\alpha(\ell,-\tau,-\bar{\tau},\sqrt{-1}\eta)

Proof.

This follows from Lemma
5.9
and the characterization of the
{\mathbb{R}}^{0|1}\rtimes{\mathbb{C}}^{\times}
-action on
{\sf Map}({\mathbb{R}}^{0|1},M)
in terms of an algebra map
[
HKST11
, Lemma 3.8]

(238)

\Omega^{\bullet}(M)\simeq C^{\infty}({\sf Map}({\mathbb{R}}^{0|1},M))\to C^{\infty}({\mathbb{R}}^{0|1}\rtimes{\mathbb{C}}^{\times}\times{\sf Map}({\mathbb{R}}^{0|1},M))\simeq\Omega^{\bullet}(M;C^{\infty}({\mathbb{C}}^{\times})[\eta])

where (
237
) restricts to
{\mathbb{Z}}/4\subset{\mathbb{C}}^{\times}
.
∎

Lemma 5.22
.

The maps (
218
) and (
222
) determine smooth functors

\displaystyle{\sf RG}_{\mu}\colon\mathscr{C}^{2|1}(M)\to\mathscr{C}^{2|1}(M),

\displaystyle(\Phi,\mu,\ell,\tau,\bar{\tau},\eta,\sqrt{{\rm Fl}})\mapsto(\Phi\circ{\sf rg}_{\mu}^{-1},\mu^{2}\ell,\mu^{2}\tau,\mu^{2}\bar{\tau},\mu\eta,\sqrt{{\rm Fl}})

\displaystyle\dagger\colon\mathscr{C}^{2|1}(M)\to\mathscr{C}^{2|1}(M),

\displaystyle(\Phi,\ell,\tau,\bar{\tau},\eta,\sqrt{{\rm Fl}})\mapsto\overline{(\Phi\circ{\sf or}^{-1},\ell,\bar{\tau},\tau,\eta,\sqrt{{\rm Fl}}{}^{-1})}

given by the indicated maps on
S
-points of objects of
{\rm Mor}(\mathscr{C}^{2|1}(M))
. Furthermore, we have the equalities of smooth functors

(239)

\displaystyle{\sf RG}_{\mu}\circ{\sf RG}_{\mu^{\prime}}={\sf RG}_{\mu\mu^{\prime}},\qquad\overline{\dagger}\circ\dagger={{\rm id}},

i.e., the functors
{\sf RG}_{\mu}
determine an
{\mathbb{R}}_{>0}
action and
\dagger
determines an involution covering conjugation of supermanifolds. The functor
\dagger
determines a reflection structure on
\mathscr{C}^{2|1}(M)
in the sense of Definition
C.19
.

Proof.

Functors on the
{\mathbb{Z}}/4
-cover
\widetilde{\mathscr{C}}^{2|1}(M)
are determined by Lemmas
5.10
and
5.12
. Since
\sqrt{{\rm Fl}}
commutes with the dilation action, the
{\mathbb{Z}}/4
-equivariance property required for the functor
{\sf RG}_{\mu}\colon\mathscr{C}^{2|1}(M)\to\mathscr{C}^{2|1}(M)
is immediate. Using Lemma
5.13
, the functor
\dagger
arises from a
{\mathbb{Z}}/4
-equivariance property relative to the inversion homomorphism on
{\mathbb{Z}}/4
,

(240)

\displaystyle\overline{\dagger}\circ\dagger={{\rm id}},\quad\overline{\sqrt{{\rm fl}}}\circ\dagger=\dagger\circ\sqrt{{\rm fl}}{}^{-1}

By definition, this determines a reflection structure on
\mathscr{C}^{2|1}(M)
.
∎

Corollary 5.23
.

The functors
{\sf RG}_{\mu}
and
\dagger
are determined by the superalgebra maps

\displaystyle{\sf RG}_{\mu}^{*}\colon\Omega^{\bullet}(M;C^{\infty}(({\mathbb{R}}_{>0}\times{\mathbb{H}}^{2|1}_{\pm})/{\mathbb{Z}}))

\displaystyle\to

\displaystyle\Omega^{\bullet}(M;C^{\infty}(({\mathbb{R}}_{>0}\times{\mathbb{H}}^{2|1}_{\pm})/{\mathbb{Z}}))

(241)

\displaystyle{\sf RG}_{\mu}^{*}(\alpha)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mu^{-|\alpha|}\alpha

\displaystyle\dagger^{*}\colon\Omega^{\bullet}(M;C^{\infty}(({\mathbb{R}}_{>0}\times{\mathbb{H}}^{2|1}_{\pm})/{\mathbb{Z}}))

\displaystyle\to

\displaystyle\overline{\Omega^{\bullet}(M;C^{\infty}(({\mathbb{R}}_{>0}\times{\mathbb{H}}^{2|1}_{\mp})/{\mathbb{Z}}))}

(242)

\displaystyle\dagger^{*}(\alpha)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\overline{\alpha}(\ell,\bar{\tau},\tau,\eta)

where
|\alpha|
denotes the
{\mathbb{Z}}
-grading of
\alpha
(as a differential form),
\overline{\alpha}
is the usual complex conjugate of
\alpha
(as a differential form defined over
{\mathbb{C}}
), and
\ell\in C^{\infty}({\mathbb{R}}_{>0})
and
(\tau,\bar{\tau},\eta)\in C^{\infty}({\mathbb{H}}^{2|1}_{\pm})
are the standard coordinates.

Proof.

We obtain (
241
) from restricting (
238
) to
{\mathbb{R}}_{>0}\subset{\mathbb{C}}^{\times}
, and (
242
) uses the fact that the conjugate supermanifold to
{\sf Map}({\mathbb{R}}^{0|1},M)\simeq\Pi TM_{\mathbb{C}}
corresponds to the opposite complex structure on
{\mathbb{C}}
-valued differential forms.
∎

From Definition
4.28
, we recall that
\mathcal{L}^{2|1}(M):={\sf Map}({\mathbb{R}}^{2|1}/{\mathbb{Z}}^{2},M)\times s{\sf Lat}
.

Lemma 5.24
.

There is a span of inclusions

(243)

\displaystyle\mathcal{L}^{2|1}(M)\hookleftarrow{\sf Map}({\mathbb{R}}^{0|1},M)\times({\mathbb{R}}_{>0}\times{\mathring{{\mathbb{H}}}}_{+})/{\mathbb{Z}}\hookrightarrow{\rm Mor}(\mathscr{C}^{2|1}(M))

identifying a subspace of morphisms in
\mathscr{C}^{2|1}(M)
with
S
-points of the super double loop space of
M
.

Proof.

The inclusion on the right is evident from the definition. The inclusion on the left is determined by

\displaystyle(\Phi_{0},\ell,\tau,\bar{\tau},0)\mapsto(\Phi_{0}\circ p,(\ell,\ell,0),(\tau,\bar{\tau},0))=(\Phi,\Lambda)\in({\sf Map}({\mathbb{R}}^{2|1}/{\mathbb{Z}}^{2},M)\times s{\sf Lat})(S).

We check that the above is well-defined. The restriction
(\tau,\bar{\tau},\theta)\in{\mathring{{\mathbb{H}}}}_{+}(S)\subset{\mathbb{H}}_{+}(S)
to the open half plane guarantees that this indeed gives a lattice, and the upper half plane condition implies that the lattice is oriented. Hence, the formula above gives an
S
-point
\Lambda\in s{\sf Lat}(S)
. The inclusion on mapping spaces comes from restricting (
225
): in view of (
227
) (taking
\eta=0
) the resulting map
\Phi=\Phi_{0}\circ p\colon(S\times{\mathbb{R}}^{2|1})/\ell{\mathbb{Z}}\to M
descends to a map out of the
{\mathbb{Z}}^{2}
-quotient by
\Lambda
. This completes the proof.
∎

6.
Supercylinders and supertori as
2|1
-Euclidean bordisms

The main goal of this section is to construct an internal functor

(244)

\displaystyle\mathscr{C}^{2|1}(M)\to 2|1\hbox{-}{\sf EBord}(M)

from the category of nearly constant supercylinders in
M
(see Definition
5.17
) to Stolz and Teichner’s
2|1
-Euclidean bordism category over
M
. Roughly, (
244
) categorifies the construction
[
BE22
, Lemma 3.15]

(245)

\displaystyle\mathcal{L}_{0}^{2|1}(M)\to 2|1\hbox{-}{\sf EBord}(M),

that sends a nearly constant super Euclidean torus in
M
(see Definition
4.28
) to a bordism whose source and target are both the empty
2|1
-Euclidean manifold in
M
.

To explain how (
244
) and (
245
) are related, we construct variations on (
244
) that regard supercylinders as bordisms with different choices of source and target data, e.g., as bordisms from the empty set to pairs of supercircles in
M
. Compositions of these bordisms corresponds to concatenation of supercylinders or gluing to form a supertorus, and therefore generalizes the basic operations in 2-dimensional Euclidean field theory depicted in (
66
). The upshot is that the geometry
2|1
-Euclidean bordisms affords a families generalization of Witten’s argument reviewed in §
2
.

Remark 6.1
.

The constructions below are all phrased within the framework of
[
ST11
]
.
However, (
244
) and its variations are totally geometric constructions that we expect to hold in any reasonable definition of the
2|1
-Euclidean bordism category.

Remark 6.2
.

Ultimately, Theorems
1.1
,
1.2
and
1.3
only involve the supercylinders whose map to
M
is nearly constant, see §
6.5
. However, for completeness we construct families of supercylinders with an arbitrary map to
M
. Under disjoint union and composition, these families are expected to generate the full Ramond sector of
2|1\hbox{-}{\sf EBord}(M)
, i.e., the subcategory of bordisms whose objects are disjoint unions of superloops in
M
with the periodic spin structure. In this way, the results of this section give an explicit geometric description of “half” of Stolz and Teichner’s
2|1
-Euclidean bordism category over
M

[
ST11
, §4.3]
. The other half (i.e., the Neveu–Schwarz sector) can also be described using the techniques below. Following Remark
5.7
, the Neveu–Schwarz sector has a somewhat less interesting structure.

6.1.
Families of bordisms in
2|1\hbox{-}{\sf EBord}(M)

We refer to §
C.4
for a brief review of the definition and notation for Stolz and Teichner’s
2|1
-Euclidean bordism category over
M
.

Definition 6.3
.

Define maps of stacks

(246)

\displaystyle{\sf S}^{+}_{M},{\sf S}^{-}_{M}\colon{\sf Map}({\mathbb{R}}^{2|1}/{\mathbb{Z}},M)\times{\mathbb{R}}_{>0}\to{\rm Ob}(2|1\hbox{-}{\sf EBord}(M))

that to an
S
-point
(\phi,\ell)
of the source assigns the object in the groupoid
{\rm Ob}(2|1\hbox{-}{\sf EBord}(M))(S)

\displaystyle\begin{array}[]{c}{\rm S}_{\ell}=(S\times{\mathbb{R}}^{1|1})/\ell{\mathbb{Z}}=Y^{c}\subset Y=S\times{\mathbb{R}}^{2|1}/\ell{\mathbb{Z}}={\rm C}_{\ell}\xrightarrow{\Phi}M\\
Y\setminus Y^{c}\simeq(S\times{\mathring{{\mathbb{H}}}}^{2|1}_{+})/{\mathbb{Z}}\coprod(S\times{\mathring{{\mathbb{H}}}}^{2|1}_{-})/{\mathbb{Z}}=\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}Y^{+}\coprod Y^{-}&({\rm S}_{\ell}^{+},\Phi)\\
Y^{-}\coprod Y^{+}&({\rm S}_{\ell}^{-},\Phi)\end{array}\right.\end{array}

where
F_{\ell}
is the isomorphism (
4.5
) of supermanifolds and
\Phi=\phi\circ F_{\ell}
. We adopt the abbreviated notation
({\rm S}_{\ell}^{\pm},\Phi)
for objects in (
6.3
). As indicated in the second line, the difference between
({\rm S}_{\ell}^{+},\Phi)
and
({\rm S}_{\ell}^{-},\Phi)
is the partition of the collar. By pulling back families, the above assignment is natural in
S
and hence leads to the claimed maps of stacks (
246
).

Remark 6.4
.

We continue to use the lowercase notation
\phi\colon S\times{\mathbb{R}}^{2|1}/{\mathbb{Z}}\to M
for a map from the
S
-family of standard cylinders, and
\Phi=\phi\circ F_{\ell}\colon(S\times{\mathbb{R}}^{2|1})/\ell{\mathbb{Z}}\to M
for the map out of the Euclidean family determined by
\ell
. In particular,
S
-points of
{\sf Map}({\mathbb{R}}^{2|1}/{\mathbb{Z}},M)\times{\mathbb{R}}_{>0}
are specified either as
(\ell,\phi)
or
(\ell,\Phi)
, depending on which (equivalent) parameterization of
{\sf Map}({\mathbb{R}}^{2|1}/{\mathbb{Z}},M)\times{\mathbb{R}}_{>0}
is more convenient.

Remark 6.5
.

The families
{\sf S}^{+}_{M}
and
{\sf S}^{-}_{M}
are isomorphic in
2|1\hbox{-}{\sf EBord}(M)
via the isometry
\sqrt{{\rm fl}}
defined in (
215
) that exchanges the partitioning of the collar; see Lemma
6.22
. For our purposes, it turns out to be more convenient to remember this isomorphism as additional data: the isometry
\sqrt{{\rm fl}}
will ultimately endow state spaces with a real structure, essentially by the argument in
[
GPPV21
, 3.2.2]
.

Remark 6.6
.

A pair of
S
-points of
{\mathbb{R}}_{>0}\times{\sf Map}({\mathbb{R}}^{2|1}/{\mathbb{Z}},M)
determine isomorphic objects under
{\sf S}^{\pm}_{M}
if the maps
S\times{\mathbb{R}}^{2|1}/{\mathbb{Z}}\to M
agree on the 1-sided neighborhood of the inclusion
S\times{\mathbb{R}}^{1|1}/{\mathbb{Z}}\hookrightarrow S\times{\mathbb{R}}^{2|1}/{\mathbb{Z}}
corresponding to
Y^{+}
. Hence, the families in Definition
6.3
pull back from a quotient of
{\mathbb{R}}_{>0}\times{\sf Map}({\mathbb{R}}^{2|1}/{\mathbb{Z}},M)
(in sheaves) that parameterizes supercircles with the 1-sided germ of a supercylinder in
M
.

Definition 6.7
.

Define maps of stacks

\displaystyle\begin{array}[]{c}{\sf C}^{+}_{M}\colon{\sf Map}({\mathbb{R}}^{2|1}/{\mathbb{Z}},M)\times({\mathbb{R}}_{>0}\times{\mathbb{H}}^{2|1}_{+})/{\mathbb{Z}}\to{\rm Mor}(2|1\hbox{-}{\sf EBord}(M))\\
{\sf C}^{-}_{M}\colon{\sf Map}({\mathbb{R}}^{2|1}/{\mathbb{Z}},M)\times({\mathbb{R}}_{>0}\times{\mathbb{H}}^{2|1}_{-})/{\mathbb{Z}}\to{\rm Mor}(2|1\hbox{-}{\sf EBord}(M))\end{array}

that to an
S
-point
(\phi,\ell,\tau,\bar{\tau},\eta)
of the source assigns the
S
-families of supermanifolds over
M
,

(255)

\displaystyle{\rm S}_{\ell}\coprod{\rm S}_{\ell}=(S\times{\mathbb{R}}^{1|1})/\ell{\mathbb{Z}}\coprod(S\times{\mathbb{R}}^{1|1})/\ell{\mathbb{Z}}\xrightarrow{i_{0}\coprod i_{\tau,\bar{\tau},\eta}}{\rm C}_{\ell}=(S\times{\mathbb{R}}^{2|1})/\ell{\mathbb{Z}}\xrightarrow{\Phi}M

where
i_{\tau,\bar{\tau},\eta}
is the inclusion from (
191
) and
\Phi:=\phi\circ F_{\ell}
for
F_{\ell}
defined in (
4.5
).
To complete the construction of an object in the groupoid
{\rm Mor}(2|1\hbox{-}{\sf EBord}(M))(S)
from the data (
255
), we require a choice of source and target. Take

(Y_{{\sf in}}^{c},Y_{{\sf out}}^{c})=\left\{\begin{array}[]{lll}\Big(({\rm S}_{\ell}^{+},\Phi),({\rm S}_{\ell}^{+},\Phi\circ{L}_{\tau,\bar{\tau},\eta}^{-1})\Big)&\implies({\rm C}_{\ell,\tau,\bar{\tau},\eta}^{+},\Phi),&(\ell,\tau,\bar{\tau},\eta)\in(({\mathbb{R}}_{>0}\times{\mathbb{H}}^{2|1}_{+})/{\mathbb{Z}})(S)\\
\Big(({\rm S}_{\ell}^{-},\Phi),({\rm S}_{\ell}^{-},\Phi\circ{L}_{\tau,\bar{\tau},\eta}^{-1})\Big)&\implies({\rm C}_{\ell,\tau,\bar{\tau},\eta}^{-},\Phi),&(\ell,\tau,\bar{\tau},\eta)\in(({\mathbb{R}}_{>0}\times{\mathbb{H}}^{2|1}_{-})/{\mathbb{Z}})(S)\end{array}\right.

i.e., the ordering in the coproduct in (
255
) lists the source followed by the target. We adopt the abbreviated notation
({\rm C}_{\ell,\tau,\bar{\tau},\eta}^{\pm},\Phi)
for objects in (
6.7
).

Remark 6.8
.

In parallel to Remark
6.6
,
S
-points of
{\sf Map}({\mathbb{R}}^{2|1}/{\mathbb{Z}},M)\times({\mathbb{R}}_{>0}\times{\mathbb{H}}^{2|1}_{\pm})/{\mathbb{Z}}
determine isomorphic objects in
{\rm Mor}(2|1\hbox{-}{\sf EBord}(M))
under
{\sf C}^{\pm}_{M}
if the associated maps
S\times{\mathbb{R}}^{2|1}/{\mathbb{Z}}\to M
agree on a 1-sided neighborhood of the core of the bordism, and so the family pulls back from the associated quotient in sheaves.

Changing the source and target data (
6.7
) give variations on the families (
6.7
).

Definition 6.9
.

Define maps of stacks

\displaystyle\begin{array}[]{c}{\sf L}^{\pm}_{M}\colon{\sf Map}({\mathbb{R}}^{2|1}/{\mathbb{Z}},M)\times({\mathbb{R}}_{>0}\times{\mathbb{H}}^{2|1}_{\pm})/{\mathbb{Z}}\to{\rm Mor}(2|1\hbox{-}{\sf EBord}(M))\\
{\sf R}^{\pm}_{M}\colon{\sf Map}({\mathbb{R}}^{2|1}/{\mathbb{Z}},M)\times({\mathbb{R}}_{>0}\times{\mathring{{\mathbb{H}}}}^{2|1}_{\pm})/{\mathbb{Z}}\to{\rm Mor}(2|1\hbox{-}{\sf EBord}(M))\end{array}

as follows. Given an
S
-point
(\Phi,\ell,\tau,\bar{\tau},\eta)
of the source, take (
255
) as above, but choose source and target data

(Y_{{\sf in}}^{c},Y_{{\sf out}}^{c})=\left\{\begin{array}[]{lll}\Big(\big(({\rm S}_{\ell}^{\pm},\Phi)\coprod({\rm S}_{\ell}^{\mp},\Phi\circ{L}_{\tau,\bar{\tau},\eta}^{-1})\big),\emptyset\Big)&\implies({\rm L}_{\ell,\tau,\bar{\tau},\eta}^{\pm},\Phi)&(\ell,\tau,\bar{\tau},\eta)\in(({\mathbb{R}}_{>0}\times{\mathbb{H}}^{2|1}_{\pm})/{\mathbb{Z}})(S)\\
\Big(\emptyset,\big(({\rm S}_{\ell}^{\mp},\Phi)\coprod({\rm S}_{\ell}^{\pm},\Phi\circ{L}_{\tau,\bar{\tau},\eta}^{-1})\big)\Big)&\implies({\rm R}_{\ell,\tau,\bar{\tau},\eta}^{\pm},\Phi)&(\ell,\tau,\bar{\tau},\eta)\in(({\mathbb{R}}_{>0}\times{\mathring{{\mathbb{H}}}}^{2|1}_{\pm})/{\mathbb{Z}})(S),\\
\end{array}\right.

i.e., in the first line the target is empty, and in the second line the source is empty.

Remark 6.10
.

There are implicit choices of additional collar data in Definitions
6.7
and
6.9
; these choices are required to satisfy the technical
[
ST11
, Condition (+), page 30]
in the definition of the stack
{\rm Mor}(2|1\hbox{-}{\sf EBord}(M))
. When choices of collar data exist, any pair of choices lead to isomorphic objects in
{\rm Mor}(2|1\hbox{-}{\sf EBord}(M))
essentially by intersection of collars. In the families above, the requisite choices exist and so the functors (
6.7
) and (
6.9
) are unique up to canonical isomorphism. We note that the restriction on
(\tau,\bar{\tau},\eta)
to the open half plane in the case of
({\rm R}_{\ell,\tau,\bar{\tau},\eta}^{\pm},\Phi)
is necessary for the existence of collar data:
the families
({\rm R}_{\ell,\tau,\bar{\tau},\eta}^{\pm},\Phi)
in (
6.9
) do not have a limit in
{\rm Mor}(2|1\hbox{-}{\sf EBord}(M))
as
(\tau,\bar{\tau},\eta)
approaches the boundary
{\mathbb{R}}^{1|1}\subset{\mathbb{H}}^{2|1}_{\pm}
; compare the moduli of “right cylinders” in
[
ST11
, §3.2]
.

6.2.
Source, target, unit and composition of bordisms

The source and target of the families of bordisms classified by
{\sf C}^{\pm}_{M}
,
{\sf L}^{\pm}_{M}
and
{\sf R}^{\pm}_{M}
are specified in their definitions. This source and target data can equivalently be phrased in terms of 2-commuting diagrams of stacks where the 2-commutativity data is the identity. For example, we have

(265)

{\sf Map}({\mathbb{R}}^{2|1}/{\mathbb{Z}},M)\times({\mathbb{R}}_{>0}\times{\mathbb{H}}^{2|1}_{\pm})/{\mathbb{Z}}
{\rm Mor}(2|1\hbox{-}{\sf EBord}(M))
{\sf Map}({\mathbb{R}}^{2|1}/{\mathbb{Z}},M)\times{\mathbb{R}}_{>0}\times{\sf Map}({\mathbb{R}}^{2|1}/{\mathbb{Z}},M)\times{\mathbb{R}}_{>0}
{\rm Ob}(2|1\hbox{-}{\sf EBord}(M))\times{\rm Ob}(2|1\hbox{-}{\sf EBord}(M))

\Rightarrow

{\sf C}^{\pm}_{M}
({\sf pr}_{\pm},{\sf act}_{\pm})
{\sf S}^{\pm}_{M}\times{\sf S}^{\pm}_{M}
{\sf s}\times{\sf t}

where
{\sf pr}
and
{\sf act}
are the projection and action maps (compare (
234
))

\displaystyle{\sf pr},\ {\sf act}\colon{\mathbb{E}}^{2|1}\times{\sf Map}({\mathbb{R}}^{2|1}/{\mathbb{Z}},M)

\displaystyle\to

\displaystyle{\sf Map}({\mathbb{R}}^{2|1}/{\mathbb{Z}},M)

(266)

\displaystyle{\sf pr}(\Phi,\ell,\tau,\bar{\tau})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle(\Phi,\ell)

\displaystyle{\sf act}(\Phi,\ell,\tau,\bar{\tau},\eta)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle(\Phi\circ{L}_{\tau,\bar{\tau},\eta}^{-1},\ell),

for
(\ell,\tau,\bar{\tau},\eta)\in(({\mathbb{R}}_{>0}\times{\mathbb{E}}^{2|1}_{\pm})/{\mathbb{Z}})(S)
and
\Phi\colon(S\times{\mathbb{R}}^{2|1})/\ell{\mathbb{Z}}\to M
, and where
{\sf pr}_{+},{\sf act}_{+}
(respectively,
{\sf pr}_{-},{\sf act}_{-}
) are the restrictions of
{\sf pr},{\sf act}
to
{\mathbb{H}}^{2|1}_{\pm}\subset{\mathbb{E}}^{2|1}
, respectively. The other structure maps in
2|1\hbox{-}{\sf EBord}(M)
lead to similar 2-commuting diagrams; the sequence of propositions below spell these diagrams out in the cases of interest.

Proposition 6.11
.

Let
{\sf u}
be the unit functor in
2|1\hbox{-}{\sf EBord}(M)
. There is a 2-commutative diagrams of stacks

(267)

{\sf Map}({\mathbb{R}}^{2|1}/{\mathbb{Z}},M)\times{\mathbb{R}}_{>0}
{\sf Map}({\mathbb{R}}^{2|1}/{\mathbb{Z}},M)\times({\mathbb{R}}_{>0}\times{\mathbb{H}}^{2|1}_{\pm})/{\mathbb{Z}}
{\rm Ob}(2|1\hbox{-}{\sf EBord}(M))
{\rm Mor}(2|1\hbox{-}{\sf EBord}(M))

\Rightarrow

{\sf S}^{\pm}_{M}
{{\rm id}}_{{\sf Map}({\mathbb{R}}^{2|1}/{\mathbb{Z}},M)}\times i_{0}
{\sf u}
{\sf C}^{\pm}_{M}

where
i_{0}\colon{\mathbb{R}}_{>0}\hookrightarrow({\mathbb{R}}_{>0}\times{\mathbb{H}}^{2|1}_{\pm})/{\mathbb{Z}}
is inclusion along
0\in{\mathbb{H}}^{2|1}_{\pm}
. Let
{\sf c}
be the composition functor in
2|1\hbox{-}{\sf EBord}(M)
. There is a 2-commutative diagrams of stacks

(268)

{\sf Map}({\mathbb{R}}^{2|1}/{\mathbb{Z}},M)\times({\mathbb{R}}_{>0}\times{\mathbb{H}}^{2|1}_{\pm}\times{\mathbb{H}}^{2|1}_{\pm})/{\mathbb{Z}}
{\rm Mor}(2|1\hbox{-}{\sf EBord}(M))^{[2]}
{\sf Map}({\mathbb{R}}^{2|1}/{\mathbb{Z}},M)\times({\mathbb{R}}_{>0}\times{\mathbb{H}}^{2|1}_{\pm})/{\mathbb{Z}}
{\rm Mor}(2|1\hbox{-}{\sf EBord}(M))

\Rightarrow

{\sf C}^{\pm}_{M}\circ p_{2}\times{\sf C}^{\pm}_{M}\circ p_{1}
{\sf m}
{\sf C}^{\pm}_{M}
{\sf c}

where the top horizontal arrow is determined by the family
{\sf C}^{\pm}_{M}
and the maps

\displaystyle\begin{array}[]{l}p_{i}\colon{\sf Map}({\mathbb{R}}^{2|1}/{\mathbb{Z}},M)\times({\mathbb{R}}_{>0}\times{\mathbb{H}}^{2|1}_{\pm}\times{\mathbb{H}}^{2|1}_{\pm})/{\mathbb{Z}}\to{\sf Map}({\mathbb{R}}^{2|1}/{\mathbb{Z}},M)\times({\mathbb{R}}_{>0}\times{\mathbb{H}}^{2|1}_{\pm})/{\mathbb{Z}},\\
p_{1}\big(\Phi,\ell,(\tau^{\prime},\bar{\tau}^{\prime},\eta^{\prime}),(\tau,\bar{\tau},\eta)\big)=(\Phi,\ell,\tau,\bar{\tau},\eta),\\
p_{2}\big(\Phi,\ell,(\tau,\bar{\tau},\eta),(\tau^{\prime},\bar{\tau}^{\prime},\eta^{\prime})\big)=(\Phi\circ{L}_{\tau,\bar{\tau},\eta}^{-1},\ell,\tau^{\prime},\bar{\tau}^{\prime},\eta^{\prime})\end{array}

and
{\sf m}
is defined as

(273)

\displaystyle{\sf m}\big(\Phi,\ell,(\tau^{\prime},\bar{\tau}^{\prime},\eta^{\prime}),(\tau,\bar{\tau},\eta)\big)=(\Phi,\ell,\tau^{\prime}+\tau,\bar{\tau}^{\prime}+\bar{\tau}+\eta^{\prime}\eta,\eta^{\prime}+\eta).

Proof.

The commutativity of the diagram (
267
) is almost immediate; the only subtlety comes the choice of collars in the construction of the family
{\sf C}^{\pm}_{M}
. Indeed, rather than a strictly commutative diagram this choice leads to 2-commutativity data gotten from the isomorphism between objects of
{\rm Mor}(2|1\hbox{-}{\sf EBord}(M))
from shrinking the collar in (
6.3
); see Remark
6.10
.

Turning to the diagram (
268
), the definition of the
S
-family
({\rm C}^{\pm}_{\ell,\tau,\bar{\tau},\eta},\Phi)
and the maps (
6.11
) gives a map to the
S
-point of the fibered product (the strict and weak fibered product coincide, see Remark
C.29
)

\big(\ell,(\tau^{\prime},\bar{\tau}^{\prime},\eta^{\prime}),(\tau,\bar{\tau},\eta),\Phi\big)\mapsto\big(({\rm C}^{\pm}_{\ell,\tau^{\prime},\bar{\tau}^{\prime},\eta^{\prime}},\Phi\circ{L}_{\tau,\bar{\tau},\eta}^{-1}),({\rm C}^{\pm}_{\ell,\tau,\bar{\tau},\eta},\Phi)\big).

The construction of 2-commutativity data comes from unpacking the definition of the functor
{\sf c}
from
[
ST11
, page 20]
, i.e., composition of bordisms. Consider the diagram

(274)

\displaystyle{\rm S}_{\ell}\coprod{\rm S}_{\ell}\coprod{\rm S}_{\ell}\xrightarrow{i_{0}\coprod i_{\tau,\bar{\tau},\eta}\coprod i_{(\tau^{\prime},\bar{\tau}^{\prime},\eta^{\prime})\cdot(\tau,\bar{\tau},\eta)}}(S\times{\mathbb{R}}^{2|1})/{\mathbb{Z}}\xrightarrow{\Phi}M

where the inclusions are as in (
191
).
By forgetting any one of these three inclusions, we obtain three possible
S
-points of the source of
{\sf C}^{\pm}_{M}
via the input datum (
255
): forgetting
i_{(\tau^{\prime},\bar{\tau}^{\prime},\eta^{\prime})\cdot(\tau,\bar{\tau},\eta)}
gives the
S
-family of bordisms
({\rm C}_{\ell,\tau,\bar{\tau},\eta}^{\pm},\Phi)
, forgetting
i_{\tau,\bar{\tau},\eta}
gives the
S
-family of bordisms
({\rm C}^{\pm}_{\ell,(\tau^{\prime},\bar{\tau}^{\prime},\eta^{\prime})\cdot(\tau,\bar{\tau},\eta)},\Phi)
, and forgetting
i_{0}
gives a family that is isomorphic to
({\rm C}_{\ell,\tau^{\prime},\bar{\tau}^{\prime},\eta^{\prime}},\Phi\circ{L}_{\tau,\bar{\tau},\eta}^{-1})
with isomorphism specified by the left-translation map
{L}_{\tau,\bar{\tau},\eta}
. These three
S
-families of bordisms gotten from (
274
) coincide with the two projections out of the fibered product and the value of the functor
{\sf c}
. The only subtle aspect in commutativity of the diagram (
268
) is again the choice of collars in the definitions of these families of bordisms, which in turn depends on the choices in the construction of the functor
{\sf C}^{\pm}_{M}
. Since any pair of choices lead to isomorphic bordisms, we obtain canonical 2-commutativity data in (
268
).
∎

.
∎

6.3.
Additional relations from isometries of bordisms

Define the map

\displaystyle{\sf inv}\colon{\sf Map}({\mathbb{R}}^{2|1}/{\mathbb{Z}},M)\times({\mathbb{R}}_{>0}\times{\mathbb{H}}^{2|1}_{\pm})/{\mathbb{Z}}

\displaystyle\to

(294)

\displaystyle(\Phi,\ell,\tau,\bar{\tau},\eta)

\displaystyle\mapsto

\displaystyle(\Phi\circ{L}_{\tau,\bar{\tau},\eta}^{-1},\ell,-\tau,-\bar{\tau},-\eta).

Lemma 6.20
.

There are equalities of maps of sheaves

{\sf act}_{\pm}={\sf pr}_{\mp}\circ{\sf inv},\qquad{\sf pr}_{\pm}={\sf act}_{\mp}\circ{\sf inv}.

.

Proof.

This follows from (
294
) and the definitions of
{\sf pr}_{\pm}
and
{\sf act}_{\pm}
,

{\sf act}_{\pm}(\Phi,\ell,\tau,\bar{\tau},\eta)=(\Phi\circ{L}_{\tau,\bar{\tau},\eta}^{-1},\ell),\qquad{\sf pr}_{\pm}(\Phi,\ell,\tau,\bar{\tau},\eta)=(\Phi,\ell)

determined by restriction of the map (
266
).
∎

Proposition 6.21
.

Let
{\sf L}^{\pm}_{M}\circ\sigma
denote the functor
{\sf L}^{\pm}_{M}
post-composed with the isomorphism in

There is a 2-commutative triangle

(295)

{\sf Map}({\mathbb{R}}^{2|1}/{\mathbb{Z}},M)\times({\mathbb{R}}_{>0}\times{\mathbb{H}}^{2|1}_{\mp})/{\mathbb{Z}}

{\sf L}^{\pm}_{M}\circ\sigma
{\sf inv}
{\sf L}^{\mp}_{M}

for the left vertical arrow (

).

Proof.

Starting with the data that determines

,

\displaystyle(S\times{\mathbb{R}}^{1|1})/\ell{\mathbb{Z}}\coprod(S\times{\mathbb{R}}^{1|1})/\ell{\mathbb{Z}}\xhookrightarrow{i_{0}\coprod i_{\tau,\bar{\tau},\eta}}(S\times{\mathbb{R}}^{2|1})/\ell{\mathbb{Z}}\xrightarrow{\Phi}M.

exchanging the factors of the coproduct and acting on

∎

Below we will use (degenerate) notation
\sqrt{{\rm Fl}}
to denote the maps of sheaves

(296)

Proposition 6.24

Remark 6.27
.

Morally, composition and disjoint union of small bordisms generates a subcategory
2|1\hbox{-{\sf ebord}}(M)\subset 2|1\hbox{-}{\sf EBord}(M)
of small bordisms in

, and
2|1\hbox{-{\sf eft}}^{n}(M)
in Theorem
1.1
considers degree
n
field theories defined on these subcategories. However, formulating the notion of generating a subcategory in a category internal to symmetric monoidal stacks gets quite technical.

In particular, the families (
6.26

6.22
determines maps of stacks

(318)

\displaystyle{\rm Ob}(\mathscr{C}^{2|1}(M))\to{\rm Ob}(2|1\hbox{-}{\sf EBord}(M)),\quad{\rm Mor}(\mathscr{C}^{2|1}(M))\to{\rm Mor}(2|1\hbox{-}{\sf EBord}(M))

for the object and morphism stacks in Definition

.

Proposition 6.28
.

. The 2-morphisms in this proposition are canonical (coming from intersecting collars of bordisms), implying that the coherence property for these 2-isomorphisms is automatic. This completes the construction of the internal functor (

).

The compatibility of the functor (

) with the additional structures follows from Propositions

and

witnessing the claimed compatibility.
∎

7.
2|1
-Euclidean field theories of degree
n

In this section we compute the values of twisted
2|1
-Euclidean field theories over
M
on the bordisms constructed in the previous section. Following Remark
6.2
, we expect this to characterize the “half” of the data of a twisted
2|1
-Euclidean field theory, i.e., the Ramond sector values.
We then specialize to the degree
n
twist relevant to conjecture (
1
). A complete construction of the degree
n
twist has yet to appear; it is expected to be the unique supersymmetric extension of the 2-dimensional chiral free fermions constructed in
[
ST11
, §5-6]
, see Hypothesis
7.10
. Below we show that such a supersymmetric extension exists and is unique on the Ramond sector bordisms from the previous section. This partial construction suffices for our purposes. In Remark
7.20
, we indicate a full construction of
{\rm Fer}_{n}
that depends upon a generators and relations presentation of
2|1\hbox{-}{\sf EBord}({\rm pt})
suggested in
[
ST11
]
.

7.1.
The values of twisted field theories

A
twist
for the
2|1
-Euclidean bordism category over
M
is an internal functor
\mathscr{T}\colon 2|1\hbox{-}{\sf EBord}(M)\to{\sf{TA}}
valued in the Morita category. A
twisted
2|1
-Euclidean field theory over
M
is an internal natural transformation
E

(320)

2|1\hbox{-}{\sf EBord}(M)
{\sf{TA}}.
E\Downarrow
\mathbbold{1}
\mathscr{T}

We refer to §
C
for a review of these definitions from
[
ST11
]
. In the following series of propositions, we describe the values of a twisted
2|1
-Euclidean field theory on the families of bordisms constructed in the previous section.

Proposition 7.1
.

A twist
\mathscr{T}\colon 2|1\hbox{-}{\sf EBord}(M)\to{\sf{TA}}
determines data

i.

a bundle of algebras
\mathscr{T}({\sf S}^{\pm}_{M})
over
{\sf Map}({\mathbb{R}}^{2|1}/{\mathbb{Z}},M)\times{\mathbb{R}}_{>0}
;

ii.

bundles of bimodules

{}_{{\sf act}_{\pm}^{*}\mathscr{T}({\sf S}^{\pm}_{M})}\mathscr{T}({\sf C}^{\pm}_{M})_{{\sf pr}_{\pm}^{*}\mathscr{T}({\sf S}^{\pm}_{M})},\quad_{\mathbb{C}}\mathscr{T}({\sf L}^{\pm}_{M})_{{\sf pr}_{\pm}^{*}\mathscr{T}({\sf S}^{\pm}_{M})\otimes{\sf act}_{\pm}^{*}\mathscr{T}({\sf S}^{\mp}_{M})},\quad

over
{\sf Map}({\mathbb{R}}^{2|1}/{\mathbb{Z}},M)\times({\mathbb{R}}_{>0}\times{\mathbb{H}}^{2|1}_{\pm})/{\mathbb{Z}}
and

{}_{{\sf pr}_{\pm}^{*}\mathscr{T}({\sf S}^{\mp}_{M})\otimes{\sf act}_{\pm}^{*}\mathscr{T}({\sf S}^{\pm}_{M})}\mathscr{T}({\sf R}^{\pm}_{M})_{\mathbb{C}}

over
{\sf Map}({\mathbb{R}}^{2|1}/{\mathbb{Z}},M)\times({\mathbb{R}}_{>0}\times{\mathring{{\mathbb{H}}}}^{2|1}_{\pm})/{\mathbb{Z}}
,

iii.

a
{\mathbb{E}}^{2|1}\rtimes{\rm Spin}(2)\times{\rm SL}_{2}({\mathbb{Z}})
-equivariant vector bundle
\mathscr{T}(\mathcal{L}^{2|1}(M))
over the sheaf
\mathcal{L}^{2|1}(M)
defined in (
156
).

Proof.

By Definition
C.3
of an internal functor, the twist determines maps of stacks

{\rm Ob}(2|1\hbox{-}{\sf EBord}(M))\to{\rm Ob}({\sf{TA}}),\qquad{\rm Mor}(2|1\hbox{-}{\sf EBord}(M))\to{\rm Mor}({\sf{TA}})

that are compatible with the source and target functors. Evaluating these on the indicated families of objects of the source stacks recovers the statement in the proposition.
∎

Proposition 7.2
.

Using the notation from Proposition
7.1
, a
\mathscr{T}
-twisted field theory
E
determines data

i.

bundles of left modules
{}_{\mathscr{T}({\sf S}^{\pm}_{M})}E({\sf S}^{\pm}_{M})
over
{\sf Map}({\mathbb{R}}^{2|1}/{\mathbb{Z}},M)\times{\mathbb{R}}_{>0}
,

ii.

maps of bimodule bundles over
{\sf Map}({\mathbb{R}}^{2|1}/{\mathbb{Z}},M)\times({\mathbb{R}}_{>0}\times{\mathbb{H}}^{2|1}_{\pm})/{\mathbb{Z}}

\displaystyle E({\sf C}^{\pm}_{M})\colon\mathscr{T}({\sf C}^{\pm}_{M})\otimes_{{\sf pr}_{\pm}^{*}\mathscr{T}({\sf S}^{\pm}_{M})}{\sf pr}_{\pm}^{*}E({\sf S}^{\pm}_{M})\to{\sf act}_{\pm}^{*}E({\sf S}^{\pm}_{M}),

\displaystyle E({\sf L}^{\pm}_{M})\colon\mathscr{T}({\sf L}^{\pm}_{M})\otimes_{{\sf pr}_{\pm}^{*}\mathscr{T}({\sf S}^{\pm}_{M})\otimes{\sf act}_{\pm}^{*}\mathscr{T}({\sf S}^{\mp}_{M})}{\sf pr}_{\pm}^{*}E({\sf S}^{\pm}_{M})\otimes{\sf act}_{\pm}^{*}E({\sf S}^{\mp}_{M})\to\underline{{\mathbb{C}}},

and maps of bimodule bundles over
{\sf Map}({\mathbb{R}}^{2|1}/{\mathbb{Z}},M)\times({\mathbb{R}}_{>0}\times{\mathring{{\mathbb{H}}}}^{2|1}_{\pm})/{\mathbb{Z}}

\displaystyle E({\sf R}^{\pm}_{M})\colon\mathscr{T}({\sf R}^{\pm}_{M})\to{\sf pr}_{\pm}^{*}E({\sf S}^{\mp}_{M})\otimes{\sf act}_{\pm}^{*}E({\sf S}^{\pm}_{M}),

for the projection and action maps
{\sf pr}^{\pm}
and
{\sf act}^{\pm}
defined in (
266
), and

iii.

a
{\mathbb{E}}^{2|1}\rtimes{\rm Spin}(2)\times{\rm SL}_{2}({\mathbb{Z}})
-invariant map of equivariant vector bundles

E(\mathcal{L}^{2|1}(M))\colon\mathscr{T}(\mathcal{L}^{2|1}(M))\to\underline{{\mathbb{C}}}

over the sheaf
\mathcal{L}^{2|1}(M)
, taking the trivial equivariant structure on
\underline{{\mathbb{C}}}
.

Proof.

By Definition
C.4
of an internal natural transformation, a twisted field theory supplies the data of a map of stacks
\eta\colon{\rm Ob}(2|1\hbox{-}{\sf EBord}(M))\to{\rm Mor}({\sf{TA}})
, and hence evaluating on the families
E({\sf S}^{\pm}_{M})
determines bundles of bimodules over
{\sf Map}({\mathbb{R}}^{2|1}/{\mathbb{Z}},M)\times{\mathbb{R}}_{>0}

\displaystyle\begin{array}[]{c}E({\sf S}^{\pm}_{M})\in{\rm Mor}({\sf{TA}}),\quad{\rm with}\quad{\sf t}(E({\sf S}^{\pm}_{M}))=\mathscr{T}({\sf S}^{\pm}_{M}),\ {\sf s}(E({\sf S}^{\pm}_{M}))=\underline{{\mathbb{C}}}.\end{array}

The right action by
\underline{{\mathbb{C}}}
is no additional data, so
E({\sf S}^{\pm}_{M})
are simply bundles of left modules. An internal natural transformation further specifies a compatibility 2-morphism
\rho
in (
503
) for every family in
{\rm Mor}(2|1\hbox{-}{\sf EBord}(M))
; this yields the data in (ii) and (iii) by the argument from
[
ST11
, page 52]
. For example,
\rho
evaluated on the family
{\sf C}^{\pm}_{M}
is a map of left modules

E({\sf C}^{\pm}_{M})\colon\mathscr{T}({\sf C}^{\pm}_{M})\otimes_{{\sf pr}_{\pm}^{*}\mathscr{T}({\sf C}^{\pm}_{M})}{\sf pr}_{\pm}^{*}E({\sf S}^{\pm}_{M})\to{\sf act}_{\pm}^{*}E({\sf S}^{\pm}_{M})

which is the first map of bimodules listed above.
∎

Remark 7.3
.

By Remarks
6.6
and
6.8
, the algebra and bimodule bundles in Proposition
7.1
and
7.2
satisfy an additional property: they are isomorphic to the pullback of a bundle along a quotient that identifies shrinking collars. For example, the algebra bundle
\mathscr{T}({\sf S}^{\pm}_{M})
pulls back along a quotient map
{\mathbb{R}}_{>0}\times{\sf Map}({\mathbb{R}}^{2|1}/{\mathbb{Z}},M)\to{\mathbb{R}}_{>0}\times{\sf Map}({\mathbb{R}}^{2|1}/{\mathbb{Z}},M)/{\sim}
where the target is the sheaf of Euclidean superloops in
M
with the 1-sided germ of a supercylinder.

In the following two propositions, we record the data and property that a twist and a twisted field theory assigns to the compositions of bordisms computed in §

.

Lemma 7.6

A twist determines the data of an isomorphism of right

∎

As before, let

\mathscr{T}({\sf R}^{\pm}_{M})

\sqrt{{\rm Fl}}^{*}({\sf pr}_{\mp}^{*}E({\sf S}^{\pm}_{M})\otimes{\sf act}_{\mp}^{*}E({\sf S}^{\mp}))

over

and

Proof.

The isometries in Lemma
6.22
give isomorphisms between maps to
{\rm Ob}(2|1\hbox{-}{\sf EBord}(M))
and

{\sf S}^{\pm}_{M}

. To deduce the property that

is the parity involution uses that a twisted field theory is a
flip preserving
internal natural transformation (see
[

, §2.6]
) where the spin involution (
148
) of super Euclidean families furnishes the flip
{\rm Fl}=\sqrt{{\rm Fl}}\circ\sqrt{{\rm Fl}}
. Similarly, the isomorphisms (

\mathscr{T}
to the commutative diagrams in Lemma

. Turning to a twisted field theory,
\sqrt{{\rm Fl}}
provides the isomorphism of modules (

). The remaining commutative diagrams follow from the diagram in Lemma
6.22
and the definition of an internal natural transformation.
∎

7.2.
Reflection structures

Next we unpack the data of a reflection structure from §
C.3
for twists and twisted field theories. We recall the notation (
221
) for the conjugation functor applied to a sheaf, and the maps of sheaves (
301
)-(
303
) used to describe the restrictions of the reflection structure on
2|1\hbox{-}{\sf EBord}(M)
in Proposition
6.23
.

Lemma 7.8
.

A reflection structure for a twist
\mathscr{T}\colon 2|1\hbox{-}{\sf EBord}(M)\to{\sf{TA}}
determines invertible
\mathscr{T}({\sf S}^{\pm}_{M})
-
\dagger^{*}\overline{\mathscr{T}({\sf S}^{\mp}_{M}))}
-bimodules
\mathscr{R}^{\pm}\to{\sf Map}({\mathbb{R}}^{2|1}/{\mathbb{Z}},M)\times{\mathbb{R}}_{>0}
with isomorphisms to the identity bimodule

(332)

\displaystyle\mathscr{R}^{\pm}\otimes_{\dagger^{*}\overline{\mathscr{T}({\sf S}^{\mp}_{M}))}}\dagger^{*}\overline{\mathscr{R}}^{\mp}\simeq{{\rm id}}_{\mathscr{T}({\sf S}^{\pm}_{M})}.

A reflection structure for
\mathscr{T}
further specifies isomorphisms of bimodules

\displaystyle\mathscr{T}({\sf C}^{\pm}_{M})\otimes_{{\sf act}_{\pm}^{*}\mathscr{T}({\sf S}^{\pm}_{M})}{\sf act}_{\pm}^{*}\mathscr{R}^{\pm}\xrightarrow{\sim}{\sf pr}_{\pm}^{*}\mathscr{R}^{\pm}\otimes_{\dagger^{*}\overline{{\sf pr}}_{\mp}^{*}\overline{\mathscr{T}({\sf S}^{\mp}_{M})}}\dagger^{*}\overline{\mathscr{T}({\sf C}^{\mp}_{M})},

\displaystyle\mathscr{T}({\sf L}^{\pm}_{M})\otimes_{{\sf pr}_{\pm}^{*}\mathscr{T}({\sf S}^{\pm}_{M})\otimes{\sf act}_{\pm}^{*}\mathscr{T}({\sf S}^{\mp}_{M})}({\sf pr}_{\pm}^{*}\mathscr{R}^{\pm}\otimes{\sf act}_{\pm}^{*}\mathscr{R}^{\mp})\xrightarrow{\sim}\dagger^{*}\overline{\mathscr{T}({\sf L}^{-}_{M})},

over
{\sf Map}({\mathbb{R}}^{2|1}/{\mathbb{Z}},M)\times({\mathbb{R}}_{>0}\times{\mathbb{H}}^{2|1}_{\pm})/{\mathbb{Z}}
, and

(333)

\displaystyle\mathscr{T}({\sf R}^{+}_{M})\xrightarrow{\sim}({\sf pr}_{\pm}^{*}\mathscr{R}^{\mp}\otimes{\sf act}_{\pm}^{*}\mathscr{R}^{\pm})\otimes_{\dagger^{*}(\overline{{\sf pr}}_{\mp}^{*}\overline{\mathscr{T}({\sf S}^{\pm}_{M})}\otimes\overline{{\sf act}}_{\mp}^{*}\overline{\mathscr{T}({\sf S}^{\mp}_{M})})}\dagger^{*}\overline{\mathscr{T}({\sf R}^{-}_{M})},

over
{\sf Map}({\mathbb{R}}^{2|1}/{\mathbb{Z}},M)\times({\mathbb{R}}_{>0}\times{\mathring{{\mathbb{H}}}}^{2|1}_{\pm})/{\mathbb{Z}}
satisfying an involutive property.

Proof.

From Definition
C.31
, a reflection structure for the twist
\mathscr{T}
determines an invertible internal natural isomorphism, i.e., a map of stacks

\eta\colon{\rm Ob}(2|1\hbox{-}{\sf EBord}({\rm pt}))\to{\rm Mor}({\sf{TA}})^{\times}

valued in invertible bimodules together with coherence data
\rho
satisfying properties. Applying
\eta
to the family
{\sf S}^{\pm}_{M}
, we obtain the bundle of invertible bimodules,
\mathscr{R}^{\pm}
. Restricting
\rho
to the families
{\sf C}^{\pm}_{M}
,
{\sf L}^{\pm}_{M}
and
{\sf R}^{\pm}_{M}
, a reflection structure provides the claimed isomorphisms of bimodules. A reflection structure also specifies involutive data (see Remark
C.22
) which when evaluated on
{\sf S}^{\pm}_{M}
yields the isomorphism (
332
) and imposes involutive properties on the isomorphisms of bimodules.
∎

\underline{{\mathbb{C}}}
\dagger^{*}(\overline{\mathscr{T}({\sf L}^{\mp}_{M})}\otimes_{\overline{{\sf pr}}_{\mp}^{*}\overline{\mathscr{T}({\sf S}^{\mp}_{M})}\otimes\overline{{\sf act}}_{\mp}^{*}\overline{\mathscr{T}({\sf S}^{\pm}_{M})}}\overline{{\sf pr}}_{\mp}^{*}\overline{E({\sf S}^{\mp}_{M})}\otimes\overline{{\sf act}}_{\mp}^{*}\overline{E({\sf S}^{\pm}_{M})}

Proof.

, reflection positivity data for a twisted field theory determines an isomorphism between the maps of stacks

. Evaluating this isomorphism on the families

and
