∎

7.3.
The degree
n
supersymmetric twist and its restrictions

In §
C.5
we review Stolz and Teichner’s construction of the chiral free fermion twist
{\rm Fer}_{n}\colon 2\hbox{-}{\sf EBord}(M)\to{\sf{TA}}
as a functor out of the 2-dimensional Euclidean bordism category (without supersymmetry). We recall the functor
\mathcal{S}\colon 2\hbox{-}{\sf EBord}(M)\to 2|1\hbox{-}{\sf EBord}(M)
from Lemma
4.7
that sends a Euclidean bordism to a super Euclidean bordism.

Hypothesis 7.10
.

The
n
-chiral free fermions constructed in
[
ST11
, §5.3]
have a
supersymmetric extension
denoted
s{\rm Fer}_{n}

(337)

2\hbox{-}{\sf EBord}
{\sf{TA}}
\varphi\ \Downarrow
2|1\hbox{-}{\sf EBord}({\rm pt})
\mathcal{S}
{\rm Fer}_{n}
s{\rm Fer}_{n}

that is unique up to an internal natural isomorphism
\varphi
. The reflection structure for the chiral fermions (see (
533
)) has a supersymmetric extension that is unique up to isomorphism of internal natural transformations.

Remark 7.11
.

Hypothesis
7.10
is part of a larger expectation: 2-dimensional chiral conformal field theories should determine
\mathcal{N}=(0,1)
supersymmetric ones, e.g., see
[
JF20
, Page 12]
or
[
LP22
, §1]
. This connection is the bridge between Segal’s early work
[
Seg88
,
Seg04
]
and the Stolz–Teichner program; see
[
ST04
, page 50-51]
and
[
ST11
, §5.2]
. The essential idea is that a chiral theory has
\bar{L}_{0}=0
(using the notation from §
2.4
), and hence a supersymmetric extension is uniquely determined by taking
\bar{G}_{0}=0
. This manipulation in super Lie algebras exponentiates to the semigroup of cylinders. In particular, this argument applies to the chiral free fermions, see Proposition
7.15
.

Notation 7.12
.

In this subsection we will use the notation
s{\rm Fer}_{n}
to denote the supersymmetric extension (
337
) to avoid confusion with the (nonsupersymmetric) twist
{\rm Fer}_{n}
; in later sections we will revert to the simpler notation
{\rm Fer}_{n}\colon 2|1\hbox{-}{\sf EBord}\to{\sf{TA}}
.

Our next goal is to verify Hypothesis
7.10
in the Ramond sector, i.e., for the
2|1
-Euclidean bordisms relevant to Theorem
1.1
. We begin with a definition.

Definition 7.13
.

A supersemigroup representation
\varphi\colon{\mathbb{H}}_{\pm}\to{\rm End}(V)
is
holomorphic
if
\partial_{\bar{\tau}}\varphi=0
. Equivalently, for an
S
-point
(\tau,\bar{\tau})\in{\mathbb{H}}_{\pm}(S)
, we have
\varphi(\tau,\bar{\tau})=\varphi(\tau)\colon S\to{\rm End}(V)
is independent of
\bar{\tau}
.

The following has been used previously by Stolz and Teichner to lift a chiral field theory to a super Euclidean one, e.g., see
[
ST11
, Proof of Theorem 1.15, second part]
.

Lemma 7.14
.

A holomorphic representation of the semigroup
{\mathbb{H}}_{\pm}
has a unique extension to a representation of the supersemigroup
{\mathbb{H}}_{\pm}^{2|1}
along the canonical inclusion
{\mathbb{H}}_{\pm}\subset{\mathbb{H}}_{\pm}^{2|1}
.

Proof.

For
p
the projection, consider the composition

(338)

\displaystyle\tilde{\varphi}\colon{\mathbb{H}}_{\pm}^{2|1}\xrightarrow{p}{\mathbb{H}}_{\pm}\xrightarrow{\varphi}{\rm End}(V).

This composition is a super semigroup representation

\tilde{\varphi}(\tau,\bar{\tau},\eta)\cdot\tilde{\varphi}(\tau^{\prime},\bar{\tau}^{\prime},\eta^{\prime})=\varphi(\tau)\cdot\tilde{\varphi}(\tau^{\prime})=\varphi(\tau+\tau^{\prime})=\tilde{\varphi}(\tau+\tau^{\prime},\bar{\tau}+\bar{\tau}^{\prime}+\eta\eta^{\prime},\eta+\eta^{\prime}).

We caution that the projection
p
is
not
a homomorphism, and the semigroup homomorphism property for
\tilde{\varphi}
critically uses holomorphicity of
\varphi
. Since
p
is a 1-sided inverse to the canonical inclusion
{\mathbb{H}}_{\pm}\subset{\mathbb{H}}_{\pm}^{2|1}
, the composition (
338
) is indeed the unique extension of
\varphi
.
∎

We recall the Lie category
\mathscr{C}=\mathscr{C}({\rm pt})
of Euclidean cylinders from Definition
5.4
.

Proposition 7.15
.

Consider the restriction of
{\rm Fer}_{n}
to the subcategory
\mathscr{C}
of Euclidean cylinders. There is an extension to the category of Euclidean supercylinders
\mathscr{C}^{2|1}
,

(339)

\mathscr{C}
{\sf{TA}}
\mathscr{C}^{2|1}
{\rm Fer}_{n}
\mathcal{S}
s{\rm Fer}_{n}
\Downarrow

along
\mathcal{S}
, Stolz and Teichner’s superfication functor (
146
). Furthermore, the reflection structure (
533
) on
{\rm Fer}_{n}
has a unique extension to
s{\rm Fer}_{n}
using the reflection structure (
5.22
) on the internal category
\mathscr{C}^{2|1}
.

Proof.

It suffices to prove the lemma for
n=-1
. The restriction of
{\rm Fer}_{-1}
to
\mathscr{C}
is characterized by the first family of bimodules in (
526
) and the anti-involution (
531
). The superfication functor (
146
) on Euclidean cylinders is given by the internal functor

\mathscr{C}=\left(\begin{array}[]{c}(({\mathbb{R}}_{>0}\times{\mathbb{H}}_{+})/{\mathbb{Z}}\coprod({\mathbb{R}}_{>0}\times{\mathbb{H}}_{-})/{\mathbb{Z}})/\!\!/{\mathbb{Z}}/4\\
\downarrow\downarrow\\
({\mathbb{R}}_{>0}\coprod{\mathbb{R}}_{>0})/\!\!/{\mathbb{Z}}/4\end{array}\right)\xhookrightarrow{\mathcal{S}}\left(\begin{array}[]{c}(({\mathbb{R}}_{>0}\times{\mathbb{H}}^{2|1}_{+})/{\mathbb{Z}}\coprod({\mathbb{R}}_{>0}\times{\mathbb{H}}^{2|1}_{-})/{\mathbb{Z}})/\!\!/{\mathbb{Z}}/4\\
\downarrow\downarrow\\
({\mathbb{R}}_{>0}\coprod{\mathbb{R}}_{>0})/\!\!/{\mathbb{Z}}/4\end{array}\right)=\mathscr{C}^{2|1}.

On objects,
\mathcal{S}
is the identity map, and on morphisms
\mathcal{S}
is determined by the canonical inclusion of the reduced manifold. We define the extension
s{\rm Fer}_{-1}
on
{\rm Ob}(\mathscr{C}^{2|1})
to be equal to the value (
523
) of
{\rm Fer}_{-1}
on
{\rm Ob}(\mathscr{C})
, i.e., the bundle of algebras
{\rm Fer}({\rm S}_{\ell}^{\pm})
where the generator of
{\mathbb{Z}}/4
acts by the anti-involution (
531
). The value of
{\rm Fer}_{-1}
on morphisms in
\mathscr{C}
comes from an
{\mathbb{R}}_{>0}
-family of holomorphic
{\mathbb{H}}
-actions on
{\rm Fer}({\rm S}_{\ell}^{\pm})
. By Lemma
7.14
, these actions have a unique extension to
{\mathbb{H}}_{\pm}^{2|1}
-actions, with formula given by

(340)

\displaystyle\tilde{\varphi}_{\ell}^{\pm}(\tau,\bar{\tau},\eta)=\varphi_{\ell}^{\pm}(\tau),

where
\varphi_{\ell}^{\pm}(\tau)
is determined by (
525
).
With this fixed,
s{\rm Fer}_{-1}
is defined in the same way as
{\rm Fer}_{-1}
: the value of
s{\rm Fer}_{-1}
on
{\rm Mor}(\mathscr{C}^{2|1})
is the family of bimodules gotten by viewing
{\rm Fer}({\rm S}_{\ell}^{\pm})
as a bimodule over itself with left-action twisted by the family of algebra automorphisms (
340
). The composition and unitor data are canonically determined by the fact that
\tilde{\varphi}^{\pm}_{\ell}(0)={{\rm id}}
and
\tilde{\varphi}^{\pm}_{\ell}(\tau)\cdot\tilde{\varphi}^{\pm}_{\ell}(\tau^{\prime})=\tilde{\varphi}^{\pm}_{\ell}(\tau+\tau^{\prime})
. The
{\mathbb{Z}}/4
-action on the resulting family of bimodules again comes from (
531
). This completes the construction of an internal functor
s{\rm Fer}_{-1}\colon\mathscr{C}^{2|1}\to{\sf{TA}}
, and (by inspection) the diagram (
339
) strictly commutes.

The reflection structure for
{\rm Fer}_{-1}
is determined by a
*
-superalgebra structure (
533
) on the algebra
{\rm Fer}({\rm S}_{\ell}^{\pm})
which then determines involutions on
{\rm Fer}({\rm S}_{\ell}^{\pm})
regarded as a bimodule. Since the objects in
\mathscr{C}
and
\mathscr{C}^{2|1}
are the same, this reflection structure uniquely extends to
s{\rm Fer}_{-1}
using the same
*
-superalgebra structure.
∎

Corollary 7.16
.

Using (
340
), the values (
526
) of the internal functor
{\rm Fer}_{-1}\colon 2\hbox{-}{\sf EBord}\to{\sf{TA}}
have unique extensions
to supersymmetric families,

\displaystyle s{\rm Fer}_{n}({\rm L}_{\ell,\tau,\bar{\tau},\eta}^{\pm})=({\rm Fer}_{\ell}^{\mp})_{{\rm Fer}_{\ell}^{\mp}\otimes^{\tau}{\rm Fer}_{\ell}^{\mp}}\qquad s{\rm Fer}_{n}({\rm R}_{\ell,\tau,\bar{\tau},\eta}^{\pm})={}_{{\rm Fer}^{\pm}_{\ell}\otimes{}^{\tau}{\rm Fer}_{\ell}^{\mp}}{\rm Fer}_{\ell}^{\mp}

and these values are compatible with composition in
2|1\hbox{-}{\sf EBord}
. Furthermore, the reflection structure (
533
) has a unique extension to the above families.

Definition 7.17
.

Define the
Clifford twist
as the internal functor
{\rm Cl}_{n}\colon\mathscr{C}\to{\sf{TA}}
or
{\rm Cl}_{n}\colon\mathscr{C}^{2|1}\to{\sf{TA}}
whose value on objects is the coproduct of trivial algebra bundles

\underline{{\mathbb{C}}{\rm l}}_{+n}\coprod\underline{{\mathbb{C}}{\rm l}}_{-n}\to({\mathbb{R}}_{>0}\coprod{\mathbb{R}}_{>0})/\!\!/{\mathbb{Z}}/4={\rm Ob}(\mathscr{C})={\rm Ob}(\mathscr{C}^{2|1})

where the generator of
{\mathbb{Z}}/4
acts by the algebra isomorphism

(341)

\displaystyle\alpha_{\mp}\colon{\mathbb{C}}{\rm l}_{\mp 1}\to{\mathbb{C}}{\rm l}_{\pm 1},\quad v\mapsto\pm iv,\quad v\in{\mathbb{C}}.

The value of the Clifford twist on morphisms is the coproduct of trivial bimodule bundles gotten from viewing
{\mathbb{C}}{\rm l}_{\pm n}
as a bimodule over itself, with
{\mathbb{Z}}/4
-equivariant structure again determined by (
341
). Unit and composition data are determined by the canonical isomorphisms of bimodules
{\mathbb{C}}{\rm l}_{\pm n}\otimes_{{\mathbb{C}}{\rm l}_{\pm n}}{\mathbb{C}}{\rm l}_{\pm n}\simeq{\mathbb{C}}{\rm l}_{\pm n}
.

We recall the equivalence
[
ST11
, §6]
between the Clifford twist and the restriction of the
{\rm Fer}_{n}
to
\mathscr{C}
, see (
534
) below.

Corollary 7.18
.

There is an internal natural isomorphism between the supersymmetric extension
s{\rm Fer}_{n}
and the Clifford twist,

(342)

\mathscr{C}^{2|1}
{\sf{TA}}
s\mathcal{F}\Downarrow
{\rm Cl}_{n}
s{\rm Fer}_{n}

and
s\mathcal{F}
restricts to the equivalence (
534
) along
\mathcal{S}\colon\mathscr{C}\hookrightarrow\mathscr{C}^{2|1}
.
Similarly, the values on the bordisms
{\rm L}_{\ell,\tau,\bar{\tau},\eta}^{\pm}
and
{\rm R}_{\ell,\tau,\bar{\tau},\eta}^{\pm}
in Corollary
7.16
are isomorphic (in
{\rm Mor}({\sf{TA}})
) with the trivial bundles of bimodules,

(343)

\displaystyle s{\rm Fer}_{n}({\rm L}_{\ell,\tau,\bar{\tau},\eta}^{\pm})\simeq({\mathbb{C}}{\rm l}_{\pm n})_{{\mathbb{C}}{\rm l}_{\pm n}\otimes{\mathbb{C}}{\rm l}_{\mp n}}\qquad s{\rm Fer}_{n}({\rm R}_{\ell,\tau,\bar{\tau},\eta}^{\pm})\simeq{}_{{\mathbb{C}}{\rm l}_{\mp n}\otimes{\mathbb{C}}{\rm l}_{\pm n}}({\mathbb{C}}{\rm l}_{\pm n}).

Under the above isomorphisms, the reflection structure on
s{\rm Fer}_{n}
is compatible with the
*
-superalgebra structure (
535
) on
{\mathbb{C}}{\rm l}_{\pm n}
.

Proof.

It again suffices to consider the case
n=-1
. The
{\mathbb{R}}_{>0}
-family of bimodules (
536
) implementing the equivalence (
534
) have a holomorphic
{\mathbb{H}}_{\pm}
-action (
538
); by Lemma
7.14
there is a unique extension to an action by
{\mathbb{H}}^{2|1}_{\pm}
. The
{\mathbb{Z}}/4
-equivariant structure on this bimodule also uniquely extends to a
{\mathbb{Z}}/4
-equivariant structure over
{\rm Ob}(\mathscr{C}^{2|1})
. This constructs the equivalence (
342
) with the claimed properties. The same bimodule with
{\mathbb{H}}_{\pm}^{2|1}
-action also constructs the isomorphisms (
343
). The reflection structure in all cases comes from the
*
-superalgebra structure on Clifford algebras, and so is compatible with the bimodule (
536
) implementing the isomorphisms.
∎

Proposition 7.19
.

The value of
{\rm Fer}_{n}
over Euclidean tori admits an extension

(344)

{\sf Lat}/\!\!/({\rm Spin}(2)\times{\rm SL}_{2}({\mathbb{Z}}))
{\rm Mor}({\sf{TA}})
s{\sf Lat}/\!\!/({\rm Spin}(2)\times{\rm SL}_{2}({\mathbb{Z}}))
{\rm Fer}^{\otimes n}
\mathcal{S}
\Downarrow

that is unique up to isomorphism. Equivalently, by (
527
), the Pfaffian line bundle for the periodic-periodic spin structure has a unique extension to
2|1
-Euclidean tori. Furthermore, a section over
s{\sf Lat}/\!\!/({\rm Spin}(2)\times{\rm SL}_{2}({\mathbb{Z}}))
descends to a section over
s{\sf Lat}/\!\!/({\rm Spin}(2)\rtimes{\mathbb{E}}^{2|1}\times{\rm SL}_{2}({\mathbb{Z}}))
if and only if its restriction to
{\sf Lat}/\!\!/({\rm Spin}(2)\times{\rm SL}_{2}({\mathbb{Z}}))
is a holomorphic section of the Pfaffian.

Proof.

Using the description of
s{\sf Lat}
from Lemma
4.26
, pulling back along the inclusion
{\sf Lat}\subset s{\sf Lat}
induces an equivalence of categories between
line bundles on
{\sf Lat}
and line bundles on
s{\sf Lat}
. Hence, there is a unique isomorphism class of line bundle given by the trivial line. It therefore remains to consider extensions of equivariant structures for the trivial line on
{\sf Lat}
. The presheaf
{\rm GL}_{1}({\mathbb{C}})
of automorphisms of the trivial line bundle is characterized by

{\rm GL}_{1}({\mathbb{C}})(S)=C^{\infty}(S)^{{\rm ev},\times}.

We have the isomorphism

C^{\infty}(s{\sf Lat}\times{\rm Spin}(2)\times{\rm SL}_{2}({\mathbb{Z}}))^{{\rm ev},\times}\simeq C^{\infty}({\sf Lat}\times{\rm Spin}(2)\times{\rm SL}_{2}({\mathbb{Z}}))^{{\rm ev},\times},

i.e., automorphism of line bundles over
s{\sf Lat}
are determined by their restriction along
{\sf Lat}\hookrightarrow s{\sf Lat}
. Hence, any
{\rm Spin}(2)\times{\rm SL}_{2}({\mathbb{Z}})
-equivariant structure for a line bundle on
s{\sf Lat}
is determined by its restriction to
{\sf Lat}
. The remaining claim about descent of sections to the unpointed stacks is implied by Proposition
1.18
with
M={\rm pt}
.
∎

Remark 7.20
.

Assuming a generators and relations presentation of
2|1\hbox{-}{\sf EBord}
as described in
[
ST11
, Proof of Theorem 1.15, second part]
, one can verify Hypothesis
7.10
in the framework of
[
ST11
]
. Propositions
7.15
and
7.19
establish the extension in the Ramond sector. By Remark
5.7
, the Neveu–Schwarz sector supercylinders assemble into a moduli space that is isomorphic to the (non-supersymmetric) moduli of Euclidean cylinders. Hence, the supersymmetric extension on this sector is trivially unique.

(355)

\displaystyle\langle-,-\rangle\colon\overline{{\mathcal{E}}}_{+}\otimes_{{\mathbb{C}}{\rm l}_{n}}{\mathcal{E}}_{+}\to\underline{{\mathbb{C}}},\qquad\langle\omega^{*}\cdot x,y\rangle=(-1)^{|\omega||x|}\langle x,\omega\cdot y\rangle,\ \omega\in{\mathbb{C}}{\rm l}_{n}.

Lemma 7.24
.

The pairing (
355
) is hermitian in the
{\mathbb{Z}}/2
-graded sense

(356)

\displaystyle\langle x,y\rangle=(-1)^{|x||y|}\overline{\langle y,x\rangle}.

Proof.

The argument uses similar ideas to the first part of
[
HST10
, Corollary 6.25]
, though the signs here are different as we are working in the oriented setting (versus the unoriented framework of
[
HST10
]
).
Consider the diagram

(357)

\overline{{\mathbb{C}}{\rm l}}_{\pm n}\otimes_{\overline{{\mathbb{C}}{\rm l}}_{\pm n}\otimes{\mathbb{C}}{\rm l}_{\pm n}}(\dagger^{*}\overline{{\sf pr}}_{\pm}^{*}\overline{E({\sf S}^{\pm}_{M})}\otimes{\sf act}_{\mp}^{*}E({\sf S}^{\pm}_{M}))
{\mathbb{C}}{\rm l}_{\mp n}\otimes_{{\mathbb{C}}{\rm l}_{\mp n}\otimes{\mathbb{C}}{\rm l}_{\pm n}}({\sf pr}_{\mp}^{*}E({\sf S}^{\mp}_{M})\otimes{\sf act}_{\mp}^{*}E({\sf S}^{\pm}_{M}))
\underline{{\mathbb{C}}}
\dagger^{*}\Big(\overline{{\mathbb{C}}{\rm l}}_{\pm n}\otimes_{\overline{{\mathbb{C}}{\rm l}}_{\pm n}\otimes\overline{{\mathbb{C}}{\rm l}}_{\mp n}}(\overline{{\sf pr}}_{\pm}^{*}\overline{E({\sf S}^{\pm}_{M})}\otimes\overline{{\sf act}}_{\pm}^{*}\overline{E({\sf S}^{\mp}_{M})})\big)
\dagger^{*}\overline{\underline{{\mathbb{C}}}}
\dagger^{*}\big(\overline{{\mathbb{C}}{\rm l}}_{\mp n}\otimes_{\overline{{\mathbb{C}}{\rm l}}_{\mp n}\otimes\overline{{\mathbb{C}}{\rm l}}_{\pm n}}(\overline{{\sf act}}_{\pm}^{*}\overline{E({\sf S}^{\mp}_{M})}\otimes\overline{{\sf pr}}_{\pm}^{*}\overline{E({\sf S}^{\pm}_{M})})
E({\sf L}^{\mp}_{M})
\overline{E({\sf L}^{\pm}_{M})}
\simeq
\sigma
\overline{{\sf inv}}^{*}\overline{E({\sf L}^{\mp}_{M})}
restricts to
\langle-,-\rangle

where the
*
-superalgebra structure on
{\mathbb{C}}{\rm l}_{\pm n}
is used implicitly throughout. The indicated arrow in the upper right restricts to the pairing
\langle-,-\rangle
by definition.
Commutativity of the lower triangle follows from (
329
). Commutativity of the inner square is the condition (
349
) on reflection positivity data.
The composition of arrows starting from the top left and using the lower triangle is the right hand side of (
356
), and hence the restriction of this diagram along (
353
) implies the claimed formula, where the sign in (
356
) comes from the symmetry isomorphism
\sigma
for the (graded) tensor product in the lower triangle.
∎

See Definition
C.31
for the notion of a reflection structure on a twisted field theory, and below we take the reflection structure (
520
) on
2|1\hbox{-}{\sf EBord}(M)
and the reflection structure for the degree
n
twist determined by (
533
). We refer to (
480
) for the signs involved in the positivity property for a
{\mathbb{Z}}/2
-graded hermitian pairing.

Definition 7.25
.

Assuming Hypothesis
7.10
, a

with reflection structure is

if the pairing (

) is a (graded) positive-definite hermitian pairing on the super vector bundle

over

.

Remark 7.26
.

For a reflection positive field theory, (

) implies that the

-action on

is automatically a

-representation relative to the hermitian inner product.

Definition 7.27
.

Assuming Hypothesis
7.10
, for each

define the groupoid

as having objects degree
n
, reflection positive
2|1
-Euclidean field theories, and morphisms isomorphisms between degree

field theories compatible with the reflection structure.

8.
Extracting geometric data from a degree
n
field theory

The main goal of this section is to prove Proposition
1.13
, extracting a superconnection with additional structure and property from an object of
2|1\hbox{-{\sf EFT}}^{n}(M)
. The mechanism that produces this geometric data is the restriction of a field theory to Euclidean supercylinders,

(358)

\mathscr{C}^{2|1}(M)
2|1\hbox{-}{\sf EBord}(M)
{\sf{TA}}.
\Downarrow
\mathbbold{1}
{\rm Fer}_{n}

With the goal of making the geometric constructions in this section as flexible as possible, the main constructions are independent from the definitions in §
6
and §
7
. This is achieved by phrasing all definitions in terms of a twisted representation of
\mathscr{C}^{2|1}(M)
as in Definition
8.1
below, omitting reference to the intermediary degree
n
field theory in (
358
). At the end of each subsection we provide statements linking the representation theory of
\mathscr{C}^{2|1}(M)
with degree
n
field theories under (
358
).

Definition 8.1
.

A
degree
n
representation of
\mathscr{C}^{2|1}(M)
is an internal natural transformation

(359)

\mathscr{C}^{2|1}(M)
{\sf{TA}}
\Downarrow
\mathbbold{1}
{\rm Cl}_{n}

where
{\rm Cl}_{n}\colon\mathscr{C}^{2|1}(M)\to{\sf{TA}}
is the pull back the Clifford twist (Definition
7.17
) along the functor
\mathscr{C}^{2|1}(M)\to\mathscr{C}^{2|1}({\rm pt})=\mathscr{C}^{2|1}
induced by the canonical map
M\to{\rm pt}
.
Degree
n
representations form a groupoid whose objects are internal natural transformations (
359
) and whose morphisms are isomorphisms between internal natural transformations.

8.1.
Clifford linear superconnections from degree
n
representations of
\widetilde{\mathscr{C}}^{2|1}(M)

Consider the restriction of a degree
n
representation (
359
) along the
{\mathbb{Z}}/4
-cover (
229
); we will also refer to the resulting twisted representation of
\widetilde{\mathscr{C}}^{2|1}(M)
as a degree
n
representation. We caution that the notation (
354
) in the previous section differs slightly from
{\mathcal{E}}_{\pm}
used in the following proposition and throughout this section.

Proposition 8.2
.

A degree
n
representation of
\widetilde{\mathscr{C}}^{2|1}(M)
determines vector bundles
{\mathcal{E}}_{+}\to M
and
{\mathcal{E}}_{-}\to M
with fiberwise
S^{1}\times{\mathbb{C}}{\rm l}_{n}
- and
S^{1}\times{\mathbb{C}}{\rm l}_{-n}
-actions, respectively. Furthermore, the bundles
{\mathcal{E}}_{\pm}
are endowed with the structure of a smooth 1-parameter family of superconnections
{\mathbb{A}}_{\ell}^{\pm}
for
\ell\in{\mathbb{R}}_{>0}
that are
{\mathbb{C}}{\rm l}_{\pm n}
-linear and
S^{1}
-invariant.

Proof.

From Definition
C.4
, part of the data of an internal natural transformation
E
in (
359
) is a map of stacks

{\rm Ob}(\widetilde{\mathscr{C}}^{2|1}(M))\to{\rm Mor}({\sf{TA}})

with specified source and target data. This is equivalent to bundles of left Clifford modules

(360)

\displaystyle{\mathbb{C}}{\rm l}_{+n}\ \rotatebox[origin]{90.0}{$\curvearrowright$}\ \widetilde{\mathcal{E}}_{+}\to{\sf Map}({\mathbb{R}}^{0|1},M)\times{\mathbb{R}}_{>0},\quad{\mathbb{C}}{\rm l}_{-n}\ \rotatebox[origin]{90.0}{$\curvearrowright$}\ \widetilde{\mathcal{E}}_{-}\to{\sf Map}({\mathbb{R}}^{0|1},M)\times{\mathbb{R}}_{>0}

over each component of
{\rm Ob}(\widetilde{\mathscr{C}}^{2|1}(M))
. The next piece of data in an internal natural transformation is an isomorphism between maps of stacks
{\rm Mor}(\widetilde{\mathscr{C}}^{2|1}(M))\to{\rm Mor}({\sf{TA}})
(see (
503
)), which is given by bundle maps

(361)

\displaystyle{\mathbb{C}}{\rm l}_{\pm n}\otimes_{{\mathbb{C}}{\rm l}_{\pm n}}{\sf pr}_{\pm}^{*}\Gamma(\widetilde{\mathcal{E}}_{\pm})\to{\sf act}_{\pm}\Gamma(\widetilde{\mathcal{E}}_{\pm})

\displaystyle\quad{\rm over}\quad{\sf Map}({\mathbb{R}}^{0|1},M)\times({\mathbb{R}}_{>0}\times{\mathbb{H}}^{2|1}_{\pm})/{\mathbb{Z}}.

The maps (
361
) are then further required to satisfy a condition with respect to composition in
\widetilde{\mathscr{C}}^{2|1}(M)
. To unpack this in terms of the differential geometry of
M
, we first observe the isomorphism

(362)

\displaystyle\Gamma(\widetilde{\mathcal{E}}_{\pm})\simeq\Omega^{\bullet}(M;C^{\infty}({\mathbb{R}}_{>0}))\otimes_{C^{\infty}(M)}\Gamma(M,{\mathcal{E}}_{\pm}),\qquad{\mathcal{E}}_{\pm}=\widetilde{\mathcal{E}}_{\pm}|_{M\times\{1\}}

of modules over
C^{\infty}({\sf Map}({\mathbb{R}}^{0|1},M)\times{\mathbb{R}}_{>0})\simeq\Omega^{\bullet}(M;C^{\infty}({\mathbb{R}}_{>0}))
. This uses the fact that (up to isomorphism) a vector bundle over
{\sf Map}({\mathbb{R}}^{0|1},M)
pulls back from
M
(see Lemma
B.13
) and a vector bundle over
M\times{\mathbb{R}}_{>0}
is isomorphic to one that pulls back along the projection
M\times{\mathbb{R}}_{>0}\to M
.
Pulling back
{\sf pr}_{\pm}^{*}\widetilde{{\mathcal{E}}}_{\pm}
and
{\sf act}_{\pm}^{*}\widetilde{{\mathcal{E}}}_{\pm}
along
p
gives the isomorphisms

(363)

\displaystyle\Gamma({\sf pr}_{\pm}^{*}\widetilde{{\mathcal{E}}}_{\pm})

\displaystyle\simeq

\displaystyle\Omega^{\bullet}(M;C^{\infty}(({\mathbb{R}}_{>0}\times{\mathbb{H}}^{2|1}_{\pm})/{\mathbb{Z}}))\otimes_{\Omega^{\bullet}(M;C^{\infty}({\mathbb{R}}_{>0}))}\Gamma(\widetilde{{\mathcal{E}}}_{\pm})

\displaystyle\simeq

\displaystyle C^{\infty}(({\mathbb{R}}_{>0}\times{\mathbb{H}}^{2|1}_{\pm})/{\mathbb{Z}}))\otimes\Omega^{\bullet}(M)\otimes_{C^{\infty}(M)}\Gamma({\mathcal{E}}_{\pm})

\displaystyle\simeq

\displaystyle C^{\infty}(({\mathbb{R}}_{>0}\times{\mathbb{H}}^{2|1}_{\pm})/{\mathbb{Z}}))\otimes\Omega^{\bullet}(M;{\mathcal{E}}_{\pm})

(364)

\displaystyle\Gamma({\sf act}_{\pm}^{*}\widetilde{{\mathcal{E}}}_{\pm})

\displaystyle\simeq

\displaystyle\Omega^{\bullet}(M;C^{\infty}(({\mathbb{R}}_{>0}\times{\mathbb{H}}^{2|1}_{\pm})/{\mathbb{Z}}))\otimes_{\Omega^{\bullet}(M;C^{\infty}({\mathbb{R}}_{>0}))}\Gamma(\widetilde{{\mathcal{E}}}_{\pm})

\displaystyle\simeq

\displaystyle C^{\infty}(({\mathbb{R}}_{>0}\times{\mathbb{H}}^{2|1}_{\pm})/{\mathbb{Z}}))\otimes\Omega^{\bullet}(M)\otimes_{C^{\infty}(M)}\Gamma({\mathcal{E}}_{\pm})

\displaystyle\simeq

\displaystyle C^{\infty}(({\mathbb{R}}_{>0}\times{\mathbb{H}}^{2|1}_{\pm})/{\mathbb{Z}}))\otimes\Omega^{\bullet}(M;{\mathcal{E}}_{\pm})

using the projective tensor product throughout and the isomorphism (
362
). The map of vector bundles (
361
) is a map between the
\Omega^{\bullet}(M;C^{\infty}(({\mathbb{R}}_{>0}\times{\mathbb{H}}^{2|1}_{\pm})/{\mathbb{Z}}))
-modules (
363
) and (
364
) for the module structure determined by the algebra maps
{\sf pr}_{\pm}^{*}
and
{\sf act}_{\pm}^{*}
determined by (
266
). This map is
C^{\infty}(({\mathbb{H}}^{2|1}_{\pm}\times{\mathbb{R}}_{>0})/{\mathbb{Z}}))
-linear map, affording the description of (
361
) in terms of a function valued in Clifford-linear endomorphisms,

(365)

\displaystyle\rho_{\pm}\in C^{\infty}(({\mathbb{R}}_{>0}\times{\mathbb{H}}^{2|1}_{\pm})/{\mathbb{Z}};{\rm End}_{{\mathbb{C}}{\rm l}_{\pm n}}(\Omega^{\bullet}(M;{\mathcal{E}}_{\pm}))).

Using the descriptions of
{\sf pr}_{\pm}^{*}
and
{\sf act}_{\pm}^{*}
from Lemma
5.19
,
\rho_{\pm}
must satisfy the property

\rho_{\pm}(\ell,\tau,\bar{\tau},\eta)(\alpha v)=(\alpha-\eta d\alpha)\rho_{\pm}(\ell,\tau,\bar{\tau},\eta)(v),\quad\begin{array}[]{l}\alpha\in\Omega^{\bullet}(M;C^{\infty}({\mathbb{R}}_{>0}\times{\mathbb{H}}_{\pm})/{\mathbb{Z}}),\\
v\in C^{\infty}(({\mathbb{R}}_{>0}\times{\mathbb{H}}^{2|1}_{\pm})/{\mathbb{Z}}))\otimes\Omega^{\bullet}(M;{\mathcal{E}}_{\pm}).\end{array}

Furthermore, Lemma
5.15
shows that
\rho_{+}
and
\rho_{-}
determine an
{\mathbb{R}}_{>0}
-family of super semigroup representations,

\rho_{\pm}(\ell,\tau,\bar{\tau},\eta)\circ\rho_{\pm}(\ell,\tau^{\prime},\bar{\tau}^{\prime},\eta^{\prime})=\rho_{\pm}(\ell,\tau+\tau^{\prime},\bar{\tau}+\bar{\tau}^{\prime}+\eta\eta^{\prime},\eta+\eta^{\prime})

since composition in
\widetilde{\mathscr{C}}^{2|1}(M)
corresponds to multiplication in
{\mathbb{H}}^{2|1}_{\pm}
.

To further analyze the geometry of (
365
), Taylor expand
\rho_{\pm}
in the odd variable
-\eta\in C^{\infty}(({\mathbb{R}}_{>0}\times{\mathbb{H}}^{2|1}_{\pm})/{\mathbb{Z}})
to obtain even and odd endomorphisms
{\sf a}_{\pm}(\ell,\tau,\bar{\tau})
and
{\sf b}_{\pm}(\ell,\tau,\bar{\tau})

\displaystyle\rho_{\pm}(\ell,\tau,\bar{\tau},\eta)={\sf a}_{\pm}(\ell,\tau,\bar{\tau})-\eta{\sf b}_{\pm}(\ell,\tau,\bar{\tau}),\qquad\begin{array}[]{l}{\sf a}_{\pm}(\ell,\tau,\bar{\tau})\in{\rm End}_{{\mathbb{C}}{\rm l}_{\pm n}}(\Omega^{\bullet}(M;{\mathcal{E}}_{\pm}))^{\rm ev},\\
{\sf b}_{\pm}(\ell,\tau,\bar{\tau})\in{\rm End}_{{\mathbb{C}}{\rm l}_{\pm n}}(\Omega^{\bullet}(M;{\mathcal{E}}_{\pm}))^{\rm odd}.\end{array}

Restricting to
{\mathbb{R}}/\ell{\mathbb{Z}}\subset{\mathbb{H}}_{\pm}/\ell{\mathbb{Z}}\subset({\mathbb{R}}_{>0}\times{\mathbb{H}}_{\pm}^{2|1})/{\mathbb{Z}}
, for each value of
\ell
we obtain an action,
{\mathbb{R}}/\ell{\mathbb{Z}}\to{\rm End}_{{\mathbb{C}}{\rm l}_{\pm n}}(\Omega^{\bullet}(M;{\mathcal{E}}_{\pm}))
. By (
8.1
), the
{\mathbb{R}}/\ell{\mathbb{Z}}
-action is linear over
\Omega^{\bullet}(M;C^{\infty}({\mathbb{R}}_{>0}))
. Hence, the
{\mathbb{R}}/\ell{\mathbb{Z}}
-action is through Clifford-linear bundle endomorphisms

(372)

\displaystyle{\mathbb{R}}/\ell{\mathbb{Z}}\to\Gamma({\rm End}_{{\mathbb{C}}{\rm l}_{\pm n}}({\mathcal{E}}_{\pm})),

\displaystyle t\mapsto{\sf a}_{\pm}(\ell,t,t),\ \ {\sf a}_{\pm}(\ell,s,s)\circ{\sf a}_{\pm}(\ell,t,t)={\sf a}_{\pm}(\ell,s+t,s+t).

These actions necessarily vary smoothly with
\ell\in{\mathbb{R}}_{>0}
. So reparameterizing by
t\mapsto\ell t
at each fiber
\ell\in{\mathbb{R}}_{>0}
, and choosing the isomorphism (
362
) to be
S^{1}
-equivariant, one obtains a single
S^{1}={\mathbb{R}}/{\mathbb{Z}}
-action that commutes with the Clifford action.

Next consider the endomorphism of
\Omega^{\bullet}(M;{\mathcal{E}}_{\pm})

(373)

\displaystyle{\mathbb{A}}_{\ell}^{\pm}:={\sf b}_{\pm}(\ell,0,0)\in{\rm End}_{{\mathbb{C}}{\rm l}_{\pm n}}(\Omega^{\bullet}(M;{\mathcal{E}}_{\pm}))^{\rm odd}.

Since
{\sf b}_{\pm}(\ell,\tau,\bar{\tau})
is odd, so is
{\mathbb{A}}_{\ell}^{\pm}
. Furthermore, because
{\sf b}_{\pm}(\ell,\tau,\bar{\tau})
is
{\mathbb{C}}{\rm l}_{\pm n}
-linear, so is
{\mathbb{A}}_{\ell}^{\pm}
. Equation (
8.1
) shows that
{\mathbb{A}}_{\ell}^{\pm}
is an endomorphism of
\Omega^{\bullet}(M;{\mathcal{E}}_{\pm})
for any
\ell\in{\mathbb{R}}_{>0}
, and in this way we view
{\mathbb{A}}_{\ell}^{\pm}
as an
{\mathbb{R}}_{>0}
-family of operators. Equation (
8.1
) also shows that
{\mathbb{A}}_{\ell}^{\pm}
commutes with the
{\mathbb{R}}/\ell{\mathbb{Z}}
-action (
372
), and hence commutes with the
S^{1}
-action on
{\mathcal{E}}_{\pm}
. Finally, (
8.1
) applied to (
8.1
) yields the pair of equations

{\sf a}_{\pm}(\ell,\tau,\bar{\tau})(\alpha v)=\alpha{\sf a}_{\pm}(\ell,\tau,\bar{\tau})(v),\qquad{\sf b}_{\pm}(\ell,\tau,\bar{\tau})(\alpha v)=d\alpha{\sf a}_{\pm}(\ell,\tau,\bar{\tau})(v)+(-1)^{|\alpha|}\alpha{\sf b}_{\pm}(\ell,\tau,\bar{\tau})(v).

Using that
{\sf a}_{\pm}(\ell,0,0)={{\rm id}}
, the value of the second equation at
(\tau,\bar{\tau})=(0,0)
is the Leibniz rule for
{\mathbb{A}}_{\ell}^{\pm}
, and so we conclude that
{\mathbb{A}}_{\ell}^{+}
and
{\mathbb{A}}_{\ell}^{-}
are superconnections. This completes the proof of the proposition.
∎

Lemma 8.3
.

Assuming Hypothesis
7.10
, the restriction of the degree
n
twist along (
358
) is equivalent to the Clifford twist
{\rm Cl}_{n}
. Under this equivalence we have the identification

(374)

\displaystyle\widetilde{{\mathcal{E}}}_{\pm}\simeq E({\sf s}_{M}^{\pm})

where
\widetilde{{\mathcal{E}}}_{\pm}
are the vector bundles (
360
), and
{\sf s}_{M}^{\pm}
is the family of small bordisms defined in (
6.26
).

Proof.

This is immediate from Definition
7.17
, Corollary
7.18
, and Corollary
7.21
.
∎

Remark 8.4
.

In light of Corollary
7.18
, an equivalent groupoid to the one in Definition
8.1
involves modules over the tensor powers of the free fermion algebras (
523
). The invertible bimodule (
536
) furnishes the equivalence between these two points of view. So without passing through the equivalence with the Clifford twist, the value (
374
) becomes

(375)

\displaystyle E({\sf s}_{M}^{\pm})\simeq({}_{{\rm Fer}_{\ell}^{\pm}}({\mathbb{C}}{\rm l}_{\pm 1}\bigotimes_{m>0}\Lambda^{\bullet}{\mathbb{C}}_{\ell,\mp m}))^{\otimes-n}\otimes_{{\mathbb{C}}{\rm l}_{\pm n}}\widetilde{{\mathcal{E}}}_{\pm}.

With the eventual goal of extracting
{\rm KO}(\!(q)\!)
-classes from
\widetilde{{\mathcal{E}}}_{\pm}
, it is more convenient in this paper to work with Clifford modules as in (
374
).

8.2.
Flip preserving representations

Next we consider descent data for a degree
n
representation of
\widetilde{\mathscr{C}}^{2|1}(M)
along the
{\mathbb{Z}}/4
-cover (
229
). For degree
n
representations that restrict from field theories, this descent data must satisfy a condition related to the spin statistics theorem described in
[
HST10
, Definition 6.44]
and
[
HST10
, Remark 6.45]
.

We start with a general observation. The category of supermanifolds has a
{\mathbb{Z}}/2
-action that acts by the parity involution on ring of functions of a supermanifold; maps between supermanifolds are automatically equivariant for this
{\mathbb{Z}}/2
-action. By naturality, the objects and morphsims of any super Lie category also inherit canonical
{\mathbb{Z}}/2
-actions for which the source, target, unit and composition maps are
{\mathbb{Z}}/2
-equivariant.
In the case of the super Lie category
\widetilde{\mathscr{C}}^{2|1}(M)
, these canonical
{\mathbb{Z}}/2
-actions have the geometric interpretation as the spin flip on super Euclidean manifolds (see Lemmas
4.9
and
5.22
). In particular, the
{\mathbb{Z}}/4
-cover (
229
) factors as the composition

\displaystyle\widetilde{\mathscr{C}}^{2|1}(M)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\left(\begin{array}[]{c}({\mathbb{R}}_{>0}\times{\mathbb{H}}_{+}\coprod{\mathbb{H}}_{-})/{\mathbb{Z}}\times{\sf Map}({\mathbb{R}}^{0|1},M)\\
{\sf s}\downarrow\downarrow{\sf t}\\
({\mathbb{R}}_{>0}\coprod{\mathbb{R}}_{>0})\times{\sf Map}({\mathbb{R}}^{0|1},M)\end{array}\right)

\displaystyle\to

\displaystyle\left(\begin{array}[]{c}({\mathbb{R}}_{>0}\times{\mathbb{H}}_{+}\coprod{\mathbb{H}}_{-})/{\mathbb{Z}}\times{\sf Map}({\mathbb{R}}^{0|1},M)/\!\!/{\mathbb{Z}}/2\\
{\sf s}\downarrow\downarrow{\sf t}\\
({\mathbb{R}}_{>0}\coprod{\mathbb{R}}_{>0})\times{\sf Map}({\mathbb{R}}^{0|1},M)/\!\!/{\mathbb{Z}}/2\end{array}\right)

\displaystyle\to

\displaystyle\left(\begin{array}[]{c}({\mathbb{R}}_{>0}\times{\mathbb{H}}_{+}\coprod{\mathbb{H}}_{-})/{\mathbb{Z}}\times{\sf Map}({\mathbb{R}}^{0|1},M)/\!\!/{\mathbb{Z}}/4\\
{\sf s}\downarrow\downarrow{\sf t}\\
({\mathbb{R}}_{>0}\coprod{\mathbb{R}}_{>0})\times{\sf Map}({\mathbb{R}}^{0|1},M)/\!\!/{\mathbb{Z}}/4\end{array}\right)=\mathscr{C}^{2|1}(M)

where in the middle line the
{\mathbb{Z}}/2
-action is the canonical one that we identify with the spin flip
{\rm Fl}
; in the last line the generator of
{\mathbb{Z}}/4
acts by the square root
\sqrt{{\rm Fl}}
.

Definition 8.5
.

A degree
n
representation of
\mathscr{C}^{2|1}(M)
is
flip preserving
if on restriction to (
8.2
) the generator of
{\mathbb{Z}}/2
acts by the parity involution on the Clifford algebras
{\mathbb{C}}{\rm l}_{\pm n}
and by the parity involution on Clifford modules
\widetilde{{\mathcal{E}}}_{\pm}
in the notation from Proposition
8.2
.

Lemma 8.6
.

A flip-preserving degree
n
representation of
\mathscr{C}^{2|1}(M)
is the data of isomorphisms

(388)

\displaystyle\alpha_{\pm}\colon{\mathbb{C}}{\rm l}_{\pm n}\to{\mathbb{C}}{\rm l}_{\mp n},\qquad{\sf f}_{\pm}\colon\widetilde{{\mathcal{E}}}_{\pm}\xrightarrow{\sim}\sqrt{{\rm Fl}}^{*}\widetilde{{\mathcal{E}}}_{\mp}.

such that
\alpha_{\pm}\circ\alpha_{\mp}
and
{\sf f}_{\pm}\circ{\sf f}_{\mp}
are parity involutions. Furthermore, the isomorphisms
{\sf f}_{\pm}
are
S^{1}
-equivariant relative to the inversion homomorphism
t\mapsto-t
on
S^{1}
, and the superconnections in Proposition
8.2
satisfy

(389)

\displaystyle{\mathbb{A}}_{\ell}^{\pm}=(-i)^{{\rm deg}+1}{\mathbb{A}}_{\ell}^{\mp}

relative to the isomorphisms
{\sf f}_{\pm}
.

Proof.

Equivariance data for the generator
\sqrt{{\rm Fl}}
of the
{\mathbb{Z}}/4
-action is equivalent to the isomorphisms (
388
). The flip preserving condition requires
\alpha_{\pm}
and
{\sf f}_{\pm}
to square to the respective parity involutions. From the definition of an internal natural transformation, we have the condition on the super semigroup representations (
365
)

\rho_{\pm}(\ell,\tau,\bar{\tau},\eta)=\sqrt{{\rm Fl}}^{*}\rho_{\mp}(-\tau,-\bar{\tau},\sqrt{-1}\eta)\in C^{\infty}({\mathbb{H}}^{2|1}_{\pm},{\rm End}_{{\mathbb{C}}{\rm l}_{\pm n}}(\Gamma(\widetilde{{\mathcal{E}}}_{\pm}))).

Using the notation from (
8.1
) and the description of
\sqrt{{\rm Fl}}^{*}
from (
237
) we obtain

{\sf a}_{\pm}(\ell,\tau,\bar{\tau})-\eta{\sf b}_{\pm}(\ell,\tau,\bar{\tau})=(-i)^{{\rm deg}}({\sf a}_{\mp}(-\tau,-\bar{\tau})-i\eta{\sf b}_{\mp}(-\tau,-\bar{\tau}))

where the equality implicitly uses the isomorphism (
388
). From this we deduce that the isomorphism (
388
) is
{\mathbb{R}}
-equivariant for the actions (
372
) relative to the automorphism
t\mapsto-t
of
{\mathbb{R}}
, and that the superconnections obey (
389
).
∎

The upshot of Lemma
8.6
is that for a flip-preserving degree
n
representation of
\mathscr{C}^{2|1}(M)
, the vector bundles
{\mathcal{E}}_{+}
and
{\mathcal{E}}_{-}
in Proposition
8.2
are determined by a single
S^{1}
-equivariant bundle with
{\mathbb{R}}_{>0}
-family of
S^{1}\times{\mathbb{C}}{\rm l}_{n}
-linear superconnections

(390)

\displaystyle{\mathcal{E}}:={\mathcal{E}}_{+}\to M,\qquad{\mathbb{A}}_{\ell}:={\mathbb{A}}_{\ell}^{+}.

There are two possible choices of
\alpha_{\pm}
in (
388
) that differ by the parity automorphism; without loss of generality we assume
\alpha_{\pm}
is the standard anti-automorphism (
341
).

Lemma 8.7
.

Assuming Hypothesis
7.10
, a degree
n
field theory (
358
) determines a flip preserving degree
n
representation of
\mathscr{C}^{2|1}(M)
.

Proof.

This follows from Lemma
6.22
and the fact that twisted field theories are defined as flip preserving functors, see
[
ST11
, §2.6]
and §
C.4
below.
∎

8.3.
Degree
n
representations with a pairing

The restriction of the map (
294
) along the inclusion
{\sf Map}({\mathbb{R}}^{0|1},M)\subset{\sf Map}({\mathbb{R}}^{2|1}/{\mathbb{Z}},M)
gives the map

(391)

\displaystyle{\sf inv}\colon{\sf Map}({\mathbb{R}}^{0|1},M)\times({\mathbb{R}}_{>0}\times{\mathbb{H}}^{2|1}_{\pm})/{\mathbb{Z}}\to{\sf Map}({\mathbb{R}}^{0|1},M)\times({\mathbb{R}}_{>0}\times{\mathbb{H}}^{2|1}_{\mp})/{\mathbb{Z}},

that we will also denote
{\sf inv}
.

Lemma 8.8
.

The pullback of (
361
) along
{\sf inv}
gives a map of vector bundles

{\sf act}_{\mp}^{*}\widetilde{{\mathcal{E}}}_{\pm}\to{\sf pr}_{\mp}^{*}\widetilde{{\mathcal{E}}}_{\pm}

over
{\sf Map}({\mathbb{R}}^{0|1},M)\times({\mathbb{R}}_{>0}\times{\mathbb{H}}^{2|1}_{+})/{\mathbb{Z}}
commuting with the
{\mathbb{C}}{\rm l}_{\pm n}
-action.

Proof.

This follows from Lemma
6.20
.
∎

Definition 8.9
.

A
pairing
on a degree
n
representation of
\mathscr{C}^{2|1}(M)
is a vector bundle map

(392)

\displaystyle{\beta}\colon{\mathbb{C}}{\rm l}_{n}\otimes_{{\mathbb{C}}{\rm l}_{-n}\otimes{\mathbb{C}}{\rm l}_{n}}(\widetilde{{\mathcal{E}}}_{-}\otimes\widetilde{{\mathcal{E}}}_{+})\to\underline{{\mathbb{C}}}

over
{\sf Map}({\mathbb{R}}^{0|1},M)\times{\mathbb{R}}_{>0}
with the property that the diagram of vector bundles over
{\sf Map}({\mathbb{R}}^{0|1},M)\times({\mathbb{R}}_{>0}\times{\mathbb{H}}^{2|1}_{\pm})/{\mathbb{Z}}
commutes

(393)

{\mathbb{C}}{\rm l}_{n}\otimes_{{\mathbb{C}}{\rm l}_{-n}\otimes{\mathbb{C}}{\rm l}_{n}}({\sf act}_{+}^{*}\widetilde{{\mathcal{E}}}_{-}\otimes{\sf pr}_{+}^{*}\widetilde{{\mathcal{E}}}_{+})
{\sf pr}_{+}^{*}({\mathbb{C}}{\rm l}_{n}\otimes_{{\mathbb{C}}{\rm l}_{-n}\otimes{\mathbb{C}}{\rm l}_{n}}(\widetilde{{\mathcal{E}}}_{-}\otimes\widetilde{{\mathcal{E}}}_{+}))
{\sf act}^{*}_{+}({\mathbb{C}}{\rm l}_{n}\otimes_{{\mathbb{C}}{\rm l}_{-n}\otimes{\mathbb{C}}{\rm l}_{n}}(\widetilde{{\mathcal{E}}}_{-}\otimes\widetilde{{\mathcal{E}}}_{+}))
\underline{{\mathbb{C}}}.
{{\rm id}}\otimes\rho_{+}
{\sf inv}^{*}\rho_{-}\otimes{{\rm id}}
{\sf pr}_{+}^{*}{\beta}
{\sf act}_{+}^{*}{\beta}

Proposition 8.10
.

A pairing on a degree
n
representation of
\mathscr{C}^{2|1}(M)
determines an
{\mathbb{R}}_{>0}
-family of
S^{1}\times{\mathbb{C}}{\rm l}_{\pm n}
-invariant pairings
{\beta}_{\ell}
on
{\mathcal{E}}_{\pm}
with the property that the 1-parameter family of superconnections
{\mathbb{A}}_{\ell}^{+},{\mathbb{A}}_{\ell}^{-}
from Proposition
8.2
satisfy the adjunction formula,

(394)

\displaystyle{\beta}_{\ell}({\mathbb{A}}_{\ell}^{-}v,w)+(-1)^{|v|}{\beta}_{\ell}(v,{\mathbb{A}}_{\ell}^{+}w)=d{\beta}_{\ell}(v,w).

Proof.

Using the descriptions of
{\sf pr}^{*}_{\pm}\widetilde{{\mathcal{E}}}_{\pm}
and
{\sf act}^{*}_{\pm}\widetilde{{\mathcal{E}}}_{\pm}
in terms of modules over
\Omega^{\bullet}(M;C^{\infty}(({\mathbb{R}}_{>0}\times{\mathbb{H}}_{\pm}^{2|1})/{\mathbb{Z}}))
(see Lemma
5.19
), commutativity of (
393
) implies

\displaystyle({\sf pr}_{+}^{*}{\beta}_{\ell})(({\sf inv}^{*}\rho_{-})(\ell,\tau,\bar{\tau},\eta)v,w)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle{\beta}_{\ell}(\rho_{-}(\ell,-\tau,-\bar{\tau},-\eta)v,w)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle{\beta}_{\ell}(({\sf a}_{-}(\ell,-\tau,-\bar{\tau})+\eta{\sf b}_{-}(\ell,-\tau,-\bar{\tau}))v,w)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle({\sf act}^{*}_{+}{\beta}_{\ell})(v,\rho_{+}(\ell,\tau,\bar{\tau},\eta)w)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle{\beta}_{\ell}(v,\rho_{+}(\ell,\tau,\bar{\tau},\eta)w)+\eta d{\beta}_{\ell}(v,\rho_{+}(\ell,\tau,\bar{\tau},\eta)w)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle{\beta}_{\ell}(v,({\sf a}_{+}(\ell,\tau,\bar{\tau})-\eta{\sf b}_{+}(\ell,\tau,\bar{\tau}))w)

\displaystyle+\eta d{\beta}_{\ell}(v,({\sf a}_{+}(\ell,\tau,\bar{\tau})-\eta{\sf b}_{+}(\ell,\tau,\bar{\tau}))w),

where the equalities above are between elements of
\Omega^{\bullet}(M;C^{\infty}(({\mathbb{R}}_{>0}\times{\mathbb{H}}_{\pm}^{2|1})/{\mathbb{Z}}))
. Setting
\eta=0
and
(\tau,\bar{\tau})=(t,t)
, the pairing is seen to be
{\mathbb{R}}
-invariant for
{\mathbb{R}}\subset{\mathbb{H}}_{\pm}
; this implies the claimed
S^{1}
-invariance.
Collecting the terms involving
\eta
, setting
(\tau,\bar{\tau})=(0,0)
, and using the definition (
373
) of the superconnections
{\mathbb{A}}_{\ell}^{+}
and
{\mathbb{A}}_{\ell}^{-}
, equation (
394
) follows.
∎

Lemma 8.11
.

Assuming Hypothesis
7.10
, the restriction of a degree
n
field theory along (
358
) equips the resulting degree
n
representation of
\mathscr{C}^{2|1}(M)
with a pairing.

Proof.

The datum (
392
) follows from restricting
E({\sf L}^{-}_{M})
in Corollary
7.21
along (
317
). The property (
393
) follows from (
326
) in Proposition
7.5
.
∎

8.4.
Reflection and real structures

The functor
\dagger
from Lemma
5.22
determines a reflection structure on
\mathscr{C}^{2|1}(M)
in the sense of Definition
C.19
.

Lemma 8.12
.

A reflection structure for the Clifford twist of
\mathscr{C}^{2|1}(M)
in the sense of Definition
C.25
is determined by a
*
-superalgebra structure
*\colon{\mathbb{C}}{\rm l}_{\pm n}\to\overline{{\mathbb{C}}{\rm l}}_{\pm n}^{\sf{op}}=\overline{{\mathbb{C}}{\rm l}}_{\mp n}
with the property

(395)

\displaystyle\overline{\alpha}_{\pm}\circ(-)^{*}=(-1)^{\sf F}\circ(-)^{*}\circ\alpha_{\mp},

for parity involution
(-1)^{\sf F}
and the anti-automorphism
\alpha_{\pm}
of
{\mathbb{C}}{\rm l}_{\pm n}
, see (
341
). In particular, a reflection structure for the Clifford twist determines a real structure
{\mathbb{C}}{\rm l}_{\pm n}\to\overline{{\mathbb{C}}{\rm l}}_{\pm n}
on the Clifford algebras
{\mathbb{C}}{\rm l}_{\pm n}
.

Proof.

Unpacking Definition
C.25
, a reflection structure for the twist
{\rm Cl}_{n}\colon\mathscr{C}^{2|1}(M)\to{\sf{TA}}
is a
{\mathbb{Z}}/4
-equivariant isomorphism of trivial bundles of algebras

{\mathbb{C}}{\rm l}_{\pm n}\coprod{\mathbb{C}}{\rm l}_{\mp n}\simeq\dagger^{*}(\overline{{\mathbb{C}}{\rm l}}_{\mp n}\coprod\overline{{\mathbb{C}}{\rm l}}_{\pm n})

over
{\rm Ob}(\widetilde{\mathscr{C}}^{2|1}(M))\simeq{\sf Map}({\mathbb{R}}^{0|1},M)\times{\mathbb{R}}_{>0}\coprod{\sf Map}({\mathbb{R}}^{0|1},M)\times{\mathbb{R}}_{>0}
with additional involutive data. It suffices to consider the case
M={\rm pt}
. An isomorphism of algebras

(396)

\displaystyle{\mathbb{C}}{\rm l}_{\pm n}\simeq\overline{{\mathbb{C}}{\rm l}}_{\mp n}=\overline{{\mathbb{C}}{\rm l}}_{\pm n}^{\sf{op}}

determines such an invertible bimodule, where the involutive data for the bimodule is inherited from an involutive property for (
396
). Indeed, the above allows us to view
{\mathbb{C}}{\rm l}_{n}
as a
\overline{{\mathbb{C}}{\rm l}}_{\pm n}^{\sf{op}}
-
{\mathbb{C}}{\rm l}_{n}
bimodule. An involutive algebra isomorphism (
396
) is precisely the data of a
*
-superalgebra structure. By Lemma
5.13
, the
{\mathbb{Z}}/4
-equivariance property for this reflection structure is the compatibility (
395
) between the
*
-superalgebra structure and the anti-involution (
341
). Finally, we observe that the
*
-superalgebra structure and anti-involution combine to define a real structure
{\mathbb{C}}{\rm l}_{\pm n}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\to}}\overline{{\mathbb{C}}{\rm l}}_{\pm n}
.
∎

Definition 8.13
.

Define a
*
-superalgebra structure (see Definition
B.4
) on
{\mathbb{C}}{\rm l}_{n}
given by the
{\mathbb{C}}
-antilinear extension of the map on generators,

(397)

\displaystyle f_{j}^{*}=-if_{j},\qquad e_{j}^{*}=ie_{j}.

using the notation of generators for the Clifford algebra from (
D.1
).

Remark 8.14
.

Under the translation between
*
-superalgebras and
*
-structures on a
{\mathbb{Z}}/2
-graded algebras (e.g., see
[
DEF+99
, page 91]
), (

) corresponds to the standard

-structure (

) on the Clifford algebras. We observe that the standard anti-involution (

) and the

-superalgebra structure (

) satisfy the compatibility property (

). These structures combine to define the standard real structure
{\mathbb{C}}{\rm l}_{\pm n}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\to}}\overline{{\mathbb{C}}{\rm l}}_{\pm n}
determining the real Clifford algebras
{\rm Cl}_{\pm n}\subset{\mathbb{C}}{\rm l}_{\pm n}
.

With the chosen reflection structure for the Clifford twist given by (
397
), a reflection structure on a degree
n
representation
E
of
\mathscr{C}^{2|1}(M)
specifies isomorphisms of Clifford module bundle over
{\sf Map}({\mathbb{R}}^{0|1},M)\times{\mathbb{R}}_{>0}
(see Definition
C.25
)

(398)

\displaystyle\widetilde{{\mathcal{E}}}_{\mp}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\to}}\dagger^{*}\overline{\widetilde{{\mathcal{E}}}_{\pm}},

relative to the
*
-superalgebra structure (

) on
{\mathbb{C}}{\rm l}_{\pm n}
.

Definition 8.15
.

Suppose we are given a reflection structure on a degree
n
representation
E
of
\mathscr{C}^{2|1}(M)
with a pairing
{\beta}
. Then
E
is
reflection positive
if the pairing

\langle-,-\rangle\colon\dagger^{*}\overline{\widetilde{{\mathcal{E}}}_{+}}\otimes_{{\mathbb{C}}{\rm l}_{n}}\widetilde{{\mathcal{E}}}_{+}\simeq{\mathbb{C}}{\rm l}_{n}\otimes_{\overline{{\mathbb{C}}{\rm l}}_{n}\otimes{\mathbb{C}}{\rm l}_{n}}(\dagger^{*}\overline{\widetilde{{\mathcal{E}}}_{+}}\otimes\widetilde{{\mathcal{E}}}_{+})\simeq{\mathbb{C}}{\rm l}_{n}\otimes_{{\mathbb{C}}{\rm l}_{-n}\otimes{\mathbb{C}}{\rm l}_{n}}(\widetilde{{\mathcal{E}}}_{-}\otimes\widetilde{{\mathcal{E}}}_{+})\xrightarrow{{\beta}}\underline{{\mathbb{C}}}

restricts along
M\times{\mathbb{R}}_{>0}\subset{\sf Map}({\mathbb{R}}^{0|1},M)\times{\mathbb{R}}_{>0}
to a positive hermitian pairing (in the
{\mathbb{Z}}/2
-graded sense), where the second isomorphism above uses (
398
).

Proposition 8.16
.

For a degree
n
reflection positive representation
E
of
\mathscr{C}^{2|1}(M)
, the associated 1-parameter family of
{\mathbb{C}}{\rm l}_{n}
-linear superconnections
{\mathbb{A}}_{\ell}
is self-adjoint for each
\ell\in{\mathbb{R}}_{>0}
, the
S^{1}
-action is unitary, and the
{\mathbb{C}}{\rm l}_{\pm}
-action is self-adjoint with respect to the pairing
\langle-,-\rangle
from Definition
8.15
.

Proof.

Relative to the isomorphisms (
398
), the reflection structure on
E
requires that the super semigroup representations (
365
) satisfy

\rho_{\pm}(\ell,\tau,\bar{\tau},\eta)=(\dagger^{*}\overline{\rho}_{\mp})(\ell,\tau,\bar{\tau},\eta)=i^{\rm deg}\overline{\rho}_{\mp}(\ell,\bar{\tau},\tau,\eta)

Taylor expanding as in (
8.1
), we find that the superconnection
{\mathbb{A}}_{\ell}^{-}
is sent to
-i^{{\rm deg}+1}\overline{{\mathbb{A}}}_{\ell}^{+}
under the isomorphism (
398
). Then by the adjunction formula (
394
) and the definition of the hermitian pairing, we obtain the desired self-adjointness property for
{\mathbb{A}}_{\ell}^{+}
. The unitary property for the
S^{1}
-action follows similarly. Self-adjointness of the Clifford action follows from the definition of the reflection structure for the Clifford twist using the
*
-superalgebra structure (
397
) and the definition of the pairing
\langle-,-\rangle
.
∎

Proposition 8.17
.

A reflection positive degree
n
representation
E
of
\mathscr{C}^{2|1}(M)
equips the vector bundle
{\mathcal{E}}={\mathcal{E}}_{+}
with a real structure such that the pairings in Definition
8.15
comes from ordinary (i.e., not
{\mathbb{Z}}/2
-graded) real pairings on the underlying real bundle of
{\rm Cl}_{n}
-modules and the 1-parameter family of self-adjoint superconnections
{\mathbb{A}}_{\ell}={\mathbb{A}}_{\ell}^{+}
is a real family of superconnections.

Proof.

The isomorphism (
388
) and the reflection structure (
398
) combine to give an isomorphism

(399)

\displaystyle\widetilde{{\mathcal{E}}}_{\pm}\xrightarrow{\sim}\dagger^{*}(\overline{\sqrt{{\rm Fl}}})^{*}\overline{\widetilde{{\mathcal{E}}}_{\pm}}

that when restricted to
M\times{\mathbb{R}}_{>0}\subset{\sf Map}({\mathbb{R}}^{0|1},M)\times{\mathbb{R}}_{>0}
is an
{\mathbb{R}}_{>0}
-family of real structures
{\mathcal{E}}_{\pm}\simeq\overline{{\mathcal{E}}}_{\pm}
, relative to the real structure on
{\mathbb{C}}{\rm l}_{n}
specified by the
*
-structure (
397
) and anti-automorphism (
532
). Below, let
x\mapsto\overline{x}
denote the image of a section under this family of real structures.
We claim that the pairing satisfies the condition

(400)

\displaystyle\langle x,y\rangle=(-1)^{|\overline{x}|}\overline{\langle\overline{x},\overline{y}\rangle}

for
x,y\in\Gamma({\mathcal{E}}_{+})
. This follows from (
240
): commuting
\dagger
and
\sqrt{{\rm Fl}}
introduces a sign depending on parity. The condition (
400
) implies that the pairing is real for pairings between even sections, zero for pairings between even and odd sections, and purely imaginary for pairings between odd sections. The translation between a super Hilbert space and a
{\mathbb{Z}}/2
-graded Hilbert space is (compare
[
DEF+99
, page 91]
)

\displaystyle\langle v,w\rangle_{0}:=\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}\langle v,w\rangle&v,w\in\Gamma({\mathcal{E}}_{+})^{\rm ev}\\
i^{-1}\langle v,w\rangle&v,w\in\Gamma({\mathcal{E}}_{+})^{\rm odd}\\
0&{\rm else}\end{array}\right.

and so we see that the condition (
400
) leads to a real pairing in the usual sense under this translation. Finally, the super semigroup representations (
365
) must pull back to themselves along the real structure (
399
)

(405)

\displaystyle\rho_{\pm}(\ell,\tau,\bar{\tau},\eta)=(\dagger^{*}(\overline{\sqrt{{\rm Fl}}})^{*}\overline{\rho}_{\pm})(\ell,\tau,\bar{\tau},\eta).

In particular, the Taylor component
{\sf b}_{+}(\ell,0,0)
(as in (
8.1
)) pulls back to itself. This is precisely the condition that the superconnection is real.
∎

Proposition 8.18
.

Assuming Hypothesis
7.10
, the restriction of a reflection structure on a degree
n
,
2|1
-Euclidean field theory along (
358
) determines a reflection structure on a degree
n
representation of
\mathscr{C}^{2|1}(M)
. The restriction of a reflection positive degree
n
,
2|1
-Euclidean field theory along (
358
) determines a reflection positive degree
n
representation of
\mathscr{C}^{2|1}(M)
.

Proof.

Hypothesis
7.10
implies that the reflection structure for the degree
n
twist comes from the
*
-superalgebra structure on
{\mathbb{C}}{\rm l}_{n}
. If we then further examine the data in Proposition
7.8
and Corollary
7.21
, the restriction of a reflection positive field theory along (
317
) determines a reflection positive degree
n
representation for the reflection structure on
\mathscr{C}^{2|1}(M)
given by
\dagger
. The symmetry of the pairing in Definition
8.15
follows from Lemma
7.24
. Finally, we observe that the positivity condition in Definition
7.25
is equivalent to the positivity condition in Definition
8.15
.
∎

8.5.
Trace class representations

We recall the super Euclidean double loop space
\mathcal{L}^{2|1}(M)
from Definition
4.28
with its action by
{\mathbb{E}}^{2|1}\rtimes{\rm Spin}(2)\times{\rm SL}_{2}({\mathbb{Z}})
and the span (
243
) that regards a subspace of supercylinders in
M
as supertori in
M
. Define the subspace of nearly constant super double loops,

(406)

\displaystyle\mathcal{L}_{0}^{2|1}(M):={\sf Map}({\mathbb{R}}^{0|1},M)\times s{\sf Lat}\subset{\sf Map}({\mathbb{R}}^{2|1}/{\mathbb{Z}}^{2},M)\times s{\sf Lat}=\mathcal{L}^{2|1}(M).

The action by
{\mathbb{E}}^{2|1}\rtimes{\rm Spin}(2)\times{\rm SL}_{2}({\mathbb{Z}})
preserves the inclusion (
406
). The assignment
M\mapsto\mathcal{L}_{0}^{2|1}(M)/\!\!/({\mathbb{E}}^{2|1}\rtimes{\rm Spin}(2)\times{\rm SL}_{2}({\mathbb{Z}}))
is natural in the manifold
M
, and hence there is a canonical map to the stack of super Euclidean tori,

(407)

\displaystyle\mathcal{L}_{0}^{2|1}(M)/\!\!/({\mathbb{E}}^{2|1}\rtimes{\rm Spin}(2)\times{\rm SL}_{2}({\mathbb{Z}}))\to s{\sf Lat}/\!\!/({\mathbb{E}}^{2|1}\rtimes{\rm Spin}(2)\times{\rm SL}_{2}({\mathbb{Z}})).

Over the target of the above map, we take the extensions of the Pfaffian line bundle specified by Proposition
7.19
. Explicitly, this line bundle is classified by the map of stacks

(408)

\displaystyle s{\sf Lat}/\!\!/{\rm Spin}(2)\times{\rm SL}_{2}({\mathbb{Z}})\to{\rm pt}/\!\!/U(1),\quad{\rm Spin}(2)\times{\rm SL}_{2}({\mathbb{Z}})\to U(1),

determined by the homomorphism specified in (
451
).
We use the same notation
{\rm Pf}
for the pullback of the line bundle classified by (
408
) along the map of stacks (
407
).
To give an explicit description of the sections of
{\rm Pf}
over
\mathcal{L}_{0}^{2|1}(M)
, we adopt notation

C^{\infty}_{j/2,k/2}({\sf Lat}):=\{f\in C^{\infty}({\sf Lat})\mid(u,\bar{u})\cdot f=u^{j}\bar{u}^{k}f,\ (u,\bar{u})\in{\rm Spin}(2)\}\subset{\mathbb{C}}^{\infty}({\sf Lat})

for functions on
{\sf Lat}
that transform with weight
(j,k)
for the
{\rm Spin}(2)\simeq U(1)
-action on
{\sf Lat}
.

Remark 8.19
.

The
{\rm SL}_{2}({\mathbb{Z}})
-invariant elements
C^{\infty}_{j/2,k/2}({\sf Lat})^{{\rm SL}_{2}({\mathbb{Z}})}
determine global sections of
{\rm Pf}^{-j/2}\otimes\overline{{\rm Pf}}{}^{-k/2}
where
{\rm Pf}
and
\overline{{\rm Pf}}
are the Pfaffian line bundle and its conjugate over the moduli stack of (non-super) Euclidean spin tori; see §
A.2
.

Proof of Proposition
1.18
.

There is an injection

\Gamma(\mathcal{L}_{0}^{2|1}(M)/\!\!/({\mathbb{E}}^{2|1}\rtimes{\rm Spin}(2)\times{\rm SL}_{2}({\mathbb{Z}}));{\rm Pf}^{\otimes n})\hookrightarrow C^{\infty}({\sf Map}({\mathbb{R}}^{0|1},M)\times s{\sf Lat})

that realizes sections as functions on
{\sf Map}({\mathbb{R}}^{0|1},M)\times s{\sf Lat}
that transform appropriately for the action by
{\mathbb{E}}^{2|1}\rtimes{\rm Spin}(2)\times{\rm SL}_{2}({\mathbb{Z}})
. Specifically, sections are invariant under the
{\mathbb{E}}^{2|1}\times{\rm SL}_{2}({\mathbb{Z}})
-action and transform with weight for the the
{\rm Spin}(2)\simeq U(1)
-action. Using the identification,

C^{\infty}({\sf Map}({\mathbb{R}}^{0|1},M)\times s{\sf Lat})\simeq C^{\infty}(s{\sf Lat};C^{\infty}({\sf Map}({\mathbb{R}}^{0|1},M)))\simeq C^{\infty}({\sf Lat};\Omega^{\bullet}(M)[\lambda_{1},\lambda_{2}]/(\lambda_{1}\lambda_{2}))

that Taylor expands in the odd variables
\lambda_{1},\lambda_{2}\in C^{\infty}(s{\sf Lat})
, any function can be written as

(409)

\displaystyle\omega

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle(2{\rm vol})^{{\rm deg}/2}(\omega_{0}+\lambda_{1}(2{\rm vol})^{1/2}\omega_{1}+\lambda_{2}(2{\rm vol})^{1/2}\omega_{2}),

\displaystyle\omega_{i}\in C^{\infty}({\sf Lat};\Omega^{\bullet}(M))\simeq\Omega^{\bullet}(M;C^{\infty}({\sf Lat}))

where
{\rm vol}:=\frac{\ell_{1}\bar{\ell}_{2}-\bar{\ell}_{1}\ell_{2}}{2i}\in C^{\infty}({\sf Lat})^{{\rm SL}_{2}({\mathbb{Z}})}
is a real-valued nonvanishing function. By
[
BE22
, Lemma 3.19]
, the function
\omega
is
{\mathbb{E}}^{2|1}
-invariant if and only if
2
2

2

Equation (
410
) differs from
[
BE22
, Lemma 3.19]
by a factor of
1/2
because the normalization for
\omega
chosen above has an extra factor of
2^{{\rm deg}/2}
, which will be more convenient below.

(410)

\displaystyle{d}\omega_{0}=0,\quad\partial_{\bar{\ell}_{i}}\omega_{0}=\frac{1}{2}{d}\omega_{i},\quad i=1,2.

Next we consider
{\rm Spin}(2)
-equivariance: for
(u,\bar{u})\in U(1)\simeq{\rm Spin}(2)
we will use that

(u,\bar{u})\cdot{\rm vol}^{1/2}=u\bar{u}{\rm vol}^{1/2},\ (u,\bar{u})\cdot\lambda_{i}=\bar{u}\lambda_{i},\ (u,\bar{u})\cdot\alpha=\bar{u}^{-k}\alpha,\ \alpha\in\Omega^{k}(M)\subset\Omega^{k}(M;C^{\infty}({\sf Lat}))

where in the last calculation, we view a
k
-form as a differential form as an element of
\Omega^{k}(M;C^{\infty}({\sf Lat}))
via the inclusion of constant functions
{\mathbb{C}}\hookrightarrow C^{\infty}({\sf Lat})
.
From the above, we deduce that for
\omega_{i}
to be in the
n^{\rm th}
weight space of of the
U(1)
-action, we require

\omega_{0}\in\bigoplus_{k+j=n}\Omega^{k}(M;C^{\infty}_{j/2,0}({\sf Lat})),\quad\omega_{i}\in\bigoplus_{k+j=n-1}\Omega^{k}(M;C^{\infty}_{j/2,1}({\sf Lat})).

We further observe that
\omega_{0}
is required to be
{\rm SL}_{2}({\mathbb{Z}})
-invariant, so by Lemma
A.4
and (
A.3
), restriction along
{\mathfrak{H}}\times{\mathbb{R}}_{>0}\hookrightarrow{\sf Lat}
gives

(411)

\displaystyle Z

\displaystyle:=

\displaystyle\ell^{({\rm deg}-n)/2}(\omega_{0}|_{{\mathfrak{H}}\times{\mathbb{R}}_{>0}})=(\ell^{({\rm deg}-n)/2}(2{\rm vol})^{-{\rm deg}/2}\omega)|_{{\mathring{{\mathbb{H}}}}\times{\mathbb{R}}_{>0}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\ell^{-n/2}(2{\rm im}(\tau))^{-{\rm deg}/2}(\omega|_{{\mathfrak{H}}\times{\mathbb{R}}_{>0}})\in\bigoplus_{j+k=n}\Omega^{j}(M;{\rm mf}^{k,0})

where in the second line we used that
{\rm vol}|_{{\mathfrak{H}}\times{\mathbb{R}}_{>0}}={\rm im}(\tau)\ell
. Unpacking the notation from §
A
,
Z
is a differential form valued in
C^{\infty}({\mathfrak{H}}\times{\mathbb{R}}_{>0})
with the transformation property

(412)

\displaystyle Z\mapsto(cz+d)^{({\rm deg}-n)/2}Z,\qquad z=\tau/\ell

for the
{\rm MP}_{2}({\mathbb{Z}})
-action on coefficients. We note the diffeomorphism

{\mathfrak{H}}\times{\mathbb{R}}_{>0}\xrightarrow{\sim}{\mathfrak{H}}\times{\mathbb{R}}_{>0},\qquad(\tau,\bar{\tau},\ell)\mapsto(\tau/\ell,\bar{\tau}/\ell,\ell{\rm im}(\tau))

giving coordinates on
{\mathfrak{H}}\times{\mathbb{R}}_{>0}
in terms of the conformal modulus
(z,\bar{z})=(\tau/\ell,\bar{\tau}/\ell)
and total volume
v=\ell{\rm im}(\tau)
of the associated torus. By
[
BE22
, Lemma 3.21]
, the data of
\omega_{1},\omega_{2}
are equivalent to

(413)

\displaystyle Z_{v}

\displaystyle\in

\displaystyle\bigoplus_{j+k=n-1}\Omega^{j}(M;{\rm mf}^{(k-2),-2})

(414)

\displaystyle Z_{\bar{z}}

\displaystyle\in

\displaystyle\bigoplus_{j+k=n-1}\Omega^{j}(M;{\rm mf}^{k,-2})

i.e., differential forms with values in
C^{\infty}({\mathfrak{H}}\times{\mathbb{R}}_{>0})
with transformation properties

Z_{\bar{z}}\mapsto(cz+d)^{({\rm deg}-n-1)/2}(c\bar{z}+d)Z_{\bar{z}},\qquad Z_{v}\mapsto(cz+n)^{({\rm deg}-n-3)/2}(c\bar{z}+d)Z_{v}

for the
{\rm MP}_{2}({\mathbb{Z}})
-action on coefficients. By
[
BE22
, Lemma 3.21]
, property (
410
) is equivalent to

\partial_{\bar{z}}Z={d}Z_{\bar{z}},\qquad\partial_{v}Z={d}Z.

This completes the proof.
∎

The element
\Gamma_{n}\in{\mathbb{C}}{\rm l}_{n}
is defined in (
D.1
) and determines the Clifford supertrace (
548
).

Definition 8.20
.

A
trace class
degree
n
representation of
\mathscr{C}^{2|1}(M)
is the data of a degree
n
representation
E
of
\mathscr{C}^{2|1}(M)
and a section
(Z,Z_{\tau},Z_{v})\in\Gamma(\mathcal{L}^{2|1}_{0}(M);{\rm Pf}^{\otimes n})
. We require these data to satisfy the properties:

(i)

the (ordinary) trace exists

(415)

\displaystyle{\sf Tr}(\Gamma_{n}\circ\rho_{+}|_{M\times({\mathbb{R}}_{>0}\times{\mathring{{\mathbb{H}}}}_{+})/{\mathbb{Z}}})\in C^{\infty}(M;C^{\infty}(({\mathbb{R}}_{>0}\times{\mathring{{\mathbb{H}}}}_{+})/{\mathbb{Z}}))

and

(ii)

the supertrace has the compatibility condition

(416)

Z=\phi(\ell,z)^{-n}(2{\rm im}(z))^{-{\rm deg}/2}{\sf Tr}_{{\mathbb{C}}{\rm l}_{n}}(\rho_{+}|_{{\sf Map}({\mathbb{R}}^{0|1},M)\times({\mathbb{R}}_{>0}\times{\mathring{{\mathbb{H}}}}_{+})/{\mathbb{Z}}})\in\Omega^{\bullet}(M;(C^{\infty}({\mathbb{R}}_{>0}\times{\mathring{{\mathbb{H}}}}_{+})/{\mathbb{Z}}))

where
{\sf Tr}_{{\mathbb{C}}{\rm l}_{n}}
denotes the Clifford supertrace and

\phi(\ell,\tau)=\prod_{n>0}(1-e^{2\pi i\tau/\ell})\in C^{\infty}({\mathbb{R}}_{>0}\times{\mathring{{\mathbb{H}}}}_{+})

is closely related to the Dedekind
\eta
-function.

Remark 8.21
.

The condition (
416
) is equivalent to requiring
Z
to be equal to the Clifford supertrace of the super semigroup representation in
{\rm Fer}^{\pm n}_{\ell}
-modules, see (
375
).

Proposition 8.22
.

Assuming Hypothesis
7.10
, the restriction of a degree
n
field theory along (
358
) determines a trace class degree
n
representation of
\mathscr{C}^{2|1}(M)
.

Proof.

The argument follows
[
ST11
, Lemma 3.20]
. In the terminology of
[
ST12
]
, the bordisms in the image of the composition

(417)

\displaystyle{\sf Map}({\mathbb{R}}^{0|1},M)\times({\mathbb{R}}_{>0}\times{\mathring{{\mathbb{H}}}}_{+})/{\mathbb{Z}}\hookrightarrow{\rm Mor}(\mathscr{C}^{2|1}(M))\to{\rm Mor}(2|1\hbox{-}{\sf EBord}(M))

are
thick
morphisms in the bordism category: this follows directly from the factorization in Proposition
6.16
. The categorical trace of a thick morphism is an endomorphism of the monoidal unit. Proposition
6.18
shows that this categorical trace is the image of the composition

(418)

\displaystyle{\sf Map}({\mathbb{R}}^{0|1},M)\times({\mathbb{R}}_{>0}\times{\mathring{{\mathbb{H}}}}_{+})/{\mathbb{Z}}\to\mathcal{L}_{0}^{2|1}(M)\to{\rm Mor}(2|1\hbox{-}{\sf EBord}(M)).

Applying a degree
n
field theory to these families, Corollary
7.21
shows that the categorical trace of the family (
417
) is sent to the composition

(419)

\displaystyle{\mathbb{C}}{\rm l}_{n}\otimes_{{\mathbb{C}}{\rm l}_{n}\otimes{\mathbb{C}}{\rm l}_{n}}{\mathbb{C}}{\rm l}_{n}\xrightarrow{{{\rm id}}_{{\mathbb{C}}{\rm l}_{n}}\otimes{\sf r}_{M}^{+}}{\mathbb{C}}{\rm l}_{n}\otimes_{{\mathbb{C}}{\rm l}_{n}\otimes{\mathbb{C}}{\rm l}_{n}}(\widetilde{{\mathcal{E}}}_{+}\otimes\widetilde{{\mathcal{E}}}_{-})\xrightarrow{\sigma}{\mathbb{C}}{\rm l}_{n}\otimes_{{\mathbb{C}}{\rm l}_{n}\otimes{\mathbb{C}}{\rm l}_{n}}(\widetilde{{\mathcal{E}}}_{-}\otimes\widetilde{{\mathcal{E}}}_{+})\xrightarrow{\mathring{\sf l}_{M}^{-}}\underline{{\mathbb{C}}},

for the families of small bordisms
{\sf r}_{M}^{+}
and
{\sf l}_{M}^{-}
from (
6.26
), where
\mathring{\sf l}_{M}^{-}
denotes the restriction of
{\sf l}_{M}^{-}
along
0\in{\mathbb{H}}_{-}^{2|1}
.
The element
\Gamma_{n}
provides an identification
{\mathbb{C}}{\rm l}_{n}\otimes_{{\mathbb{C}}{\rm l}_{n}\otimes{\mathbb{C}}{\rm l}_{n}}{\mathbb{C}}{\rm l}_{n}\simeq\underline{{\mathbb{C}}}
with the trivial line bundle, and the composition (
419
) becomes the Clifford supertrace. Using that nuclear Fréchet spaces have the approximation property
[
Sch71
, page 109]
, we find that the categorical trace above is independent of the choice of thickener
[
ST12
, Theorem 1.7]
. Finally, Lemma
C.40
translates between the Clifford trace and the trace associated to
{\rm Fer}_{n}
, giving the property (
416
). The argument for the trace property (
415
) is similar, but one modifies the outgoing boundary of the supercylinder in the image of (
418
) by the spin flip automorphism. This changes the supertrace to an ordinary trace.

To recover the claimed compatibility formula (
416
), the above argument gives

(420)

\displaystyle\omega|_{{\mathfrak{H}}\times{\mathbb{R}}_{>0}}=\ell^{n/2}\phi(\ell,\tau)^{-n}{\sf Tr}_{{\mathbb{C}}{\rm l}_{n}}(\rho_{+}|_{{\sf Map}({\mathbb{R}}^{0|1},M)\times({\mathbb{R}}_{>0}\times{\mathring{{\mathbb{H}}}}_{+})/{\mathbb{Z}}})

for
\omega
the function in (
409
) determining the section of
{\rm Pf}^{-\otimes n}
. The prefactor before the Clifford supertrace in (
420
) is the
-n
th power of the character of the invertible bimodule (
536
) as a function on
{\mathbb{R}}_{>0}\times{\mathring{{\mathbb{H}}}}_{+}
, see (
540
). Then by (
411
), we obtain (
416
).
∎

9.
Energy cutoffs and the cocycle map

In this section we use the notation (
390
) for the super vector bundle
{\mathcal{E}}
with 1-parameter family of superconnections
{\mathbb{A}}_{\ell}
associated with a degree
n
representation of
\mathscr{C}^{2|1}(M)
. For
\ell\in{\mathbb{R}}_{>0}
, we also set
\rho_{\ell}(\tau,\bar{\tau},\eta):=\rho_{+}(\ell,\tau,\bar{\tau},\eta)
, i.e.,
\rho_{\ell}
is the restriction of
\rho_{+}
of (
365
) to a fixed
\ell\in{\mathbb{R}}_{>0}
.

9.1.
Extracting a sequence of superconnections

Lemma 9.1
.

A degree
n
, reflection positive representation of
\mathscr{C}^{2|1}(M)
(in the sense of Definition
8.15
) determines an orthogonal direct sum of metrized super vector bundles

(421)

\displaystyle{\mathcal{E}}\simeq\bigoplus_{k\in{\mathbb{Z}}}{\mathcal{E}}_{k},\qquad{\mathcal{E}}_{k}\to M,

with respect to which the action of
{\mathbb{H}}^{2|1}_{+}/\ell{\mathbb{Z}}
is given by

(422)

\displaystyle\rho_{\ell}(\tau,\bar{\tau},\eta)=\bigoplus_{k\in{\mathbb{Z}}}e^{2\pi ik\tau/\ell}\rho_{\ell,k}({\rm im}(\tau),\theta),

where for each
(\ell,k)\in{\mathbb{R}}_{>0}\times{\mathbb{Z}}
,
\rho_{\ell,k}\colon{\mathbb{R}}^{1|1}_{\geq 0}\to{\rm End}(\Omega^{\bullet}(M;{\mathcal{E}}_{k,\ell}))
is a
{\mathbb{C}}{\rm l}_{-n}
-linear super semigroup representation. If the representation is also trace class, then
{\mathcal{E}}_{k}=\{0\}
for
k\ll 0
.

Proof.

The exact sequence (
4.21
) of super Lie groups gives exact sequences of super semigroups for each
\ell\in{\mathbb{R}}_{>0}
,

\displaystyle\begin{array}[]{ccccccccc}1&\to&{\mathbb{R}}/\ell{\mathbb{Z}}&\to&{\mathbb{H}}_{+}^{2|1}/\ell{\mathbb{Z}}&\to&{\mathbb{R}}^{1|1}_{\geq 0}&\to&1,\\
&&t&\mapsto&(t,t,0),\ (\tau,\bar{\tau},\eta)&\mapsto&(2{\rm im}(\tau),\eta).\end{array}

By Proposition
8.16
the action of
{\mathbb{R}}/\ell{\mathbb{Z}}
on
{\mathcal{E}}
is a unitary, and hence we obtain (
421
) as an orthogonal direct sum of weight spaces. For
p_{k}\colon{\mathcal{E}}\to{\mathcal{E}}_{k}
the orthogonal projection onto a weight space, define the super semigroup representation

\displaystyle\rho_{\ell,k}\colon{\mathbb{H}}^{2|1}_{+}/\ell{\mathbb{Z}}

\displaystyle\to

\displaystyle{\rm End}(\Omega^{\bullet}(M;{\mathcal{E}}_{k,\ell}))

(426)

\displaystyle\rho_{\ell,k}(\tau,\bar{\tau},\eta)

\displaystyle:=

\displaystyle e^{-2\pi ik\tau/\ell}\otimes(p_{k}\circ\rho_{\ell}(\tau,\bar{\tau},\eta)\circ p_{k})

where
e^{-2\pi ik\tau/\ell}\colon{\mathbb{H}}^{2|1}_{+}/\ell{\mathbb{Z}}\to{\rm End}({\mathbb{C}})
a 1-dimensional representation. The subgroup
{\mathbb{R}}/\ell{\mathbb{Z}}\subset{\mathbb{H}}^{2|1}_{+}/\ell{\mathbb{Z}}
acts trivially, and so the exact sequence (
9.1
) shows that (
426
) is determined by a representation of the quotient. This gives the desription (
422
).

Finally, the trace class condition implying
{\mathcal{E}}_{k}=\{0\}
for sufficiently negative
k
follows from a standard argument, e.g., see
[
ST11
, page 43]
or
[
ST04
, Proof of Theorem 3.3.14]
. In brief,
\rho_{\ell,k}
needs to have trace zero for
k\ll 0
if the trace (
415
) is to converge. Continuity of this trace in the parameter
(\tau,\bar{\tau},0)
implies that the identity operator
\rho_{\ell,k}(0,0,0)={{\rm id}}_{{\mathcal{E}}_{\ell,k}}
is the zero operator for
k\ll 0
, and hence
{\mathcal{E}}_{k}=\{0\}
for
k\ll 0
.
∎

Lemma 9.2
.

The 1-parameter family of superconnections from Proposition
8.2
decomposes with respect to (
421
) as an infinite direct sum of self-adjoint, real Clifford linear superconnections,

(427)

\displaystyle{\mathbb{A}}_{\ell}=\bigoplus_{k\in{\mathbb{Z}}}{\mathbb{A}}_{\ell,k},\qquad{\mathbb{A}}_{\ell,k}\colon\Omega^{\bullet}(M;{\mathcal{E}}_{k})\to\Omega^{\bullet}(M;{\mathcal{E}}_{k}).

Proof.

The infinite direct sum of superconnections comes from Taylor expanding the right hand side of (
422
) in the odd variable
\eta
and applying the argument in the proof of Proposition
8.2
, where self-adjointness follows from Proposition
8.16
and the real structure is from Proposition
8.17
.
∎

Definition 9.3
.

A reflection positive degree
n
representation of
\mathscr{C}^{2|1}(M)

admits smooth index bundles
if for all
(\ell,k)\in{\mathbb{R}}_{>0}\times{\mathbb{Z}}
, the superconnections (
427
) admit smooth index bundles in the sense of Definition
D.14
.

Proposition 9.4
.

A reflection positive degree
n
representation
E
of
\mathscr{C}^{2|1}(M)
that admits smooth index bundles determines a class

(428)

\displaystyle[E]\in{\rm KO}^{n}(M)(\!(q)\!).

Proof.

We claim it suffices to consider the case that
\mathcal{E}_{k}=\{0\}
for
k<0
. To see this, multiply the representation (
422
) by
q^{J}
for a sufficiently large power so that the resulting representation
q^{J}E
satisfies
\mathcal{E}_{k}=\{0\}
for
k<0
. The argument below then constructs an index
[q^{J}E]\in{\rm KO}^{n}(M)[\![q]\!]
. Finally, define (
428
) as
[E]:=q^{-J}[q^{J}E]\in{\rm KO}^{n}(M)(\!(q)\!).
Such a global power
J
may not exist if
M
is not compact, but by choosing an exhaustion function on
M
and constructing compatible classes on compact subsets we obtain the same reduction.

Below, let
{\mathbb{A}}_{k}:={\mathbb{A}}_{1,k}
, i.e., the
k
th 1-parameter family of superconnections (
427
) specialized to
\ell=1
. We apply the index bundle construction to the sequence of superconnections
{\mathbb{A}}_{k}
analogously to the proof Lemma
3.15
. For each
N
, define an open cover of
X
indexed by
\vec{\lambda}=(\lambda_{0},\dots,\lambda_{N})\in{\mathbb{R}}_{>0}^{N}

\displaystyle U^{\vec{\lambda}}

\displaystyle:=

\displaystyle\{x\in X\mid\lambda_{k}\notin{\rm Spec}(({\mathbb{A}}_{k}^{[0]})^{2}_{x})\}\subset X\ {\rm for}\ k\leq N\}.

Over each component of this cover one has finite-rank vector bundles
{\mathcal{E}}_{k}^{<\lambda_{k}}\to U^{\vec{\lambda}}
, and on overlaps
U^{\vec{\lambda}}\bigcap U^{\vec{\lambda^{\prime}}}
there are odd endomorphisms for
k\leq N
,

f_{k}^{\lambda_{k}\lambda_{k}^{\prime}}\in{\rm End}(U^{\vec{\lambda}}\bigcap U^{\vec{\lambda^{\prime}}},({\mathcal{E}}_{k}^{<\lambda})^{\perp}),\qquad\lambda_{k}<\lambda_{k}^{\prime}

defined on the orthogonal complement to the inclusion
{\mathcal{E}}_{k}^{<\lambda}\hookrightarrow{\mathcal{E}}_{k}^{<\lambda^{\prime}}
. Hence, for each
N
Proposition
D.17
gives a map

(429)

\displaystyle\left[\{U^{\vec{\lambda}},\bigoplus_{k=0}^{N}{\mathcal{E}}_{k}^{<\lambda_{k}},\bigoplus_{k=0}^{N}f_{k}^{\lambda_{k}\lambda_{k}^{\prime}}\}_{\vec{\lambda}}\right]\colon X\to({\rm KO}\wedge\{1,q,\dots q^{N}\}_{+})^{n}

determined by a topological functor (see (
560
)) from the Čech category of
\{U^{\vec{\lambda}}\}
to a topological category whose classifying space represents the
n
th space in the
N
th spectrum in the homotopy colimit diagram (
91
). A refinement of an open cover determines a functor between Čech categories inducing an equivalence; so by
[
Seg68
, Proposition 2.1]
the maps constructed above come equipped with canonical homotopy compatibilities for increasing
N
. Therefore, the maps (
429
) are part of the data of a map into the homotopy colimit diagram (
91
) and hence defines a map into the spectrum
{\rm KO}[\![q]\!]
.
∎

9.2.
Modular invariance and the Pontryagin character

Definition 9.5
.

A self-adjoint representation of
\mathscr{C}^{2|1}(M)
is
infinitesimally generated
if
\rho_{\ell}(\tau,\bar{\tau},\eta)
is determined by the formula

(430)

\displaystyle\rho_{\ell}(\tau,\bar{\tau},\eta)=\bigoplus_{k\in{\mathbb{Z}}}e^{2\pi ik\tau/\ell}\rho_{\ell,k}(2{\rm im}(\tau),\eta)=\bigoplus_{k\in{\mathbb{Z}}}e^{2\pi ik\tau/\ell}e^{-2{\rm im}(\tau){\mathbb{A}}_{\ell,k}^{2}+\eta{\mathbb{A}}_{\ell,k}}

for the sequence of self-adjoint, Clifford linear superconnections,

{\mathbb{A}}_{\ell,k}:=p_{k}\circ{\mathbb{A}}_{\ell}\circ p_{k}

where
{\mathbb{A}}_{\ell}
is the 1-parameter family of self-adjoint superconnections on
{\mathcal{E}}
from (
390
) and
p_{k}\colon{\mathcal{E}}\to{\mathcal{E}}_{k}
is the orthogonal projection.

Remark 9.6
.

Assuming existence and uniqueness of solutions to ordinary differential equations, a self-adjoint representation of
\mathscr{C}^{2|1}(M)
is automatically infinitesimally generated, e.g., if the vector bundles
{\mathcal{E}}_{k}
are finite-dimensional for all
k
. However, existence and uniqueness can fail in general topological vector spaces, see
[
Ham82
, Example 5.6.1]
for counterexamples in Fréchet spaces. From the physics point of view, one expects field theories to be determined by infinitesimal data. This is essential if one is to make sense out of physical quantities like energy as arising from eigenvalues of operators, which is a prerequisite for discussing energy cutoffs in effective field theory.
To force existence and uniqueness, one could modify the functional analytic framework for field theories, e.g., to tame Fréchet spaces and tame maps, or some variant of Hilbert bundles and continuous maps. A more Wilsonian approach would be to consider representations in Ind-finite vector bundles with compatibilities via the energy filtration. Such conditions quickly become technical, and the correct analytical context for extending down to points in conjecture (
1
) is not yet clear. As such, we prefer to leave these analytical choices flexible, whence Definition
9.5
.

Proposition 9.7
.

For a sequence of superconnections extracted from an infinitesimally generated trace class degree
n
representation of
\mathscr{C}^{2|1}(M)
, the normalized Clifford supertrace

Z=\phi(\ell,\tau)^{-n}\sum q^{k}{\sf sTr}_{{\mathbb{C}}{\rm l}_{n}}(e^{-({\mathbb{A}}_{\ell,k}(2{\rm im}(\tau)))^{2}})\in\Omega^{\bullet}(M;C^{\infty}({\mathring{{\mathbb{H}}}}\times{\mathbb{R}}_{>0}))

transforms as (
412
) for the
{\rm MP}_{2}({\mathbb{Z}})
-action on
{\mathring{{\mathbb{H}}}}_{+}\times{\mathbb{R}}_{>0}
, i.e., transforms as a section of
{\rm Pf}^{-\otimes n}
. In the above,
{\mathbb{A}}_{\ell,k}(2{\rm im}(\tau))
is the rescaling (
113
) of the superconnection
{\mathbb{A}}_{\ell,k}
by
2{\rm im}(\tau)
.

Proof.

Using the definition of a trace class representation, the equality (
416
) from Proposition
8.20
yields

(431)

\displaystyle Z

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle(2{\rm im}(\tau))^{-{\rm deg}/2}\phi(\ell,\tau)^{-n}{\sf sTr}_{{\mathbb{C}}{\rm l}_{n}}(\rho_{+}(\tau,\bar{\tau},0))

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\phi(\ell,\tau)^{-n}\sum q^{k}(2{\rm im}(\tau))^{-{\rm deg}/2}{\sf sTr}_{{\mathbb{C}}{\rm l}_{n}}(e^{-2{\rm im}(\tau){\mathbb{A}}_{\ell,k}^{2}})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\phi(\ell,\tau)^{-n}\sum q^{k}{\sf sTr}_{{\mathbb{C}}{\rm l}_{n}}(e^{-({\mathbb{A}}_{\ell,k}(2{\rm im}(\tau))^{2}}),

where
Z
is modular by (
411
). This completes the proof.
∎

9.3.
Homotopy compatibility of the index bundle and the partition function

Lemma 9.8
.

There is a uniquely determined coboundary between the Pontryagin character of the cocycle constructed in Proposition
9.4
and the differential form

(432)

\displaystyle\phi(1,\tau)^{-n}\sum q^{k}{\sf sTr}_{{\rm Cl}_{n}}(e^{-{\mathbb{A}}_{k}(2{\rm im}(\tau))^{2}})\in\Omega^{\bullet}(M;C^{\infty}({\mathbb{R}}_{>0})(\!(q)\!))=Z|_{\ell=1}

where above we identify the rescaling paramter
2{\rm im}(\tau)\in C^{\infty}({\mathbb{R}}_{>0})\subset C^{\infty}({\mathbb{R}}_{>0})(\!(q)\!)
with the standard coordinate.

Proof.

The existence of index bundles for
{\mathbb{A}}_{k}
implies that for each
(\lambda,k)
there are uniquely determined coboundaries (see Lemma
D.18
)

(433)

\displaystyle d\omega_{k}^{\lambda}={\sf sTr}_{{\rm Cl}_{n}}(e^{-{\mathbb{A}}_{k}(2{\rm im}(\tau))^{2}})|_{U_{\lambda}}-{\rm Ch}(\nabla^{<\lambda_{k}})\in\Omega(U_{\lambda_{k}};{\mathbb{C}}[u^{\pm 2}]).

The Pontryagin character of the cocycle constructed in Proposition
9.4
is a Čech–de Rham cocycle for each
N
(compare (
130
)) with compatibilities between different
N
coming from refinements of the cover. For each
N
, restricting the coboundaries (
433
) along a refinement of covers gives a coboundary in the Čech–de Rham complex between the Pontryagin character of the index bundle and (
432
).
∎

Lemma 9.9
.

Given an infinitesimally generated, self-adjoint representation of
\mathscr{C}^{2|1}(M)
, there are Chern–Simons form associated to the
\ell
-parameter family of superconnections, giving

\sum q^{k}{\sf sTr}_{{\mathbb{C}}{\rm l}_{n}}(e^{-({\mathbb{A}}_{\ell,k}(2{\rm im}(\tau))^{2}})=\sum q^{k}{\sf sTr}_{{\mathbb{C}}{\rm l}_{n}}(e^{-({\mathbb{A}}_{1,k}(2{\rm im}(\tau))^{2}})+d\sum q^{k}\zeta_{k}\in\Omega^{\bullet}(M;C^{\infty}({\mathbb{R}}_{>0})(\!(q)\!))

Proof.

The supertrace
{\sf sTr}(e^{-{\mathbb{A}}_{\ell,k}(2{\rm im}(\tau))^{2}})
varies smoothly with
\ell
. The lemma then follows from the standard construction of Chern–Simons forms applied to the 1-parameter family of superconnections
{\mathbb{A}}_{\ell,k}
for the parameter
\ell\in{\mathbb{R}}_{>0}
,

\displaystyle\zeta_{k}(\ell)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\int_{1}^{\ell}{\sf sTr}_{{\mathbb{C}}{\rm l}_{n}}\left(\frac{d{\mathbb{A}}_{s,k}(2{\rm im}(\tau))}{ds}e^{-{\mathbb{A}}_{s,k}(2{\rm im}(\tau))^{2}}\right)ds\in\Omega^{\bullet}(M;C^{\infty}({\mathbb{R}}_{>0}))

\displaystyle d\zeta_{k}(\ell)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle{\sf sTr}_{{\mathbb{C}}{\rm l}_{n}}(e^{-({\mathbb{A}}_{\ell,k}(2{\rm im}(\tau)))^{2}})-{\sf sTr}_{{\mathbb{C}}{\rm l}_{n}}(e^{-({\mathbb{A}}_{1,k}(2{\rm im}(\tau))^{2}})\in\Omega^{\bullet}(M;C^{\infty}({\mathbb{R}}_{>0}))

where (as before),
2{\rm im}(\tau)\in C^{\infty}({\mathbb{R}}_{>0})
is identified with the standard coordinate function in the coefficient ring.
∎

9.4.
The groupoid
2|1\hbox{-{\sf eft}}^{n}(M)
and the proof of Theorem
1.1

By Definition
1.21
of the groupoid
2|1\hbox{-{\sf eft}}^{n}(M)
, an object
E\in 2|1\hbox{-{\sf eft}}^{n}(M)
determines the geometric data in Proposition
1.13
satisfying the cutoff property in Definition
1.14
.

Proof of Theorem
1.1
.

Proposition
9.4
gives a cocycle
{\rm Ind}(E)
representing a class
[{\rm Ind}(E)]\in{\rm KO}^{-n}(M)(\!(q)\!)
depending on choices of cutoffs. Choose a
{\rm MP}_{2}({\mathbb{Z}})
-equivariant,
\partial_{\bar{z}}
-preimage
T
of
Z_{\bar{z}}
so that

(434)

\displaystyle Z-dT\in\Omega^{\bullet}(M;\mathcal{O}({\mathfrak{H}})\otimes C^{\infty}({\mathbb{R}})))^{{\rm MP}_{2}({\mathbb{Z}})},\quad(Z-dT)|_{{\rm vol}=1}\in\Omega^{\bullet}(M;\mathcal{O}({\mathfrak{H}}))^{{\rm MP}_{2}({\mathbb{Z}})}.

The restriction to
{\rm vol}=1
is the map on coefficients induced by restricting to the subspace
{\mathfrak{H}}\subset{\mathfrak{H}}\times{\mathbb{R}}_{>0}\subset{\sf Lat}
of lattices with volume 1. In light of (
431
), we may choose
T
so that the
q
-expansion of
(Z-dT)|_{{\rm vol}=1}
has finitely many negative powers of
q
over any subset of
M
contained in a compact subspace.

Lemmas
9.8
and
9.9
give a coboundary mediating between a cocycle representative of the Pontryagin character of
E
and
Z\in\Omega^{\bullet}(M;C^{\infty}({\mathfrak{H}}\times{\mathbb{R}}_{>0}))^{{\rm MP}_{2}({\mathbb{Z}})}
. Together with
T
we obtain a coboundary between the Pontryagin character of the index bundle and
(Z-dT)|_{{\rm vol}=1}
. Explicitly, for each
N
and
\vec{\lambda}
, we have an equality of elements in
\Omega^{\bullet}(U^{\vec{\lambda}};{\mathbb{C}}(\!(q)\!)/q^{N+1})

((Z-dT)|_{{\rm vol}=1})|_{U^{\vec{\lambda}}}=\sum^{N}q^{k}{\sf sTr}(e^{-(\nabla^{<\lambda_{k}})^{2}})-\phi(\tau)^{-n}d(\sum^{N}q^{k}(\omega_{k}^{\lambda}+\zeta_{k}|_{U^{\vec{\lambda}}})+T|_{U^{\vec{\lambda}}})|_{{\rm vol}=1}

between the restriction of the globally defined form and the component of the Čech–de Rham cocycle. Above, the
\omega_{k}^{\lambda}
come from Lemma
9.8
and the
\zeta_{k}
are from Lemma
9.9
. The cocycles
{\rm Ind}(E)
and
Z-dT
together with the above homotopy above determines a class
[E]\in{\rm KO}_{{\rm MF}}^{n}(M)
.

A priori this class depends on the choice of
\partial_{\bar{z}}
preimage
T
and the choice of cutoffs
\{\vec{\lambda}\}
for each
N
determining the locally finite cover
\{U^{\vec{\lambda}}\}
. The independence from choices of cutoffs follows from general index bundle considerations, see Proposition
D.17
. On the other hand, any pair of choices for
T
differ by an element of
\Omega^{\bullet}(M;{\rm MF})
. This changes the representative
Z-dT
of the class
[Z]\in{\rm H}^{\bullet}(M;{\rm MF})
by a coboundary in
\Omega^{\bullet}(M;{\rm MF})
while simultaneously modifying the homotopy compatibility data
T
. This determines a homotopic map to
n
th space in the spectrum representing
{\rm KO}_{\rm MF}
, and hence does not change the class
[E]\in{\rm KO}_{{\rm MF}}^{n}(M)
.
∎

9.5.
A field theory representative of the
{\rm KO}_{\rm MF}
-Euler class

We have the following recipe for constructing objects in
2|1\hbox{-{\sf eft}}^{n}(M)
.

Proposition 9.10
.

Consider data

i.

Fréchet vector bundles
{\mathcal{E}}_{k}\to M
with fiberwise
{\mathbb{C}}{\rm l}_{n}
-action and
{\mathcal{E}}_{k}=\{0\}
for
k\ll 0
;

ii.

smooth, 1-parameter families of superconnections
{\mathbb{A}}_{k,\ell}
on
{\mathcal{E}}_{k}
for
\ell\in{\mathbb{R}}_{>0}
that are
{\mathbb{C}}{\rm l}_{n}
-linear;

iii.

hermitian metrics on each
{\mathcal{E}}_{k}
relative to which the
{\mathbb{C}}{\rm l}_{n}
-action is self-adjoint and the superconnection
{\mathbb{A}}_{k,\ell}
is self-adjoint;

iv.

real structures on
{\mathcal{E}}_{k}
and
{\mathbb{C}}{\rm l}_{n}
compatible with the Clifford action and superconnection; and

v.

differential forms

(Z,Z_{\bar{z}},Z_{v})\in\Omega^{\bullet}(M;C^{\infty}({\mathfrak{H}}\times{\mathbb{R}}_{>0})[u^{\pm 2}]),\qquad|u^{2}|=-4

where
Z
has total degree
n
,
Z_{\bar{z}},Z_{v}
have total degree
n-1
, they satisfy the
{\rm MP}_{2}({\mathbb{Z}})
-invariance conditions (
411
), (
413
), and (
414
), and

{d}Z=0,\quad\partial_{\bar{z}}Z={d}Z_{\bar{z}},\quad\partial_{v}Z={d}Z_{v}

where
v\in C^{\infty}({\mathbb{R}}_{>0})
is the standard real coordinate, and
(z,\bar{z})\in C^{\infty}({\mathfrak{H}})
