and the identity on
F
, i.e.,
\phi
is an involutive 2-morphism.

Example C.23
.

The trivial twist
\mathbbold{1}\colon{\sf C}\to{\sf{TA}}
has canonical equivariance data for any choice of reflection structure on the source.

Categories internal to stacks also have a notion of equivariance data for internal natural transformations, defined as follows.

Definition C.24
.

Let
({\sf C},\tau_{\sf C},\eta_{\sf C},\delta_{\sf C})
and
({\sf D},\tau_{\sf D},\eta_{\sf D},\delta_{\sf D})
be internal categories with internal involution, and let
F,G\colon{\sf C}\to{\sf D}
be internal functors with equivariance data
(\phi_{F},\beta_{F})
and
(\phi_{G},\beta_{G})
.
Equivariance data
for an internal natural transformation
E\colon F\Rightarrow G
is an isomorphism
\sigma\colon\phi_{G}\circ E\simeq E\circ\phi_{F}
of natural transformations satisfying a compatibility condition with
\beta_{F}
and
\beta_{G}
.

The above combine to define reflection structures on twisted representations of internal categories.

Definition C.25
.

Given an internal category
{\sf C}
with reflection structure
(\tau,\eta,\delta)
, a
reflection structure
for a twist
\mathscr{T}\colon{\sf C}\to{\sf{TA}}
is equivariance data for
\mathscr{T}
(in the sense of Definition
C.21
) relative to the reflection structure on
{\sf{TA}}
from Lemma
C.20
. Isomorphisms between twists with reflection structure are internal natural isomorphisms
\mathscr{T}\Rightarrow\mathscr{T}^{\prime}
with equivariance data (in the sense of Definition
C.24
).

Given a
\mathscr{T}
-twisted representation
E
(
505
) and a reflection structure for
\mathscr{T}
, a
reflection structure
for
E
is equivariance data for the internal natural transformation
E
in the sense of Definition
C.24
. Isomorphisms between twisted representations with reflection structures are isomorphisms between internal natural transformations
E\simeq E^{\prime}
that are compatible with the equivariance data
\sigma
.

Example C.26
.

Building on Example
C.14
of an ordinary manifold
M
regarded as an internal category, a reflection structure for a twist is a real structure on a bundle of algebras
A
over
M
. A reflection structure for a twisted representation
E
is a real structure on
E
for which the
A
-action is real.

Example C.27
.

Building on Example
C.15
of a super Lie group
G
regarded as an internal category, a reflection structure for a twist determined by
A
is a real structure on
A
and a compatible real structure on the bundle of
A-{\mathbb{C}}
bimodules over
G
. A reflection structure for a twisted representation
E
is a real structure on
E
compatible with the real structure on
A
and the action by
G
. When
A={\mathbb{C}}
, the real structure for the twist is the same data as a
{\mathbb{Z}}/2
-extension of
G
, and a real structure on
E
is the same data as a real projective representation.

C.4.
Twisted geometric field theories and reflection structures

See §
4.1
for a review of rigid geometries.

Definition C.28

(Sketch of
[
ST11
, Definitions 2.21, 2.46, end of 2.48, and 4.4]
)
.

Given a rigid geometry
(G,{\mathbb{M}})
with involution, let
(G,{\mathbb{M}})\hbox{-{\sf Bord}}(M)
denote Stolz and Teichner’s
(G,{\mathbb{M}})
-bordism category over
M
as a category internal to symmetric monoidal stacks with involution. Hence,
(G,{\mathbb{M}})\hbox{-{\sf Bord}}(M)
includes the data of symmetric monoidal stacks with involution

(509)

\displaystyle{\rm Ob}((G,{\mathbb{M}})\hbox{-{\sf Bord}}(M)),\qquad{\rm Mor}((G,{\mathbb{M}})\hbox{-{\sf Bord}}(M))

as well as source, target, unit and compositions functors between symmetric monoidal stacks with involution

\displaystyle\begin{array}[]{c}{\sf s},{\sf t}\colon{\rm Mor}((G,{\mathbb{M}})\hbox{-{\sf Bord}}(M))\to{\rm Ob}((G,{\mathbb{M}})\hbox{-{\sf Bord}}(M)),\\
{\sf u}\colon{\rm Ob}((G,{\mathbb{M}})\hbox{-{\sf Bord}}(M))\to{\rm Mor}((G,{\mathbb{M}})\hbox{-{\sf Bord}}(M)),\\
{\sf c}\colon{\rm Mor}((G,{\mathbb{M}})\hbox{-{\sf Bord}}(M))^{[2]}\to{\rm Mor}((G,{\mathbb{M}})\hbox{-{\sf Bord}}(M)).\end{array}

There is associator and unitor data that satisfy further compatibility properties.

Above and throughout, we use the notation

{\rm Mor}((G,{\mathbb{M}})\hbox{-{\sf Bord}}(M))^{[k]}:=\underbrace{{\rm Mor}((G,{\mathbb{M}})\hbox{-{\sf Bord}}(M))\times_{{\rm Ob}((G,{\mathbb{M}})\hbox{-{\sf Bord}}(M))}\cdots\times_{{\rm Ob}((G,{\mathbb{M}})\hbox{-{\sf Bord}}(M))}{\rm Mor}((G,{\mathbb{M}})\hbox{-{\sf Bord}}(M))}_{k-{\rm fold\ fibered\ product}}

to denote the
k
-fold fibered product of
{\rm Mor}((G,{\mathbb{M}})\hbox{-{\sf Bord}}(M))
over
{\rm Ob}((G,{\mathbb{M}})\hbox{-{\sf Bord}}(M))
with respect to the maps
{\sf s}
and
{\sf t}
; hence,
S
-points of
{\rm Mor}((G,{\mathbb{M}})\hbox{-{\sf Bord}}(M))^{[d]}
are
k
-tuples of composable bordisms.

Remark C.29
.

In
[
BE23b
, Appendix B.3]
, it is shown that the source and target functor (
C.28
) in the construction of
(G,{\mathbb{M}})\hbox{-{\sf Bord}}(M)
are fibrations of stacks, and hence the strict fibered product agrees with the weak fibered product of stacks. We use this fact throughout when considering compositions of bordisms.

We sketch the construction of
(G,{\mathbb{M}})\hbox{-{\sf Bord}}(M)
as an internal category. The stack
{\rm Ob}((G,{\mathbb{M}})\hbox{-{\sf Bord}}(M))
has objects specified by data: (i) a
(G,{\mathbb{M}})
-pair
(Y^{c},Y)
over
S
, (ii) a map
\Phi\colon Y\to M
, and (iii) a partition of
Y\setminus Y^{c}
:

(514)

\displaystyle(Y^{c}\subset Y),\qquad Y\xrightarrow{\Phi}M,\qquad Y\setminus Y^{c}\simeq Y^{-}\coprod Y^{+}.

The
S
-family
Y\to S
is a
collar
and the partition specifies an
incoming collar

Y^{-}
and an
outgoing collar

Y^{+}
. The
core

Y^{c}\to S
is required to be proper over
S
. These objects admit an involution determined by the involution on the
(G,{\mathbb{M}})
-geometry, and a monoidal structure from disjoint union of supermanifolds. Isomorphisms in
{\rm Ob}((G,{\mathbb{M}})\hbox{-{\sf Bord}}(M)
are spans of
(G,{\mathbb{M}})
-isometric embeddings
Y\hookleftarrow W\hookrightarrow Y^{\prime}
satisfying compatibility properties with
Y^{c}
and
Y^{\pm}
, up to an equivalence relation that allows for shrinking of collars.

Similarly, an object of the stack
{\rm Mor}((G,{\mathbb{M}})\hbox{-{\sf Bord}}(M))
is specified by data

(515)

\displaystyle Y^{c}_{\sf in}\subset Y_{\sf in}\hookrightarrow\Sigma\hookleftarrow Y_{\sf out}\supset Y^{c}_{\sf out},\qquad\Phi\colon\Sigma\to M,

where
(Y^{c}_{{\sf in}/{\sf out}},Y_{{\sf in}/{\sf out}})
are
(G,{\mathbb{M}})
-pairs associated with the source and target of the bordism. There is an analogously defined core
\Sigma^{c}:=\Sigma\setminus(i_{\rm in}(Y_{\rm in}^{-})\cup i_{\rm out}(Y_{\rm out}^{+}))
that is required to be proper over
S
. Isomorphisms in
{\rm Mor}((G,{\mathbb{M}})\hbox{-{\sf Bord}}(M))
are again specified by spans of isometric embeddings
\Sigma\hookleftarrow W\hookrightarrow\Sigma^{\prime}
with compatibility properties subject to an equivalence relation that allows for shrinking of collars. We note that the complete definition replaces the inclusions in (
515
) with spans, which is essential for the fibration property described in Remark
C.29
. We will only require bordisms of the form (
515
) in this paper.

Definition C.30

(
[
ST11
, Definition 5.2]
)
.

A
twisted geometric field theory
is a twisted representation of
(G,{\mathbb{M}})\hbox{-{\sf Bord}}(M)
, i.e., an internal natural transformation
E

(516)

(G,{\mathbb{M}})\hbox{-{\sf Bord}}(M)
{\sf{TA}}
E\Downarrow
\mathbbold{1}
\mathscr{T}

where
\mathscr{T}
is a
twist functor
and
\mathbbold{1}
is (
504
). Twisted field theories are the objects of a groupoid whose morphisms are isomorphisms between internal natural transformations.

Reflection structures for field theories are special cases of Definitions
C.21
and
C.24
.

Definition C.31
.

Given a reflection
\dagger
of
(G,{\mathbb{M}})\hbox{-{\sf Bord}}(M)
, a
reflection structure
for a twist and
reflection structure
for a twisted field theory is given by Definition
C.25
.

Lemma C.32
.

A reflection structure on a geometric bordism category
(G,{\mathbb{M}})\hbox{-{\sf Bord}}(M)
is determined by a map
\dagger\colon{\mathbb{M}}\to\overline{{\mathbb{M}}}
and a homomorphism
\alpha\colon G\to\overline{G}
satisfying

(517)

\displaystyle\overline{\dagger}\circ\dagger={{\rm id}}_{\mathbb{M}},\qquad\dagger|_{{\mathbb{M}}^{c}}=\overline{{\mathbb{M}}}^{c}\subset\overline{{\mathbb{M}}},\qquad\overline{\alpha}\circ\alpha={{\rm id}}_{G}.

Proof.

The statement follows from the argument in
[
HST10
, Lemma 6.19]
. Given an
S
-family of
(G,{\mathbb{M}})
-supermanifolds
(Y\to S)
, the map (
517
) gives a unique
(G,{\mathbb{M}})
-stucture for the
\overline{S}
-family
(\overline{Y}\to\overline{S})
. This extends to an internal functor

(518)

\displaystyle(G,{\mathbb{M}})\hbox{-{\sf Bord}}(M)\to(G,{\mathbb{M}})\hbox{-{\sf Bord}}(M),\qquad(S\leftarrow Y\to M)\mapsto(\overline{S}\leftarrow\overline{Y}\to\overline{M}\simeq M).

The identification
\overline{M}\simeq M
uses that
M
is an ordinary manifold, and hence has a canonical real structure when regarded as a supermanifold. Via the forgetful functor
(G,{\mathbb{M}})\hbox{-{\sf Bord}}(M)\to{\sf SMfld}
that sends an
S
-family of bordisms to
S
, the internal functor (
518
) covers the conjugation functor on supermanifolds.
∎

The assignment
M\mapsto(G,{\mathbb{M}})\hbox{-{\sf Bord}}(M)
is natural in
M
: a smooth map
f\colon M^{\prime}\to M
induces an internal functor

(519)

\displaystyle f_{*}\colon(G,{\mathbb{M}})\hbox{-{\sf Bord}}(M^{\prime})\to(G,{\mathbb{M}})\hbox{-{\sf Bord}}(M)

by precomposing the map
\Phi
in (
515
) with
f
. This implies that twisted geometric field theories (with reflection structures) determine a contravariant functor from manifolds to groupoids.

We will primarily be interested in the
2|1
-dimensional Euclidean bordism category over
M
, denoted
2|1\hbox{-}{\sf EBord}(M)
, defined as the geometric bordism category for the geometry
({\mathbb{E}}^{2|(0,1)}\rtimes{\rm Spin}(2),{\mathbb{R}}^{2|1},{\mathbb{R}}^{1|1})
from Definition
4.14
with involution from the spin flip, see Lemma
4.9
. We describe some structures on the
2|1
-Euclidean bordism category and the affect of these structure on twisted field theories.

Lemma C.33
.

For
\mu\in{\mathbb{R}}_{>0}
, the internal category
2|1\hbox{-}{\sf EBord}(M)
admits an internal endofunctor
{\sf RG}_{\mu}\colon 2|1\hbox{-}{\sf EBord}(M)\to 2|1\hbox{-}{\sf EBord}(M)
that dilates a super Euclidean bordism by
\mu
. Furthermore, for
\mu,\mu^{\prime}\in{\mathbb{R}}_{>0}
there are internal natural isomorphisms
{\sf RG}_{\mu}\circ{\sf RG}_{\mu^{\prime}}\simeq{\sf RG}_{\mu\mu^{\prime}}
.

Sketch of proof.

The functors
{\sf RG}_{\mu}
are inherited from the endofunctors on
2|1
-Euclidean supermanifolds given by Lemma
4.11
. This determines maps on the object and morphisms stacks of
2|1\hbox{-}{\sf EBord}(M)
, and it remains to supply the 2-morphisms in (
502
) and verify their compatibilities.
∎

Definition C.34
.

The
renormalization group flow
is the (weak)
{\mathbb{R}}_{>0}
-action on twisted
2|1
-Euclidean field theories over
M
gotten from pulling back internal natural transformations (
516
) along the endofunctors of the
2|1
-Euclidean bordism category in Lemma
C.33
.

Lemma C.35
.

The internal category
2|1\hbox{-}{\sf EBord}(M)
admits a reflection

(520)

\displaystyle\dagger\colon 2|1\hbox{-}{\sf EBord}(M)\to 2|1\hbox{-}{\sf EBord}(M),\qquad\dagger\circ\dagger\simeq{{\rm id}}.

Proof.

The involution of
2|1\hbox{-}{\sf EBord}(M)
is inherited from the involution
2|1
-Euclidean supermanifolds in Definition
4.19
.
∎

C.5.
The degree
n
twist on the Euclidean bordism category

We review the
free fermion twist
(also called the
degree twist
) from
[
ST11
, §5-6]
, which is a particular collection of
\otimes
-invertible internal internal functors

(521)

\displaystyle{\rm Fer}_{n}\colon 2\hbox{-}{\sf EBord}\to{\sf{TA}},\qquad{\rm Fer}_{n}\otimes{\rm Fer}_{n}\simeq{\rm Fer}_{n+m}

indexed by
n\in{\mathbb{Z}}
together with the indicated internal natural isomorphisms. Above,
2\hbox{-}{\sf EBord}=2\hbox{-}{\sf EBord}({\rm pt})
is the
2-dimensional Euclidean bordism category
(over
M={\rm pt}
), i.e., the geometric bordism category associated to the 2-dimensional Euclidean geometry defined in §
4.2
. The morphisms in this bordism category are (roughly) flat 2-manifolds with spin structure and geodesic boundary.

Remark C.36
.

Free fermions haven been studied extensively in the mathematical literature in a variety of languages, e.g., see
[
Was98
,
Seg04
,
ST04
,
DH11
]
.

We begin by reviewing some families of Euclidean bordisms from
[
ST11
, §3.2]
. There are maps of stacks
[
ST11
, page 34]

{\sf S}^{\pm}\colon{\mathbb{R}}_{>0}\to{\rm Ob}(2\hbox{-}{\sf EBord}),\qquad\ell\mapsto{\rm S}_{\ell}^{\pm}

sending
\ell\in{\mathbb{R}}_{>0}
to a circumference
\ell
Euclidean circle with periodic spin structure, and choice of collar (indicated by the
\pm
) analogous to (
6.3
). In analogy to (
6.7
) and (
6.9
), there are maps of stacks
[
ST11
, page 34-35]

\displaystyle{\sf C}^{\pm}\colon({\mathbb{R}}_{>0}\times{\mathbb{H}}_{\pm})/{\mathbb{Z}}\to{\rm Mor}(2\hbox{-}{\sf EBord}),

\displaystyle(\ell,\tau)\mapsto{\rm C}_{\ell,\tau}^{\pm}

(522)

\displaystyle{\sf L}^{\pm}\colon({\mathbb{R}}_{>0}\times{\mathbb{H}}_{\pm})/{\mathbb{Z}}\to{\rm Mor}(2\hbox{-}{\sf EBord}),

\displaystyle(\ell,\tau)\mapsto{\rm L}_{\ell,\tau}^{\pm}

\displaystyle{\sf R}^{\pm}\colon({\mathbb{R}}_{>0}\times{\mathring{{\mathbb{H}}}}_{\pm})/{\mathbb{Z}}\to{\rm Mor}(2\hbox{-}{\sf EBord}),

\displaystyle(\ell,\tau)\mapsto{\rm R}_{\ell,\tau}^{\pm}

classifying families of bordisms whose cores are flat cylinders of diameter
\ell
and height
{\rm im}(\tau)
. The distinction in the 3 functors (
522
) comes from the choice of source and target data. Composition among the families (
522
) is determined by addition in
{\mathbb{H}}_{\pm}
; we refer to the pictures (
275
), (
280
), and (
286
) for depictions of some of these compositions. Formulas can be recovered from (
268
), (
276
), (
281
), and (
287
). For example, concatenation of Euclidean cylinders yields
[
ST11
, Proposition 3.5]
, see (
275
)

{\rm C}_{\ell,\tau^{\prime}}^{\pm}\circ{\rm C}_{\ell,\tau}^{\pm}\simeq{\rm C}_{\ell,\tau^{\prime}+\tau}^{\pm}.

Following
[
ST11
, §5.3]
, we will describe the values of
{\rm Fer}_{-1}
in (
521
) on the bordisms (
522
) and their compositions.
Define algebra bundles
{\rm Fer}({\sf S}^{\pm})\to{\mathbb{R}}_{>0}
whose fiber at
{\rm S}^{\pm}_{\ell}
is
[
ST11
, Equation 6.1]

(523)

\displaystyle{\rm Fer}_{\ell}^{\pm}:={\rm Fer}({\rm S}_{\ell}^{\pm})={\mathbb{C}}{\rm l}_{\pm 1}\otimes\bigotimes_{m\in{\mathbb{N}}}{\mathbb{C}}{\rm l}({\mathbb{C}}_{\ell,\pm m}\oplus{\mathbb{C}}_{\ell,\mp m}),

for the following definitions of the above factors. For
m\in{\mathbb{R}}
, let
{\mathbb{C}}_{\ell,m}
denote the 1-dimensional representation of
{\mathbb{R}}
with action by
e^{2\pi imt/\ell}
for
t\in{\mathbb{R}}
. The Clifford algebras
{\mathbb{C}}{\rm l}({\mathbb{C}}_{\ell,m}\oplus{\mathbb{C}}_{\ell,-m})
are determined by the
{\mathbb{R}}
-invariant (hyperbolic) pairing

(524)

\displaystyle\omega\colon{\mathbb{C}}_{\ell,m}\oplus{\mathbb{C}}_{\ell,-m}\to{\mathbb{C}},\qquad\omega((v_{m},v_{-m}),(w_{m},w_{-m}))=v_{m}w_{-m}+v_{-m}w_{m}.

Finally, the infinite tensor product in (
523
) is the
restricted
tensor product,
i.e., the closure of finite sums of tensor products
\otimes_{m}v_{m}
where
v_{m}=1
for all but finitely many
m
. Throughout we will use the canonical isomorphism

({\rm Fer}_{\ell}^{\pm})^{\sf{op}}={\rm Fer}_{\ell}^{\mp}

whose map on underlying sets is the identity.

Next construct an
{\mathbb{H}}_{\pm}
-action on the algebras (
523
), following
[
ST11
, page 59]
. Let
\tau\in{\mathbb{H}}_{\pm}
act by
e^{2\pi im\tau/\ell}
on
{\mathbb{C}}_{\ell,m}
. When applied diagonally to
{\mathbb{C}}_{\ell,m}\oplus{\mathbb{C}}_{\ell,-m}
, this
{\mathbb{H}}_{\pm}
-action preserves the pairing (
524
), and so determines an automorphism

(525)

\displaystyle\varphi_{\ell,m}^{\pm}(\tau)\colon{\mathbb{C}}{\rm l}({\mathbb{C}}_{\ell,m}\oplus{\mathbb{C}}_{\ell,-m})\to{\mathbb{C}}{\rm l}({\mathbb{C}}_{\ell,m}\oplus{\mathbb{C}}_{\ell,-m}),\qquad\tau\in{\mathbb{H}}_{\pm}

extending the
{\mathbb{R}}
-action on (
523
). Varying
m
, these automorphisms assemble into an
{\mathbb{H}}_{\pm}
-family of automorphisms
\varphi^{\pm}_{\ell}(\tau)\colon{\rm Fer}_{\ell}^{\pm}\to{\rm Fer}_{\ell}^{\pm}
. Define bundles of bimodules over
{\mathbb{R}}_{>0}\times{\mathbb{H}}_{\pm}
whose fibers are denoted
{}_{{}^{\tau}{\rm Fer}_{\ell}^{\pm}}({\rm Fer}_{\ell}^{\pm})_{{\rm Fer}_{\ell}^{\pm}}
and defined by taking
{\rm Fer}_{\ell}^{\pm}
as a bimodule over itself where the left action is twisted by the automorphism
\varphi^{\pm}_{\ell}(\tau)
. Since the algebra maps
\varphi_{\ell,m}(\tau+\ell)=\varphi_{\ell,m}(\tau)
are equal, this bundle of bimodules descends to the quotients
({\mathbb{R}}_{>0}\times{\mathbb{H}}_{\pm})/{\mathbb{Z}}
. The twist (
521
) assigns the bimodule bundles over the families (
522
) whose fibers at
(\ell,\tau)
are

\displaystyle{\rm Fer}_{-1}({\sf C}^{\pm})_{\ell,\tau}={\rm Fer}_{-1}({\rm C}_{\ell,\tau}^{\pm})

\displaystyle=

{}_{{}^{\tau}{\rm Fer}_{\ell}^{\mp}}({\rm Fer}_{\ell}^{\mp})_{{\rm Fer}_{\ell}^{\mp}}

(526)

\displaystyle{\rm Fer}_{-1}({\sf L}^{\pm})_{\ell,\tau}={\rm Fer}_{-1}({\rm L}_{\ell,\tau}^{\pm})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle({\rm Fer}_{\ell}^{\mp})_{{\rm Fer}_{\ell}^{\mp}\otimes^{\tau}{\rm Fer}_{\ell}^{\pm}}

\displaystyle{\rm Fer}_{-1}({\sf R}^{\pm})_{\ell,\tau}={\rm Fer}_{-1}({\rm R}_{\ell,\tau}^{\pm})

\displaystyle=

{}_{{\rm Fer}^{\pm}_{\ell}\otimes{}^{\tau}{\rm Fer}_{\ell}^{\mp}}{\rm Fer}_{\ell}^{\mp}.

The value of (
521
) on the moduli of Euclidean tori is the Pfaffian line bundle
[
ST11
, page 56]

(527)

\displaystyle{\rm Fer}_{-1}({\sf Lat}/\!\!/({\mathbb{E}}^{2}\rtimes{\rm Spin}(2)\times{\rm SL}_{2}({\mathbb{Z}})))={\rm Pf},

which we shall regard as an
{\mathbb{E}}^{2}\rtimes{\rm Spin}(2)\times{\rm SL}_{2}({\mathbb{Z}})
-equivariant structure on the trivial bundle on lattices
{\sf Lat}
; we refer to §
A
for a review of this line bundle.

Remark C.37
.

The values on the antiperiodic versions of the Euclidean cylinders (
522
) follows a similar discussion as above, but starting with the bundle of algebras over
{\mathbb{R}}_{>0}\coprod{\mathbb{R}}_{>0}
whose fiber at
(\ell,\pm)
is
[
ST11
, Equation 6.1]

(528)

\displaystyle\bigotimes_{m\in{\mathbb{N}}_{0}+1/2}{\mathbb{C}}{\rm l}({\mathbb{C}}_{\ell,\pm m}\oplus{\mathbb{C}}_{\ell,\mp m}).

The value of
{\rm Fer}_{-1}
on the other components (
154
) of the moduli stack of spin tori is again the Pfaffian line, which on these components is canonically trivialized by the Pfaffian section (the Dirac operator is invertible for the even spin structure).

A priori, an internal functor (
521
) requires additional data for compositions and units. Most of this is supplied from the fact the compositions of the bimodules (
526
) corresponds to composition of the algebra maps
\varphi^{\pm}_{\ell}(\tau)
, e.g., there are canonical bimodule maps

(529)

\displaystyle{\rm Fer}_{-1}({\rm C}_{\ell,\tau^{\prime}})\circ{\rm Fer}_{-1}({\rm C}_{\ell,\tau})={\rm Fer}_{-1}({\rm C}_{\ell,\tau^{\prime}})\otimes_{{}^{\tau}{\rm Fer}_{\ell}^{\mp}}{\rm Fer}_{-1}({\rm C}_{\ell,\tau})\to{\rm Fer}_{-1}({\rm C}_{\ell,\tau^{\prime}+\tau})

since
\varphi_{\ell}^{\pm}(\tau^{\prime})\circ\varphi^{\pm}_{\ell}(\tau)=\varphi_{\ell}^{\pm}(\tau^{\prime}+\tau).
The remaining data to be specified corresponds to compositions that glue cylinders into tori
[
ST11
, Equation 3.11]
(see the picture (
293
)). For such a composition the twist assigns the data of an isomorphism of line bundles over
{\mathbb{R}}_{>0}\times{\mathring{{\mathbb{H}}}}_{\pm}

(530)

\displaystyle{\rm Fer}_{-1}({\sf L}^{\mp})_{\ell,\tau}\circ{\rm Fer}_{-1}({\sf R}^{\pm})_{\ell,\tau}\simeq{\rm Fer}^{\pm}_{\ell}\otimes_{{\rm Fer}^{\pm}_{\ell}\otimes{}^{\tau}{\rm Fer}_{\ell}^{\mp}}{\rm Fer}_{\ell}^{\mp}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle{\rm data}}}{{\simeq}}{\rm Pf}|_{(\ell,\tau)}

between the indicated tensor product and the restriction of the Pfaffian along the inclusion
{\mathbb{R}}_{>0}\times{\mathring{{\mathbb{H}}}}_{+}\hookrightarrow{\sf Lat}
. The above is essentially a choice of trace on the bimodules
{}^{\tau}{\rm Fer}_{\ell}^{\pm}
, which we characterize in Lemma
C.40
below.

We obtain further structure on the algebras (
523
) from evaluating the twist on isometries, e.g., the isometry
{\rm S}_{\ell}^{\pm}\simeq{\rm S}_{\ell}^{\mp}
determined by the action of
\sqrt{-1}\in U(1)\simeq{\rm Spin}(2)
(analogous to Lemma
6.22
) gives an isomorphism

(531)

\displaystyle{\mathbb{C}}{\rm l}_{\mp 1}\otimes\bigotimes_{m\in{\mathbb{N}}}{\mathbb{C}}{\rm l}({\mathbb{C}}_{\ell,\mp m}\oplus{\mathbb{C}}_{\ell,\pm m})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle{\rm data}}}{{\simeq}}{\mathbb{C}}{\rm l}_{\pm 1}\otimes\bigotimes_{m\in{\mathbb{N}}}{\mathbb{C}}{\rm l}({\mathbb{C}}_{\ell,\pm m}\oplus{\mathbb{C}}_{\ell,\mp m}),

that squares to the parity involution. The map (
531
) is unique up to sign, given by

(532)

\displaystyle{\mathbb{C}}{\rm l}_{\mp 1}\to{\mathbb{C}}{\rm l}_{\pm 1},\quad v\mapsto\pm iv,\quad v\in{\mathbb{C}}

{\mathbb{C}}{\rm l}({\mathbb{C}}_{\ell,\mp m}\oplus{\mathbb{C}}_{\ell,\pm m})\to{\mathbb{C}}{\rm l}({\mathbb{C}}_{\ell,\pm m}\oplus{\mathbb{C}}_{\ell,\mp m}),\qquad(v,w)\mapsto(iw,iv),\quad(v,w)\in{\mathbb{C}}_{m}\oplus{\mathbb{C}}_{-m}).

Similarly, a reflection structure on
{\rm Fer}_{-1}
is determined by the conjugate-linear involutive isomorphism of algebras,

\displaystyle{\mathbb{C}}{\rm l}_{\mp 1}

\displaystyle\to

\displaystyle\overline{{\mathbb{C}}{\rm l}}_{\pm 1},\quad v\mapsto\pm i\overline{v},\quad v\in{\mathbb{C}}

(533)

\displaystyle{\mathbb{C}}{\rm l}({\mathbb{C}}_{\ell,\mp m}\oplus{\mathbb{C}}_{\ell,\pm m})

\displaystyle\to

\displaystyle\overline{{\mathbb{C}}{\rm l}({\mathbb{C}}_{\ell,\pm m}\oplus{\mathbb{C}}_{\ell,\mp m})}\simeq{\mathbb{C}}{\rm l}(\overline{{\mathbb{C}}}_{\ell,\mp m}\oplus\overline{{\mathbb{C}}}_{\ell,\pm m})

\displaystyle(v,w)

\displaystyle\mapsto

\displaystyle(\pm i\overline{w},\mp i\overline{v}),\quad(v,w)\in{\mathbb{C}}_{\mp m}\oplus{\mathbb{C}}_{\pm m})

i.e., the
{\mathbb{Z}}/2
-graded version of the
*
-structure (
544
) on the Clifford algebras. The above determines conjugate-linear maps between the bimodules (
526
), and so determines the data gotten by restriction of a reflection structure, compare Lemma
7.8
.

Remark C.38
.

The degree
n
twist is defined as a functor out of the Euclidean bordism category in
[
ST11
, page 56]
, and then described as a functor out of the “conformal Euclidean” bordism category in
[
ST11
, §6]
. We indicate the translation between these descriptions. To begin, the conformal Euclidean bordism category includes the renormalization group action as an isometry, i.e., the conformal Euclidean geometry is
({\mathbb{E}}^{2}\rtimes{\mathbb{C}}^{\times},{\mathbb{R}}^{2},{\mathbb{R}})
under the identification
{\rm Spin}(2)\times{\mathbb{R}}_{>0}\simeq{\mathbb{C}}^{\times}
. Decomposing into Fourier modes gives an
{\mathbb{R}}
-equivariant algebra isomorphism between (
523
) and
the Clifford algebras on the spaces of (even) sections of the spinor bundle over the periodic circle (
68
).
This recovers the values of Stolz and Teichner’s Euclidean twist on objects from
[
ST11
, page 56]
. We remark that (
528
) is the version of (
68
) where the spinor bundle is for the anti-periodic spin structure. The algebra bundles (
523
) are isomorphic to trivial bundles with fiber
{\rm Fer}_{-1}({\rm S}^{\pm}_{1})
(i.e., the fiber at
\ell=1
); on the negative circle
{\rm S}^{-}_{\ell}
this recovers the conformal Euclidean twist
[
ST11
, Equation 6.1]
. The trivialization is
{\mathbb{H}}_{\pm}
-equivariant relative to homomorphisms between semigroups at each fiber. This sketches the data of a 2-commutative triangle

2\hbox{-}{\sf EBord}
{\sf{TA}}.
\Downarrow
2\hbox{-}{\sf CEBord}({\rm pt})
forget
{\rm Fer}_{-1}
{\rm c}{\rm Fer}_{-1}

where
{\rm Fer}_{-1}
is (
521
) defined in
[
ST11
, page 56]
, whereas
{\rm c}{\rm Fer}_{-1}
is the conformal version of this functor from
[
ST11
, §6]
, and the 2-commuting data is essentially determined by the
{\mathbb{H}}_{\pm}
-equivariant isomorphism of algebra bundles over
{\mathbb{R}}_{>0}\coprod{\mathbb{R}}_{>0}
. Said differently, the degree twist in dimension 2 is RG-invariant, which in this framework demands the additional (descent) data in the 2-commuting diagram above.

We recall the internal
\mathscr{C}=\mathscr{C}({\rm pt})
of Euclidean cylinders from Definition
5.4
.

Lemma C.39

(
[
ST11
, Equation 6.2]
)
.

Consider the restriction of
{\rm Fer}_{n}
to the subcategory
\mathscr{C}\hookrightarrow 2\hbox{-}{\sf EBord}
of Euclidean cylinders. There is an internal natural isomorphism

(534)

\mathscr{C}
{\sf{TA}}
\mathcal{F}\Downarrow
{\rm Cl}_{n}
{\rm Fer}_{n}

from the internal functor
{\rm Cl}_{n}
in Definition
7.17
. Furthermore, the internal natural isomorphism
\mathcal{F}
has a reflection structure relative to the reflection structures on the internal functors determined by the conjugate linear anti-involution (
533
) of
{\rm Fer}_{n}
, and the
*
-superalgebra structure

(535)

\displaystyle{\mathbb{C}}{\rm l}_{\pm n}

\displaystyle\to

\displaystyle\overline{{\mathbb{C}}{\rm l}}_{\mp n},\quad v\mapsto\mp i\overline{v},\quad v\in{\mathbb{C}}^{n}.

Proof.

To construct an internal natural isomorphism
\mathcal{F}
in (
534
) it suffices to consider the case
n=-1
. The basic ingredient is a bundle of invertible bimodules over
{\mathbb{R}}_{>0}\coprod{\mathbb{R}}_{>0}
with fibers
[
ST11
, Equation 6.2]
,

(536)

\displaystyle{}_{{\rm Fer}_{\ell}^{\mp}}({\mathbb{C}}{\rm l}_{\mp 1}\bigotimes_{m>0}\Lambda^{\bullet}{\mathbb{C}}_{\ell,\pm m})_{{\mathbb{C}}{\rm l}_{\mp 1}}

where
{\mathbb{C}}{\rm l}_{\pm 1}
is regarded as a bimodule over itself, and
\Lambda^{\bullet}{\mathbb{C}}_{\ell,\pm m}
is a left
{\mathbb{C}}{\rm l}({\mathbb{C}}_{\ell,\pm m}\oplus{\mathbb{C}}_{\ell,\mp m})
-module for the action determined by the Lagrangian subspace
{\mathbb{C}}_{\ell,\pm m}\subset{\mathbb{C}}_{\ell,\pm m}\oplus{\mathbb{C}}_{\ell,\mp m}
.
4
4

4

For example, see
[
ST04
, Definition 2.2.4]
for Clifford modules constructed out of Lagrangian subspaces.
The bundle (
536
) provides part of the data of a map of stacks
{\rm Ob}(\mathscr{C})\to{\rm Mor}({\sf{TA}})^{\times}
; the remaining data is a
{\mathbb{Z}}/4
-equivariant structure determined by the action of
\pm i
on
{\mathbb{C}}_{\ell,\pm m}
and the map
{\mathbb{C}}{\rm l}_{\pm 1}\to{\mathbb{C}}{\rm l}_{\mp 1}
in (
532
). This furnishes the first part of the data of the internal natural isomorphism (
534
). The remaining data are
{\mathbb{Z}}/4
-equivariant isomorphisms of bimodules over
({\mathbb{R}}_{>0}\times{\mathbb{H}}_{\pm})/{\mathbb{Z}}

(537)

{}_{{}^{\tau}{\rm Fer}_{\ell}^{\mp}}({\rm Fer}_{\ell}^{\mp})\otimes_{{\rm Fer}_{\ell}^{\mp}}({\mathbb{C}}{\rm l}_{\mp 1}\bigotimes_{m>0}\Lambda^{\bullet}{\mathbb{C}}_{\ell,\pm m})_{{\mathbb{C}}{\rm l}_{\mp 1}}\to{}_{{\rm Fer}_{\ell}^{\mp}}({\mathbb{C}}{\rm l}_{\mp 1}\bigotimes_{m>0}\Lambda^{\bullet}{\mathbb{C}}_{\ell,\pm m})\otimes_{{\mathbb{C}}{\rm l}_{\mp 1}}{{\mathbb{C}}{\rm l}_{\mp 1}}.

Identifying the source with the bimodule (
536
) but with left action twisted by
\varphi^{\pm}_{\ell}(\tau)
, the map (
537
) is given by

(538)

\displaystyle{\mathbb{C}}_{\ell,\pm m}\to{\mathbb{C}}_{\ell,\pm m},\qquad v\mapsto e^{\pm 2\pi i\tau m/\ell}v.

This completes the construction of the natural isomorphism (
534
). In this case, the reflection structure on
\mathcal{F}
is a property, corresponding to the compatibility of the anti-linear anti-involutions (
533
) and (
535
).
∎

Finally, we characterize the isomorphism (
530
) specified in Stolz and Teichner’s construction; this map is implicit in
[
ST04
, Lemma 2.3.14]
and
[
ST11
, Proof of Theorem 1.16]
, though the presentation there is somewhat terse. For our purposes, one can take the following as a definition rather than a lemma.
Up to a constant phase that can be absorbed into the trivialization of
{\rm Pf}
, the following is also consistent with conventions in the physics literature, e.g., see
[
GJF19
, page 6]
, as well as
[
ST04
, Equation 3.3.10]
.

Lemma C.40

(
[
ST11
, §6]
)
.

There is a commutative triangle of line bundles over
{\mathbb{R}}_{>0}\times{\mathring{{\mathbb{H}}}}_{+}

(539)

{\rm Fer}^{\mp}_{\ell}\otimes_{{\rm Fer}^{\mp}_{\ell}\otimes{}^{\tau}{\rm Fer}_{\ell}^{\pm}}{\rm Fer}_{\ell}^{\pm}
\underline{{\mathbb{C}}}\simeq{\rm Pf}|_{{\mathbb{R}}_{>0}\times{\mathring{{\mathbb{H}}}}_{+}}
{\mathbb{C}}{\rm l}_{\mp 1}\otimes_{{\mathbb{C}}{\rm l}_{\mp 1}\otimes{\mathbb{C}}{\rm l}_{\pm 1}}{\mathbb{C}}{\rm l}_{\pm 1}
\simeq
\simeq

where the upper isomorphism is the data (
530
) coming from the internal functor
{\rm Fer}
, and the trivialization of the Pfaffian over
{\mathbb{R}}_{>0}\times{\mathring{{\mathbb{H}}}}_{+}\hookrightarrow{\sf Lat}
is the canonical one (from restricting the trivial bundle on
{\sf Lat}
). Then the dashed arrow is given by the map on sections

\displaystyle C^{\infty}({\mathbb{R}}_{>0}\times{\mathring{{\mathbb{H}}}}_{+};{\mathbb{C}}{\rm l}_{-1}/[{\mathbb{C}}{\rm l}_{-1},{\mathbb{C}}{\rm l}_{-1}])

\displaystyle\to

\displaystyle C^{\infty}({\mathbb{R}}_{>0}\times{\mathring{{\mathbb{H}}}}_{+})

(540)

\displaystyle\Gamma

\displaystyle\mapsto

\displaystyle\ell^{1/2}\prod_{m=1}^{\infty}(1-e^{2\pi im\tau/\ell})

where
\Gamma=\Gamma_{1}\in{\mathbb{C}}{\rm l}_{1}
as defined in (
D.1
).

Sketch of proof.

The invertible bimodule (
536
) supplies the vertical isomorphism in (
539
). The upper isomorphism in (
539
) is a fiberwise trace on the bimodules
{}^{\tau}{\rm Fer}_{\ell}^{\pm}
. This is determined by a trace on the Clifford algebras modified by the character of the invertible bimodule (
536
). Since the character of each factor
\Lambda^{\bullet}{\mathbb{C}}_{\ell,m}
is
1-e^{2\pi i\tau m/\ell}
, the total character is the claimed infinite product. The factor of
\ell^{1/2}
comes from the fact that the zero modes in (
68
) are the Clifford algebra associated with
\ell
times the standard metric; see the description (
68
). This Clifford algebra is isomorphic to
{\mathbb{C}}{\rm l}_{\mp 1}
, but the natural Clifford supertrace differs by a factor of
\ell^{1/2}
. Indeed, by
[
BGV92
, Proposition 3.2.1]
a Clifford supertrace is determined by a (metric) trivialization of
\Lambda^{\rm top}{\mathbb{R}}^{n}
, which introduces the factor of
\ell^{1/2}
.
∎

Appendix D
The families index in KO-theory

D.1.
Clifford algebras, spin structures, and Dirac operators

Define the (complex) Clifford algebras

\displaystyle{\mathbb{C}}{\rm l}_{n}:=\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}\langle f_{1},\dots f_{n}\mid[f_{j},f_{k}]=-2\delta_{jk}\rangle&n\geq 0\\
\langle e_{1},\dots e_{n}\mid[e_{j},e_{k}]=2\delta_{jk}\rangle&n<0\end{array}\right.

where
\{e_{1},\dots,e_{n}\}
or
\{f_{1},\dots,f_{n}\}
denotes the standard basis of
{\mathbb{R}}^{n}
. These Clifford algebras have an evident real structure and also a
*
-structure determined by

(544)

\displaystyle f_{j}^{\dagger}=-f_{j},\qquad e_{j}^{\dagger}=e_{j}.

Define the element
\Gamma_{n}
of the
n^{\rm th}
Clifford algebra

\displaystyle\Gamma_{n}:=\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}2^{-n/2}f_{1}f_{2}\dots f_{n}&n\geq 0\\
2^{n/2}e_{1}e_{2}\dots e_{n}&n<0.\end{array}\right.

The
Clifford supertrace
of a
{\mathbb{C}}{\rm l}_{-n}
-linear endomorphism
T\colon V\to V
is (see §
B.2
)

(548)

\displaystyle{\sf sTr}_{{\mathbb{C}}{\rm l}_{n}}(T):={\sf sTr}(\Gamma_{n}\circ T).

Definition D.1
.

A
spin structure
for a rank
n
real, oriented, metrized vector bundle
V\to M
is a
{\rm Spin}(n)
-principal bundle
P_{V}\to M
equipped with a double cover
P_{V}\to{\rm Fr}(V)
of the oriented frame bundle of
V
that is equivariant relative to
{\rm Spin}(n)\to{\rm SO}(n)
.

The collection of spin structures on
V
forms a category (possibly empty) whose morphisms are isomorphisms of principal bundles compatible with the map to the frame bundle of
V
. A spin structure on a family
\pi\colon X\to M
of Riemannian manifolds is a spin structure on the vertical tangent bundle
T(X/M)=\ker(d\pi)\subset TM
. We will require two types of bundles of Clifford modules built out of a spin structure. In the following, for a metrized vector bundle
V\to M
the bundle
{\rm Cl}(V)\to M
is the bundle of fiberwise Clifford algebras.

Definition D.2
.

Given a vector bundle with spin structure
V\to M
, the
Euler bundle
is

(549)

\displaystyle{\mathbb{S}}_{V}=P_{V}\times_{{\rm Spin}(n)}{\rm Cl}_{-n},

which is a bundle of invertible
{\rm Cl}(V)
-
{\rm Cl}_{-n}
-bimodules. Hence, one obtains a left
{\rm Cl}_{n}={\rm Cl}_{-n}^{\sf{op}}
-module structure on
{\mathbb{S}}_{V}
that graded commutes with the
{\rm Cl}(V)
-action.
For
\pi\colon X\to M
a bundle of Riemannian manifolds with chosen spin structure on
T(X/M)
, the
spinor bundle
is

(550)

\displaystyle{\mathbb{S}}_{X/M}=P_{T(X/M)}\times_{{\rm Spin}(n)}{\rm Cl}_{n},

which is a bundle of invertible
{\rm Cl}(T(X/M))
-
{\rm Cl}_{n}
-bimodules. The right
{\rm Cl}_{n}
-action determines a left
{\rm Cl}_{-n}={\rm Cl}_{n}^{\sf{op}}
-action that graded commutes with the left
{\rm Cl}(T(X/M))
-action. This allows for the construction of the
{\rm Cl}_{-n}
-linear Dirac operator supported on sections of
{\mathbb{S}}_{X/M}
.

D.2.
Clifford modules and KO-theory

We overview a model for the KO-spectrum due to
[
Che07
, §1.3-1.4]
and
[
HST10
, Theorem 7.1]
, see also
[
BE23a
, §4]
. Let
\mathcal{H}_{n}
denote an infinite-dimensional
{\mathbb{Z}}/2
-graded separable Hilbert space with a self-adjoint action by
{\rm Cl}_{n}
with the property that every irreducible representation of
{\rm Cl}_{n}
appears with infinite multiplicity in
\mathcal{H}_{n}
. Let
\mathcal{B}(\mathcal{H}_{n})
denote the algebra of
{\rm Cl}_{n}
-linear bounded operators on
\mathcal{H}_{n}
equipped with the norm topology.

Definition D.3
.

Let
\mathcal{M}_{n}
be the category whose objects are finite-dimensional
{\rm Cl}_{n}
-submodules
W\subset\mathcal{H}_{n}
and whose morphisms are

\displaystyle{\rm Mor}(W_{0},W_{1}):=\left\{\begin{array}[]{cl}\begin{array}[]{c}{\rm Cl}_{n}\otimes{\rm Cl}_{-1}-{\rm action\ on\ }W_{0}^{\perp}\\
{\rm extending\ the}\ {\rm Cl}_{n}{\rm-action}\end{array}&{\rm if}\ W_{0}\subseteq W_{1}\subset\mathcal{H}_{n}\\
\hbox{}\\
\emptyset&{\rm else}\end{array}\right.

where
W_{0}^{\perp}\subseteq W_{1}
denotes the orthogonal complement of the subspace
W_{0}\subseteq W_{1}
. Composition for a nested inclusion
W_{0}\subseteq W_{1}\subseteq W_{2}
uses the direct sum of Clifford modules to obtain a
{\rm Cl}_{n}\otimes{\rm Cl}_{-1}
-module structure on the orthogonal complement of
W_{0}\subseteq W_{2}
. Endow the objects and morphisms in this category with the subspace topology for the embeddings

(557)

\displaystyle{\rm Obj}(\mathcal{M}_{n})\hookrightarrow\mathcal{B}(\mathcal{H}_{n}),\qquad{\rm Mor}(\mathcal{M}_{n})\hookrightarrow\mathcal{B}(\mathcal{H}_{n})^{\times 3}

where the map identifies a submodule
W_{0}\subset\mathcal{H}_{n}
with a finite rank projection operator, and
(W_{0},W_{1},e)\in{\rm Mor}(\mathcal{M}_{n})
determines a pair of finite rank projection operators and a bounded operator.

Definition D.4
.

The
nerve
of a topological category
\mathcal{C}
is the simplicial space whose
k
-simplices are
N_{k}\mathcal{C}=\mathcal{C}_{k}
for
k=0,1
, and for
k>1
are length
k
chains of composable morphisms

\displaystyle N_{k}\mathcal{C}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\{x_{0}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle f_{1}}}{{\to}}x_{1}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle f_{2}}}{{\to}}x_{2}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle f_{3}}}{{\to}}\cdots\stackrel{{\scriptstyle f_{k}}}{{\to}}x_{k}\mid x_{i}\in\mathcal{C}_{0},f_{i}\in\mathcal{C}_{1}\}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mathcal{C}_{1}\times_{\mathcal{C}_{0}}\cdots\times_{\mathcal{C}_{0}}\mathcal{C}_{1},

topologized as the fibered product.
The face maps in
N_{\bullet}\mathcal{C}
are determined by composing morphisms, and degeneracies are determined by the unit map in
\mathcal{C}
.

The
geometric realization
of a simplicial space
Z_{\bullet}
is

(558)

\displaystyle|Z_{\bullet}|:=(\coprod_{n}Z_{n}\times\Delta^{n})/{\sim}\qquad(f^{*}x,t)\sim(x,f_{*}t)\quad\forall f\colon[l]\to[k]

where
\Delta^{n}
is the standard
n
-simplex, and
f\colon[l]\to[k]
is an order-preserving map. Geometric realization is natural: a map of simplicial spaces
F\colon Z_{\bullet}\to Y_{\bullet}
determines a map between realizations,
|F|\colon|Z_{\bullet}|\to|Y_{\bullet}|
.

Proposition D.5

(
[
Che07
, §1.3-1.4]
,
[
HST10
, Theorem 7.1]
,
[
BE23a
, §4]
)
.

The classifying space
{\rm B}\mathcal{M}_{n}
represents the functor
{\rm KO}^{n}(-)
.

Given a topological space
Z
with open cover
\{U_{i}\}_{i\in I}
, for a nonempty finite subset
\sigma\subset I
define
U_{\sigma}=\bigcap_{i\in\sigma}U_{i}
as the intersection.

Definition D.6
.

For
Z
a topological space and
\{U_{i}\}_{i\in I}
an open cover, the
Čech category
, denoted
\check{{\rm C}}(U_{i})
, is the topological category whose objects are
\check{{\rm C}}(U_{i})_{0}=\coprod_{\sigma}U_{\sigma}
and whose morphisms are the space
\check{{\rm C}}(U_{i})_{1}=\coprod_{\sigma\subseteq\tau}U_{\tau}
. The source and target maps are determined by the projection and inclusion
U_{\tau}\subseteq U_{\sigma}
, the unit is induced by the identity map
U_{\sigma}\to U_{\sigma}
, and composition comes from nested inclusions.

Lemma D.7

(
[
Seg68
, Proposition 4.12]
,
[
DI04
, Theorem 2.1]
)
.

For
Z
a topological space and
\{U_{i}\}_{i\in I}
an open cover, the canonical map

(559)

\displaystyle{\rm B}\check{C}(U_{i})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\to}}Z,

is a homotopy equivalence.

Corollary D.8
.

For
Z
a topological space and
\{U_{i}\}_{i\in I}
an open cover, a continuous functor

(560)

\displaystyle\check{{\rm C}}(U_{i})\to\mathcal{M}_{n}

determines a map
Z\to B\mathcal{M}_{n}\simeq{\rm KO}^{n}
into the
n
th space of
{\rm KO}
spectrum.

Proof.

This follows from Proposition
D.5
and Lemma
D.7
.
∎

D.3.
Super connections and and the families index bundle

Definition D.9
.

Let
{\mathcal{E}}\to M
be a real vector bundle with a fiberwise
{\rm Cl}_{n}
-action. A
Clifford linear superconnection

{\mathbb{A}}
on
{\mathcal{E}}
is an odd,
{\rm Cl}_{n}
-linear map of Fréchet spaces satisfying a Leibniz rule

(561)

\displaystyle{\mathbb{A}}\colon\Omega^{\bullet}(M;{\mathcal{E}})\to\Omega^{\bullet}(M;{\mathcal{E}}),\quad{\mathbb{A}}(\alpha\cdot v)=d\alpha\cdot v+(-1)^{|\alpha|}\alpha\cdot{\mathbb{A}}v

where
v\in\Omega^{\bullet}(M;{\mathcal{E}})
and
\alpha\in\Omega^{\bullet}(M)
.

Using the
{\mathbb{Z}}
-grading on forms, a superconnection can be written as

(562)

\displaystyle{\mathbb{A}}=\sum{\mathbb{A}}^{[k]},\qquad{\mathbb{A}}^{[k]}\colon\Omega^{\bullet}(M;{\mathcal{E}})\to\Omega^{\bullet+k}(M;{\mathcal{E}})

where
{\mathbb{A}}^{[1]}=\nabla
is an ordinary connection on
{\mathcal{E}}
, and
{\mathbb{A}}^{[2k]}\in\Omega^{2k}(M;{\rm End}({\mathcal{E}})^{{\rm odd}})
,
{\mathbb{A}}^{[2k+1]}\in\Omega^{2k+1}(M;{\rm End}({\mathcal{E}})^{{\rm ev}})
are endomorphism-valued forms.

Definition D.10
.

Suppose that
\langle-,-\rangle\colon\overline{{\mathcal{E}}}\otimes{\mathcal{E}}\to\underline{{\mathbb{C}}}
is a positive (super) hermitian pairing. Then a superconnection
{\mathbb{A}}
on
{\mathcal{E}}
is
self-adjoint
if

(563)

\displaystyle\langle{\mathbb{A}}^{[1]}x,y\rangle+(-1)^{|x||y|}\langle x,{\mathbb{A}}^{[1]}y\rangle=d\langle x,y\rangle,\qquad({\mathbb{A}}^{[k]})^{*}=i^{k+1}\overline{{\mathbb{A}}}{}^{[k]},\qquad k\neq 1,

where
({\mathbb{A}}^{[k]})^{*}
denotes the super adjoint of
{\mathbb{A}}^{[k]}
.

Remark D.11
.

Translating from super adjoints to ordinary adjoints as in (
B.1
), the above is equivalent to the standard definition of self-adjoint superconnection
[
BGV92
, page 117]
, i.e., the usual metric preserving condition for the ordinary connection
{\mathbb{A}}^{[1]}=\nabla
and

({\mathbb{A}}^{[k]})^{\dagger}=\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}{\mathbb{A}}^{[k]}&k=1\mod 4\\
-{\mathbb{A}}^{[k]}&k=1\mod 4\\
-{\mathbb{A}}^{[k]}&k=2\mod 4\\
{\mathbb{A}}^{[k]}&k=0\mod 4\end{array}\right.\qquad\qquad k\neq 1

where
({\mathbb{A}}^{[k]})^{\dagger}
denotes the ungraded adjoint relative to an ordinary hermitian inner product.

For a superconnection
{\mathbb{A}}
, define

\displaystyle{\mathbb{A}}(u):=u^{-1/2}{\mathbb{A}}^{[0]}+{\mathbb{A}}^{[1]}+u^{1/2}{\mathbb{A}}^{[2]}+\dots+u^{j/2}{\mathbb{A}}^{[j]}+\dots\quad

for a formal parameter
u^{-1/2}
of degree
+1
.

Definition D.12
.

The
Chern form
of a
{\rm Cl}_{n}
-linear superconnection is the Clifford supertrace (which need not exist for infinite rank bundles)

(564)

\displaystyle{\rm Ch}({\mathbb{A}}):={\sf sTr}_{{\mathbb{C}}{\rm l}_{n}}(u^{-n/2}e^{-u{\mathbb{A}}(u)^{2}})={\sf sTr}(\Gamma_{n}u^{-n/2}e^{-u{\mathbb{A}}(u)^{2}})\in\Omega^{\bullet}(M;{\mathbb{C}}[u^{\pm 2}]))

for the Clifford supertrace defined in (
548
).

The absence of fractional powers of
u
in
{\rm Ch}({\mathbb{A}})
follows from the fact that the supertrace of an odd endomorphism is zero. Self-adjoint superconnections have Chern forms with only even powers of
u
. The following is a standard algebraic argument, e.g.,
[
BGV92
, §1.5]
.

Lemma D.13
.

When it exists, the Chern form of a
{\rm Cl}_{n}
-linear superconnection
{\mathbb{A}}
is closed and has total degree
n
.

Given a self-adjoint,
{\rm Cl}_{n}
-linear superconnection
{\mathbb{A}}
on a Fréchet vector bundle
{\mathcal{E}}\to M
, let
\mathcal{H}_{m}
denote the Hilbert space completion of the fiber of
\mathcal{E}
at
m\in M
. Define the subset
U_{\lambda}\subset M
for
\lambda\in{\mathbb{R}}_{>0}
as follows. A point
m\in M
belongs to
U_{\lambda}
if
\lambda
is not an eigenvalue of the (non-negative) operator
({\mathbb{A}}^{[0]})^{2}_{m}
on
\mathcal{H}_{m}
:

(565)

\displaystyle U_{\lambda}=:\{m\in M\mid\lambda\notin{\rm Spec}(({\mathbb{A}}^{[0]})^{2}_{m})\}\subset M.

Over each subset
U_{\lambda}\subset M
, define a subset
\mathcal{H}^{<\lambda}\subset\coprod_{m\in U_{\lambda}}\mathcal{H}_{m}
as the direct sum of
\nu
-eigenspaces
\mathcal{H}_{m}^{\nu}
of
({\mathbb{A}}^{[0]})^{2}_{m}
with eigenvalue
\nu<\lambda

(566)

\displaystyle\mathcal{H}^{<\lambda}_{m}:=\bigoplus_{\nu<\lambda}\mathcal{H}_{m}^{\nu}\subset\mathcal{H}_{m}.

Letting
m
vary,
\mathcal{H}^{<\lambda}
denotes the
{\rm Cl}_{n}
-invariant subset of
\coprod_{m\in U_{\lambda}}\mathcal{H}_{m}
whose fibers are
\mathcal{H}^{<\lambda}_{m}
. Define the maps (of sets) over
U_{\lambda}

(567)

\displaystyle p^{\lambda}\colon\coprod_{m\in U_{\lambda}}\mathcal{H}_{m}\to\mathcal{H}^{<\lambda},\qquad i_{\lambda}\colon\mathcal{H}^{<\lambda}\to\coprod_{m\in U_{\lambda}}\mathcal{H}_{m}

where
p^{\lambda}
is the fiberwise orthogonal projection, and
i_{\lambda}
is the fiberwise inclusion.

Definition D.14
.

Suppose that
{\mathbb{A}}
is a self-adjoint
{\rm Cl}_{n}
-linear superconnection for which the differential form-valued Chern character (
564
) is well-defined. Then
{\mathbb{A}}

admits smooth index bundles
if the following properties are satisfied:

(1)

the collection of subsets
\{U_{\lambda}\}_{\lambda\in{\mathbb{R}}_{>0}}
defined in (
565
) form a smooth open cover of
M
;

(2)

the subsets
\mathcal{H}^{<\lambda}\subset{\mathcal{E}}|_{U_{\lambda}}\to U_{\lambda}
are smooth vector subbundles;

(3)

the composition defines a smooth connection:

\nabla^{<\lambda}:=p^{\lambda}\circ{\mathbb{A}}^{[1]}|_{U_{\lambda}}\circ p^{\lambda}\colon\Gamma(\mathcal{H}^{<\lambda})\to\Omega^{1}(U_{\lambda};\mathcal{H}^{<\lambda}),

(4)

the limit of Chern–Simons forms

(568)

\displaystyle\eta_{\lambda}:=\lim_{r\to\infty}\int_{1}^{r}{\sf sTr}_{{\rm Cl}_{n}}(\frac{d{\mathbb{A}}_{\lambda}(s)}{ds}\exp({\mathbb{A}}_{\lambda}(s)))ds\in\Omega^{\bullet}(U_{\lambda};C^{\infty}({\mathbb{R}}_{>0})[u^{\pm 1}])

exists and satisfies

(569)

\displaystyle d\eta_{\lambda}={\rm Ch}({\mathbb{A}})|_{U_{\lambda}}-{\rm Ch}(\nabla^{<\lambda})

where in (
568
),
{\mathbb{A}}_{\lambda}(s)
is the 1-parameter family of superconnections on
{\mathcal{E}}|_{U_{\lambda}}\oplus\Pi\mathcal{H}^{<\lambda}
,

\displaystyle{\mathbb{A}}_{\lambda}(s)={\mathbb{A}}_{\lambda}(s)^{[0]}+{\mathbb{A}}^{[1]}\oplus\nabla^{<\lambda}+\sum_{i>1}s^{-i}{\mathbb{A}}^{[i]}\oplus 0_{\Pi\mathcal{H}^{<\lambda}},\quad{\mathbb{A}}_{\lambda}(s)^{[0]}=\left[\begin{array}[]{cc}{\mathbb{A}}^{[0]}&s\cdot i_{\lambda}\\
s\cdot p^{\lambda}&0\end{array}\right]

where
i_{\lambda}
and
p^{\lambda}
are the maps (
567
).

Remark D.15
.

Conditions (1) and (2) are required to obtain an index of
{\mathbb{A}}
valued in
{\rm KO}^{n}(M)
. Conditions (3) and (4) are required to relate a Čech-de Rham cocycle representing the Chern character of this index bundle to the Chern character of
{\mathbb{A}}
. We also note that for a superconnection on a bundle over
M={\rm pt}
, conditions (1)-(3) are automatic; the cutoff condition in this case is therefore convergence of the integral (
4
), which in turn requires that the eigenvalues
{\mathbb{A}}^{[0]}
grow sufficiently quickly.

Proposition D.16
.

Any finite-rank self-adjoint
{\rm Cl}_{n}
-linear superconnection admits smooth index bundles. The Clifford linear Bismut superconnection adapted to a family of Clifford linear Dirac operators admits smooth index bundles.

Proof.

For
{\rm Cl}_{0}={\mathbb{R}}
, the finite rank case is
[
BGV92
, Theorem 9.7]
and for Bismut superconnections this follows from
[
BGV92
, Proposition 9.10 and Theorem 9.26]
, see also
[
FL10
, §7.2]
. The straightforward extension of these results to Clifford linear superconnections is
[
BE23a
, §8.2-8.3]
.
∎

For a superconnection admitting smooth index bundles, we review the index bundle construction from
[
BE23a
, §8.2-8.3]
.

Proposition D.17

(
[
BE23a
, §8.2]
)
.

Suppose a
{\rm Cl}_{n}
-linear superconnection admits smooth index bundles. Then a choice of locally finite open cover
\{U_{\lambda}\}_{\lambda\in\Lambda}
associated with a discrete subset
\Lambda\subset{\mathbb{R}}_{>0}
determines a continuous functor,

(573)

\displaystyle\check{{\rm C}}(U_{\lambda})\to\mathcal{M}_{n}\quad\implies\quad{\rm Ind}_{\Lambda}({\mathbb{A}})\colon M\to{\rm KO}^{n},

whose realization is a map to the
n
th space in the
{\rm KO}
-spectrum. Given choices
\Lambda\subset\Lambda^{\prime}\subset{\mathbb{R}}_{>0}
related by a refinement of open covers, there is a uniquely specified homotopy between the associated maps (
573
).

Proof.

The
{\rm Cl}_{n}
-modules
\mathcal{H}^{<\lambda}\to U_{\lambda}
determine classes
[\mathcal{H}^{<\lambda}]\in{\rm KO}^{n}(U_{\lambda})
for each
\lambda\in{\mathbb{R}}_{>0}
. On overlaps
U_{\lambda}\bigcap U_{\mu}
for
\lambda<\mu
there are canonical inclusions

(574)

\displaystyle g_{\lambda\mu}\colon\mathcal{H}^{<\lambda}\hookrightarrow\mathcal{H}^{<\mu}

and the orthogonal complement to the image of
g_{\lambda\mu}
has a
{\rm Cl}_{n}\otimes{\rm Cl}_{-1}
-action extending the
{\rm Cl}_{n}
-action, determined by the invertible odd endomorphisms

(575)

\displaystyle e^{\lambda\mu}|_{\mathcal{H}_{b}^{\nu}}=\frac{1}{\sqrt{\nu}}{\mathbb{A}}^{[0]}|_{\mathcal{H}_{b}^{\nu}},\quad\mathcal{H}_{b}^{\nu}\subset(\mathcal{H}^{<\lambda})^{\perp}\subset\mathcal{H}^{<\mu}

that define the action of the generator
e\in{\rm Cl}_{-1}
. The inclusions
g_{\lambda\mu}
and odd endomorphisms
e^{\lambda\mu}
satisfy a compatibility property on triple intersections. By the contractibility of the orthogonal group of a separable infinite dimensional Hilbert space
[
Kui65
]
, the data (
566
), (
574
) and (
575
) determine a continuous functor (
560
) uniquely up to contractible choice. Hence by Corollary
D.8
, we obtain a map to the
n
th space in the
{\rm KO}
-spectrum (
573
).
A refinement of open covers associated with
\Lambda\subset\Lambda^{\prime}\subset{\mathbb{R}}_{>0}
, determines a continuous functor between the associated Čech categories that after realization is a homotopy equivalence. This leads to a pair of functors (
560
) and restriction of the operators (
575
) furnish a natural isomorphism between these functors. By
[
Seg68
, Proposition 2.1]
, the resulting maps
{\rm Ind}_{\Lambda}({\mathbb{A}}),{\rm Ind}_{\Lambda^{\prime}}({\mathbb{A}})\colon M\to{\rm KO}^{-n}
are homotopic.
∎

The Pontryagin character of the class associated with (
573
) is determined by the local data
{\rm Ch}(\nabla^{<\lambda})
with compatibilities on overlaps coming from Chern–Simons forms; more precisely we obtain a cocycle in the Čech–de Rham complex denoted

(576)

\displaystyle{\rm Ch}({\rm Ind}_{\Lambda}({\mathbb{A}}))=[U_{\lambda},{\rm Ch}(\nabla^{<\lambda}),{\rm CS}(\nabla^{<\lambda},\nabla^{<\lambda^{\prime}})]\in C^{*}(\{U_{\lambda}\},\Omega^{\bullet}).

Lemma D.18
.

The differential forms (
568
) witness a coboundary in the Čech–de Rham complex between (
576
) and the (globally defined) cocycle
{\rm Ch}({\mathbb{A}})
.

Proof sketch.

This follows from (
569
) and the defining properties of Chern–Simons forms; we refer to
[
BE23a
, §8.3]
for details.
∎
